# Changelog

All notable changes to the YGT Platform.

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **Per-cell color on the Shipment Sheet (feat(p3)).** Right-click any cell → **Cell color**: ten preset swatches, a custom Ant `ColorPicker`, and **Clear color**. Stored per `(shipment, field_key)` in the new `SheetCellColor` table (migration `export 0064`); clearing deletes the row rather than storing an empty string. New `POST /shipments/{id}/set-cell-color/` sits in `ShipmentViewSet._OPEN_ACTIONS`, so — like the column tint — every authenticated Sheet viewer may paint regardless of field grants, while deleted/archived shipments are refused with 403 and an unknown `field_key` with 400. Render precedence is **cell > per-value > row style > column tint**. The sheet payload gained a `cell_colors` map (one query); `useSetCellColor` paints optimistically across every cached sheet query with rollback on error. The ten swatches moved to a shared `SHEET_PRESET_COLORS` so the column picker and the cell picker offer the same palette. `backend/apps/export/models/sheet_settings.py`, `views.py`, `tests_cell_color.py` (10 tests), `frontend/src/components/sheet/SheetCell.tsx`, `SheetGrid.tsx`, `SheetColumnHeader.tsx`, `SheetStyles.css`, `hooks/useShipmentSheet.ts`, `constants/sheetOptions.ts`, `pages/export/ShipmentSheet.tsx`, `types/index.ts`, `SheetCell.colors.test.tsx` (5 tests), i18n ×3, `docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md`.
- **Delete an unused contract from the Contracts list (feat(p4)).** A right-fixed trash button per row, shown only to roles the matrix grants `contract.can_delete` (`canDo`), on both the Framework and One-time tabs. "Unused" = **no `ContractSale` attached** — the list row carries a new `has_sales` flag and renders the button disabled with a tooltip when it is true, and `ContractViewSet.destroy` refuses the same case with **409** before touching the row. `has_sales` is annotated with `Exists`, deliberately **not** `Count`: an aggregate would force every `select_related` column into `GROUP BY` and MSSQL cannot group by `nvarchar(max)`, which SQLite would have swallowed silently. The annotation sits above the `action != 'list'` early return so `ContractDetailSerializer` (which extends the list serializer) still resolves the field on retrieve. The global `ProtectedError` → 409 handler stays as the race net for a sale created between the check and the DELETE. Attachments cascade away with the contract (their files stay on disk) — the confirm dialog says so. Note the list never returns `cancelled` contracts, so a cancelled-and-unused contract has no row to delete from here. `backend/apps/contracts/views.py`, `serializers.py`, `tests/test_contract_api.py`, `frontend/src/hooks/useContracts.ts`, `pages/contracts/ContractList.tsx`, `types/contract.ts`, i18n ×3, `docs/obsidian/screens/contract-list.md`.

### Data
- **Deleted the rows stranded outside every season (data(p3), owner instruction).** Follow-up to the seller-panel fix: rows whose `season` FK disagreed with their own date, or whose week no season covers at all. Deleted after a row-level backup: **15 `WeeklyTruckAllocation` + 45 `TruckDestinationSplit`** — 6 at W35/2026 (mislabelled `2025-2026`), 3 at W29/2026 (the July gap), and 6 at W1/2025 whose Monday is 2024-12-30, i.e. a **second** gap before the first season starts, carrying 627,540 kg of planned tonnage — plus **`Contract#66`** (`2/26-HG-EXP`, no sales attached). **`Contract#62/63/64/65` were NOT deleted:** each carries one `ContractSale` and `ContractSale.contract` is `on_delete=PROTECT`, so the delete fails at the DB layer unless the sale goes too — which would detach the invoice and quota line from shipments 663/664. That is a materially larger deletion than the one authorised, so it was left for an explicit call (FINDINGS_BACKLOG S2). Every deleted row was serialised first to `backups/2026-08-23_season_gap_rows.json` in Django `loaddata` format; `backups/` was added to `.gitignore` — it is production data and must never be committed. Restoring is one `manage.py loaddata`. **Correction to an earlier entry in this file:** the count of "14 rows in the July hole" conflated two different gaps — only 3 truck rows were actually in July 2026; 6 were in December 2024.
- **Cleared the W34/2026 local-sell plan (data(p3), owner instruction).** All **25** `WeeklyLocalSellPlan` rows for ISO week 34 of 2026 deleted — 3 carried kg (Ýigit H.J. 15 000, "Ak Bulut" HJ 6 400, "Döwletli-Döwran" HJ 600) and 22 were empty placeholders. Unlike the two records below these were **correctly** stamped `season_id=3`; they were removed because the owner wanted the new season's Firm breakdown starting clean, not because of the mis-stamp. Worth knowing: week 34 (17–23 Aug) was the **current** week, so this is a live plan, not a stale one. `WeeklyLocalSellPlan` has no related objects — nothing cascaded — and `invalidate_quota_caches()` ran after. The 2026-2027 quota dashboard is now fully empty (`per_firm: []`, `weekly_flow: []`, every KPI 0); 2025-2026 is untouched (24 firms, 3 548 821 kg sales, 26 733 000 kg issued) and 323 plan rows remain overall. Backup: `backups/2026-08-23_local_sell_plan_W34.json`. **Left in place:** `Task#1718` (`local_sell_plan`, seller, state=done, scope 2026/W34) — audit history of who filled the plan, but `reconcile_tasks` may reopen it now that its completion condition no longer holds.
- **Deleted two test records mis-stamped to 2025-2026 (data(p3)).** `QuotaIssuance #35` (1 234 000 kg → "Höwesli gurluşyk" HJ, issued 2026-08-22, validity *this month*) and draft shipment `2208001/26` (id 685, 18 100 kg, created 2026-08-22 by `admin`) were entered during the browser-test run while the 2026-2027 season was accidentally closed, so `perform_create`'s `get_active_season()` stamped both `season_id=1`. Being date-anchored, the Firm breakdown showed them under 2026-2027 while every FK-anchored view (Sheet firm balances, the issuance list) filed them under 2025-2026. Owner confirmed they were test entries. Removed through `ShipmentViewSet._hard_delete_targets()` — the app's own cascade, so the two **approved** `QuotaUsageRecord` rows (1020/1021, 9 000 kg each) were deleted rather than orphaned to `shipment_id=NULL`, where `counted()` would keep counting them as historical imports — plus 14 cascade rows (2 firm splits, 2 block sources, 2 status logs, 5 tasks, …) and 1 issuance allocation; `invalidate_quota_caches()` run after both. The 2026-2027 breakdown now shows only the three real weekly local-sell plan rows (Sales 22 000, everything else 0). Backup: `backups/2026-08-23_test_issuance35_shipment685.json` (all 16 objects). **Not touched, still mis-stamped:** shipments `2008880/26` (id 684, gumruk_girish) and `2208002/26` (id 687, draft, created by `sulgun` — a real user, likely not test data); neither surfaces on this tab as they have no approved quota usage.

### Changed
- **Framework-contract creation reworked: document date, price per kg, auto-calculated plan (`feat(p4)`).** `Contract` gained `contract_date` (the date the document itself carries) and `price_per_kg` (`DecimalField(8,4)`, USD per net kg) — migration `contracts/0013`. In the generated contract the header (`ş. Asgabat  <date>`, and the appendix `Контракт № … от <date>`) now prints `contract_date` instead of `start_date`, and it is also the date embedded in the auto-generated contract number; **§2.6** ("delivery until") now prints `start_date`, with the generate-modal's `delivery_deadline` kept as a per-generation override (blank = use `start_date`). The unit price in the appendix comes from the stored `price_per_kg`, falling back to `planned_amount_usd / planned_quantity_kg` so older contracts still render. On the create modal: **Incoterm defaults to FCA**, trucks ⇄ quantity convert through **18 100 kg per truck** (editing either fills the other) and the planned amount is always quantity × price — all three stay editable. The **deal passport** field was dropped from the create modal (the bank issues it after signing); the column, its `?search=` filter and the detail display are unchanged. `models/contract.py`, `serializers.py`, `services/document_context.py`, `ContractCreate.tsx`, `ContractDetail.tsx`, `types/contract.ts`, i18n ×3, `docs/obsidian/reference/contracts-contract-model.md`, `screens/contract-list.md`, `processes/document-generation.md`.
- **The contract generator is no longer Kazakhstan-only (`feat(p4)`).** §4.1/§4.2 name the *buyer* country's authorities, and they were the template's only hardcoded "Kazakhstan" — a sweep for `РК` / `Алматы` / `Астана` / `KZT` found nothing else (the `Туркменистана` in §4.3/§7 is the **seller's** law and stays). Both clauses became `{{ dest_country_gen_tk }}` / `{{ dest_country_gen_ru }}` in `contract_kz.docx`; the RU literal was split across two runs (`Казахстан` + `а`), so a naive replace would have matched nothing. The clause needs the **genitive** case, which `Country` stores no form of and neither language derives by rule, so the forms come from the fixed ISO-code map `_COUNTRY_GENITIVE` (KZ/KG/RU/TJ/UZ/AE — every destination in the DB). That map **is** the gate: the endpoint 400s a country without an entry, and the button now reads the server-owned `contract_template_supported` flag on the contract detail serializer instead of a second hardcoded list in TypeScript. Covers all 25 previously-blocked non-KZ contracts. Adding a country = one map row, no migration. **Assumption on record (owner-confirmed):** §4 wording is identical across destinations apart from the country name — verified against KZ contracts only, so the first non-KZ output needs a human read before it goes to a buyer.
- **Split the Shipment Sheet and Shipment Dashboard out of the `export.shipments` page code (feat(p3)).** All three shipment routes shared one code, so the permission matrix could not hide one view without hiding the list. New codes `export.shipments_sheet` / `export.shipments_dashboard`; deliberately flat rather than nested under `export.shipments.`, because `canSeePage` grants a parent whenever any child is visible and the nested form would have silently re-opened the list. Migration `core/0036` copies each role's existing `export.shipments` value into both new codes, so deploy is access-neutral — but it must run before the new frontend bundle is served.
- **Removed the per-user "extra users" grant from the Sheet Rows admin panel (`feat(frontend)`).** Owner call: since the trigger gate is AND-composed with the field permission, a per-user grant can only narrow *within* a role that already holds that permission — it can never hand access to someone whose role lacks it — so with ~one person per role it added a control that could not do anything the trigger roles couldn't. The Select, the `extra_user_ids` half of the save draft, the second `permissions/bulk/` call, the now-uncalled `useBulkPermissions` hook and 4 i18n keys are gone; the row-list trigger badge and the "with triggers" filter count roles only, and the Access wording says "roles" instead of "roles and users". **Backend untouched** — model, `permissions/bulk/` endpoint and `matched_extra` in `can_edit_sheet_field`/`get_sheet_edit_map` still work, and existing `SheetRowUserPermission` rows keep granting access; they are just invisible from this screen (dev DB: 2 active rows, both for `admin`, who bypasses every gate anyway). 533 frontend tests pass, tsc clean.
- **Synced the Obsidian vault to state machine v2 (docs).** `shipment-lifecycle.md` and `status-codes.md` described the retired v1 13-step machine throughout; both rewritten from `services/shipment.py::TRANSITIONS` + `seed_task_rules.py::TASK_RULES` — 12-step chain, `has_peregruz` fork, per-step Sheet trigger field with its verified R-number, new **Sheet-Driven Auto-Advance** section, `draft` join guard. Same fact corrected in `00-index.md`, `assignment-board.md`, `comments-tasks.md`, `detail-vs-sheet.md`, `draft-shipments.md`, `shipment-creation.md`, `data-model-map.md`, `boss.md`, `shipment-sheet.md`; `task-rules.md` rows absent from the seed removed.
- **Documented (not fixed) two v2 gaps.** `phases.py::PHASE_MAP` has no entry for `dest_entry` / `transshipment` / `cancelled`, so `get_phase()` drops them into `CLOSE` on the board, dashboard and KPI. `StatusTag.tsx` keys colours on v1 English display names, so 8 of 13 statuses render grey `default`.
- **Flagged two stale code comments (docs only, code untouched).** `seed_task_rules.py` header and `create_shipment()` docstring both say the customs-entry trigger is `documents_status == 'in_progress'`; the rule body correctly says `'ready'`. `CLAUDE.md`'s AD-1 rule ("timestamps written ONLY by `transition_to()`") is inverted in v2 — left for the owner to decide.
- **Sheet Rows admin rebuilt as list + detail (feat(frontend)).** `/admin/shipment-settings` → Sheet Rows was one 13-column table: 45 rows × ~2000px of horizontal scroll, 270 text inputs on screen, every cell saving on its own PATCH. Now a **searchable list on the left** (search over field_key + label, filter all/hidden/locked/with-triggers/custom, badges per row, reorder arrows disabled while a filter narrows the list because `/reorder/` takes the full order) and a **pinned detail panel on the right** with five sections — Labels, Who, Tooltip, **Access**, Display. The panel edits a draft and saves it as **one PATCH with every changed field** (`useSaveRowDraft`); the extra-user list still goes through its own `permissions/bulk/` call, sequentially, and a failure there reports "settings saved, users not updated" instead of a clean save. Batching is not cosmetic: `useSaveSheetRowSetting` invalidates on success, so two mutations from one action would send a stale `version` and 409. **Access is the comprehension fix** — the section states the rule the backend actually applies: no lock + no triggers → the field permission decides; any trigger set → only those roles/users **and** they still need the field permission (the gate is AND, never OR, and the lock makes no difference here); lock + nobody selected → nobody but admin/director/export_manager. `role_group` is called out as visual grouping that grants no access. The «Sheet Control — Configuration Levels» info plate above the tab was dropped. No backend change. `SheetRowsTab.tsx` + new `sheet-rows/` (list, item, detail, header, access section, localized field group, `rowDraft.ts`, `useSaveRowDraft.ts`, custom-row modal); `SheetRowStylePopover`/`SheetRowTooltipPopover`/`InlineSavedInput` deleted (shared `SheetRowStyleControls` kept); 28 i18n keys × tk/ru/en; 6 new tests — 533 frontend tests pass, tsc clean.
- **The frontend Docker image no longer typechecks during the build (`fix(docker)`).** `frontend/Dockerfile` now runs `npx vite build` instead of `npm run build` (= `tsc && vite build`). Measured on the beta host 10.10.11.25 on 2026-08-27: that step took **683s**, inside a `./update.sh` run whose total wall clock was **76 minutes**. The box has 3.8 GB RAM with ~1.3 GB actually free and 1.2 GB of swap already in use before the build starts; a whole-program `tsc` over ~490 files needs 1.5-4 GB resident, and Compose bakes `backend` and `frontend` concurrently, so the two builds contend and the machine pages. Everything else in the pipeline was ruled out by measurement first: the backend image builds in **16s** fully cached, the frontend in **2.8s** when no source changed, there is no `--no-cache`/`--pull`/`system prune -a` anywhere in `update.sh`, and its `docker image prune -f` reclaimed **0B**. **Typechecking is unchanged as a LOCAL gate** — `npx tsc --noEmit --ignoreDeprecations 5.0` — which is where a type error should surface anyway rather than mid-deploy; the deployed bundle is byte-identical either way, since `tsc` in that script only typechecks and Vite does the emit. Verified locally: `npx vite build` alone succeeds in 15.67s.
- **The three permission tabs became one role-first screen (`feat(frontend)`).** `/admin/permissions` had three tabs — Page Visibility, Resource Permissions, Field Permissions — each rendering its own `item × 15-role` matrix with its own Save button. That layout answers "who can see the quota page" while an admin's actual question is "what can Sulgun do", and it charged a 15-column horizontal scroll for the privilege. Now: pick a role on the left, see everything it may do on the right in three collapsible sections (pages grouped by code prefix, resources as view/create/edit/delete rows, fields as chips per resource), with **one** Save and an unsaved-changes counter in the header. **No backend change** — the same three endpoints back it; the page loads all three matrices and slices them by the selected role. Save fires only the endpoints whose section actually changed, and for fields that means one PUT per touched resource (the field endpoint writes one resource per request, so re-sending an untouched one would clobber a concurrent edit by another admin). A failed save deliberately KEEPS the drafts so the admin can retry without re-ticking everything. Split per `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s 150-line rule into `RoleSidebar`, `RolePermissionEditor`, three `sections/*`, a `useRolePermissionDrafts` hook for state+save, and a React-free `rolePermissionModel.ts` holding the grouping and diff rules — 19 unit tests cover exactly the logic that decides which PUTs fire. The three old tab components are deleted. 527 frontend tests pass, `tsc` clean, verified in-browser.
- **Eight resources in the permission matrix are now marked ⚠ "does not actually enforce this" (`feat(frontend)`).** Audit finding, 2026-08-27: `pallet`, `manifest_close`, `sales_report`, `quality_document`, `shipment_assign`, `domestic_sale`, `weekly_plan` and `greenhouse_block` all render four checkboxes that save correctly and change nothing — the real decision is a hardcoded role list inside the view (`PALLET_WRITE_ROLES`, `HARVEST_DAY_WRITE`, `PRIVILEGED_ROLES | {...}`, `write_permission(...)`), or in `greenhouse_block`'s case nobody at all, since its viewset is read-only and exposes no writes. For `pallet` / `manifest_close` / `sales_report` this was **deliberate**: the class-level `DynamicResourcePermission` gates on `shipment.can_create`, not on the junction's own resource, so it wrongly 403'd the very roles that own the manifest — the same mis-scoped-gate bug that `packing_template` had. Those rows are now dimmed with a tooltip naming what really decides and the file:line to look at. `deadResources.ts` is a hand-maintained list on purpose (deriving it needs runtime DRF-router introspection, tracked separately) and a test pins the current set so it fails loudly when the list and the code drift. **Moving these eight onto the matrix is NOT done** — this change only stops the UI from lying about them. Also recorded, unfixed: `page_code` is read by `/auth/me/` and nowhere else, so unticking a page hides the menu entry and blocks the route but does not stop the API behind it answering a direct request.
- **The packing-template catalog is now a row in the permission matrix, and `document_team` holds full CRUD on it (`feat(p3)`).** `PackingTemplateViewSet` gated its writes on a hardcoded `write_permission('admin', 'director', 'export_manager')` tuple, so `packing_template` never appeared in `RESOURCE_REGISTRY` — it could not be seen, let alone delegated, from Admin → Permissions. It now uses a new `resource_write_permission('packing_template')` (`apps/core/permissions.py`), which reads the `RoleResourcePermission` row: POST → `can_create`, PATCH → `can_edit`, DELETE → `can_delete`, no row → no writes (fail-closed). **Reads stay open to every authenticated user** — `DynamicResourcePermission` was deliberately *not* used here because it gates GET on `can_view`, which would 403 the Sheet packing panel's template dropdown for `transport`, `sales_rep`, `warehouse_chief`, `weight_master` and every other role that picks a template on a truck but holds no catalog row. Seeded holders: admin/director/export_manager/boss (via the `_ALL_RESOURCES` spread) and **`document_team`** — they build the CMR/Invoice packets, so they own the gross-net catalog they pick from; they also gain the `export.packing_presets` page, which is how they reach it. Back-fill migration `core/0035_grant_packing_template_document_team` creates the rows on existing installs (without it every role including admin would fail closed) and `update()`s the page row rather than `get_or_create`, since `seed_permissions` will not flip an existing `is_visible=False`. 2 new tests in `tests_packing_template.py` — `document_team`'s full GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE cycle, and that a role with **no** row still reads the catalog while its writes 403. 5 packing-template + 220 core tests pass; verified on the dev DB (5 CRUD rows, `document_team` page visible).
- **Draft Shipment and Assignment Board removed from every sidebar (chore(frontend)).** Owner request, 2026-08-24. `/export/drafts` and `/export/assign` were previously withheld from the boss sidebar only (2026-08-20) and still showed for every other role's `nav.group_export`; both are now dropped from `STAFF_MENU_GROUPS` too, so no role has a top-level nav entry for either page. Same treatment already given `/export/trucks`: their now-unreachable `ITEMS` entries were deleted (`IconLayoutGrid`/`IconLayoutKanban` stay — both used elsewhere), while the routes, `export.drafts`/`export.assign` page permissions, `ROUTE_LABELS` breadcrumb entries, feedback path labels, and the pages themselves are all untouched — still reachable by direct URL. The boss/staff compositions are key-for-key identical again (46 routes each), just grouped differently (process-phase vs. module); `AppLayout.menuGroups.test.tsx` updated (staff 48 → 46 keys) and its "boss withholds 2 pages" test rewritten to assert both compositions now exclude the same two keys. 12 tests pass, `tsc` clean.
- **`initialize-week` reported success for a week it had not created (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** Found testing the seller panel: the button returned **200** and the grid stayed empty. `weekly_local_sell_plans` is `UNIQUE (export_firm_id, week_number, year)` with **no season in the key**, so a week can exist under exactly one season — but the LIST is season-scoped. All 25 W34/2026 rows on the live DB carry `season_id = 1` (2025-2026), written before 2026-2027 existed, so the active-season grid showed nothing; `initialize_week`'s dedupe (`existing_firm_ids`) and its response queryset were **both unscoped**, so it matched those 25 rows, created nothing, and echoed them back as `{count: 25}`. The caller saw "initialized 25" and an empty table. It now returns **409 `{error: 'week_exists_in_other_season', season, count}`** naming the offending season, and scopes its 200 response to the season actually written so `count` describes what the caller's own list will show. The dedupe stays unscoped **deliberately** — season-scoping it would build rows that violate the unique constraint on `bulk_create`, trading a silent no-op for a 500. Frontend: `handleInitWeek` had no `onError` at all, so any failure was invisible; it now toasts the named conflict (`local_sell.init_other_season`) or a generic `local_sell.init_error`, both tk/ru/en. 6 new backend tests. **The data half was applied separately** — new `fix_local_sell_plan_seasons` command (sibling of `backfill_season_fks`, which cannot help: it only fills `season IS NULL` and never re-points a row) re-stamped the 25 W34/2026 rows `2025-2026 → 2026-2027`, the season their own ISO-week Monday (2026-08-17) falls in. Idempotent, `--dry-run` first, verified: a second run reports nothing, and `t_seller` now gets 25 rows on W34 where it got 0. Rows the command cannot place — weeks no season covers — are skipped and reported, never guessed (4 new tests in `tests_season_backfill.py` beside `backfill_season_fks`'s own, including that gap-skip branch, since FINDINGS_BACKLOG points at this command as the shape to copy for S1). **The rest of the drift is recorded, not fixed** (owner decision, 2026-08-23; docs/FINDINGS_BACKLOG.md S1/S2) — the same audit across every season-scoped table found **1 `QuotaIssuance` and 6 `WeeklyTruckAllocation`** rows still disagreeing with their own dates (same `1 → 3` shape), plus **14 rows in the July 2026 hole** between season 1's `end_date` (2026-06-30) and season 3's `start_date` (2026-08-01), which no season covers at all — those cannot be assigned by any date-matching backfill until a season's range moves.
- **Seller's panel trimmed to the sell plan, and the seller can now open their own week (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + fix(frontend)).** Four owner-reported items on one screen, all rooted in the same place. **(1) The "error loading data".** `seller` holds one real page, `export.quota.local_sell`, and `canSeePage()` deliberately treats access to any CHILD page as access to the parent — right for the sidebar (the seller must reach `/export/quota` to get their grid), wrong for everything on it. `canSeeQuota` was `canSeePage(user, 'export.quota')`, so it resolved **true** for them: the page fired `GET /export/quota-dashboard/`, the backend 403'd it (`resource_code = 'quota_issuance'`, which `seller` does not hold), and the red **"Failed to load quota data"** banner rendered on every visit — the very case `useQuotaDashboard`'s `enabled` flag was added to prevent. The three gates moved into `quotaPanelAccess(user)` (`QuotaDashboard.helpers.ts`): `canSeeQuota` now reads `canDo(user, 'quota_issuance', 'view')`, the exact `resource_code` the endpoint enforces, so the UI asks the question the API answers. **(2) Quota machinery hidden.** With that gate correct the seller loses the KPI pipeline, the period filter panel, the kg/ton toggle, and the Quota Usage / Issuance Log / Firm Breakdown tabs. `canSeeAnalytics` (Firm Chart, Weekly Trend — both rendered from the dashboard payload) became `canSeeQuota && canDo(user,'local_sell_plan','view')`; the first term is "this is quota data", the second is what still excludes `document_team`. A **single** visible tab now renders bare, without the `<Tabs>` strip — chrome offering no choice, and `activeTab` is seeded by `useState(defaultTab)` captured on the first render, so a lone `<Tabs>` can point at a key absent from `items` and render nothing. The header reads **"Sell Plan"** (`quota_dashboard.title_local_sell`, tk/ru/en) instead of "Quota Dashboard" for this audience. **(3) Initialize Week.** `initialize-week` moved from `LOCAL_SELL_APPROVE` to `LOCAL_SELL_WRITE` (`views_planning.py`) so the seller opens their own week rather than waiting on an export_manager — seeding all-zero draft rows commits to nothing, and every gate that *decides* something (approve, reject, bulk-approve) stays APPROVE-only. The button's frontend gate went `isManager` → `canEdit` to match. `boss` is in neither set and stays blocked, which is why the grid uses `canDoBackendGated`. **(4) Fleet Map** dropped from the seller's sidebar (`AppLayout` `/transport/map` roles) — unlike `/worklog` and `/team/kpi` beside it, that list is not an every-authenticated-user grant; the route stays a bare `<ProtectedRoute>`, so a typed URL still reaches it. Also fixed while in there: `LocalSellPlanGrid` initialized into `useSeasons().find(is_active)` while listing by `useSelectedSeason()` — an initialize that appeared to do nothing whenever the header switcher pointed at another season — and that `useSeasons()` call was a second `GET /export/admin/seasons/` **403** for the seller, who has no `season.can_view`. It now reads `useSelectedSeason()`, off `/auth/me/` — and because that season CAN be a closed one, `canEdit`/`isManager` are now ANDed with `useSeasonReadOnly()`, which this grid (alone among the editable grids) never composed, so a closed season no longer renders enabled controls that 409. **`season.can_view` was deliberately NOT granted to `seller`** — that would fix a symptom by widening the role this change exists to narrow. Verified against the live permission matrix for all 15 roles (2026-08-23): the only role whose `canSeeQuota` changes is `seller`, and no role holds `quota_usage` without `quota_issuance`, so one flag covers both tabs. 6 new frontend tests + 6 new backend tests; 439 frontend tests pass, 152 backend tests across the four touched suites pass, `tsc` clean.
- **Truck Forecast (`/export/trucks`) removed from every sidebar (chore(frontend)).** Owner request: the page is read-only and shows the same `weekly_truck_allocations` rows that the *Truck allocation* collapsible at the bottom of `/export/plan` both shows **and** edits, so it earned no nav slot in either composition. Dropped from `BOSS_MENU_GROUPS` (`nav.group_planning`) and `STAFF_MENU_GROUPS` (`nav.group_export`), and its now-unreachable `ITEMS` entry deleted; `IconTruck` stays (three other items use it). **Route, page file, `export.trucks` page code and the `nav.trucks` i18n keys are all untouched** — the page still resolves at `/export/trucks` by direct URL, with its `ROUTE_PAGE_MAP` entry in `utils/permissions.ts` (so `ProtectedRoute` keeps gating it), its breadcrumb label (`ROUTE_LABELS`) and its feedback path label all intact. The withheld-pages comment beside `/export/drafts` and `/export/assign` was extended to cover it, because an "orphaned routes" sweep (421068f) is how those two got into the menu in the first place. `AppLayout.menuGroups.test.tsx` updated: boss 47 → 46 keys, staff 49 → 48 — and three counts baked into its test names and comments were **already** stale before this change (a "46" comment over a 47-entry array, a "48 route keys" test name asserting 49), so every number in that file was resynced against the arrays rather than decremented. 10 tests pass, `tsc` clean.
- **The local sell plan autosaves and auto-submits on the first non-zero save (`feat(p3)` + `feat(frontend)`).** "Submit All" is gone: leaving a cell saves it, and the first day carrying a value sends the whole week for approval in the same request. Days that are still empty stay fillable after submit; a day that already holds a value is locked to non-approvers with `409 cell_locked_after_submit`. An all-zero save stays `draft`.
- **Fleet Map access is enforced at the endpoint, not only by the `AppLayout` nav list (`fix(p3)`).** The seller's missing sidebar link was presentation; the 403 is the boundary. Both layers are now pinned by tests.

### Fixed
- **The contract generator's dialog still said "(Kazakhstan)" (`fix(p4)`).** Follow-up to 3acd9b3: the gate, tooltip and backend were all un-gated, but `contracts.generate.title` — the heading of the dialog the button opens — kept the country in all three languages. Dropped it. Also added `BY` (`Belarusyň` / `Беларуси`) to `_COUNTRY_GENITIVE`; a Belarus contract has since appeared and was the last of 77 still blocked.
- **The quota dashboard no longer breaks for roles that cannot list seasons (`fix(p3)`).** The quota tabs are gated on `quota_issuance.can_view` but the page's season dropdown is fed by `GET /export/admin/seasons/`, gated on `season.can_view` — `document_team`, `loading_dept_head` and `loading_dept_head_deputy` hold the first and not the second, so every tab rendered for them while the seasons request 403'd and the page ended up with no season id at all. The Firm Quota tab is `enabled: !!seasonId`, so it silently never fetched (an empty table with no error), and the dashboard query sent `season=0`, which `resolve_season()` resolves as a real id lookup and 404s — breaking the KPI row and the Firm Breakdown / Chart / Weekly tabs as well. The backend was never wrong: `/quota-firm-summary/` answers 200 with data for all seven roles. New `resolveQuotaSeasonId()` falls back to `user.active_season.id` from `/auth/me/`, which every authenticated role carries, and the `?? 0` sentinel is replaced by an `enabled` guard. Those three roles are pinned to the active season rather than granted `season.can_view` (owner's call) — their dropdown stays empty because they genuinely cannot enumerate seasons. Deliberately reads `/auth/me/` and not `useSelectedSeason()`, which also carries the global header switcher's selection. 5 new tests, written failing first.
- **Uploaded signatures and seals reported success but rendered as broken images (`fix(p3)` + `fix(frontend)` + `fix(docker)`).** Three stacked defects; the first is the one that actually reached the user. **(1) The API handed the browser a url that does not resolve.** DRF's stock `FileField` returns `request.build_absolute_uri(value.url)` — an absolute url built from the `Host` header Django received — and *neither* proxy in front of this app sets that header to the browser's origin. Production nginx forwards `proxy_set_header Host $host`, and nginx's `$host` **drops the port**: a browser on `http://10.10.11.25:8080` was handed `http://10.10.11.25/media/import_firms/seals/shah.png`, i.e. port 80, which 404s — verified against the live host, and the exact url the user reported. The Vite dev proxy sets `changeOrigin: true`, which rewrites the Host to the proxy *target*, so dev answered `http://127.0.0.1:8000/media/...` — a url that resolves only for a browser running **on** the dev machine, which is why "it works in dev" held right up until someone opened dev from another PC. New `apps.core.serializer_fields.RelativeFileField` returns the **root-relative** `/media/...` path instead, same-origin by construction and immune to any proxy header config; applied to `ExportFirm`/`ImportFirm` `director_signature` + `director_seal` and `FeedbackAttachment.file` (5 fields — the only media the frontend renders; contract attachments go through an authenticated download view and were never affected). Only `to_representation` is overridden, so the multipart PATCH that *uploads* a seal is untouched — pinned by an upload round-trip test. **(2) Neither server had a route for `/media/`.** `frontend/nginx.conf` defined `location /`, `/api/` and `/ws/` but no `/media/`, so the request fell through to the SPA's `try_files … /index.html` and an `<img src>` received **200 `text/html`** (614 bytes of `index.html`, confirmed by curl). `frontend/vite.config.ts` had the identical hole in dev. nginx now serves `/media/` with `alias /var/www/media/` and Vite proxies `/media` to Django. nginx serves the files **directly rather than proxying to Django**: the media route in `config/urls.py` is `static()`, a no-op whenever `DJANGO_DEBUG=False`, so a `proxy_pass` would have broken the day DEBUG is turned off (which `docker-compose.prod.yml` already plans). **(3) Production mounted no media directory at all.** Prod drops the dev bind mount `./backend:/app`, so uploads landed in the container's **writable layer** and were destroyed by the next `up -d --build`. `./backend/media` is now bind-mounted read-write at `/app/media` on `backend` and read-only at `/var/www/media` on `frontend` — a **host bind, not a named volume**, deliberately: the beta host already holds the live uploads at that path and a named volume would have mounted over the directory and hidden them. 4 new tests in `apps/core/tests_media_urls.py`; 122 backend tests pass across the feedback, sheet-settings-admin, permission-matrix and reference-data suites. `api-contract` skill documents the new field shape. **Dev half verified live** — the API now returns `/media/import_firms/seals/shah.png` and `:3000` serves it `200 image/png`. **Two known properties, unchanged by this fix:** `/media/` is served **unauthenticated** (same as Django's own debug serving — fine on the trusted LAN, revisit with `X-Accel-Redirect` if exposed wider), and media is **per-host** — dev and the beta server share the database `YIGIT_PLATFROM_NEW` but not the media directory, so `Yumak.jpg` and `sah_gol.png` (uploaded on the server) have no bytes on dev and vice-versa. **Deliberately NOT changed:** nginx's `Host $host` → `$http_host`, which would fix the dropped port at source for *every* absolute url Django builds — the relative-url fix makes it unnecessary for media, the frontend never consumes DRF's absolute `next`/`previous` pagination links (checked), and touching the Host header reaches `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, django-axes lockout keying and CSRF. Recorded in `docs/FINDINGS_BACKLOG.md`. `/static/` has the same routing hole and is likewise left alone — it is what `DJANGO_DEBUG=True` currently covers for.
- **27 of the backend suite's 42 failures were stale tests, not broken code (test(p3)).** Fixed in three unrelated modules, test files only — no production code, models or migrations changed, because none of the three was a production bug. (1) `tests_pallet_manifest` (15) died in `setUp` on `Season.objects.get_or_create(year=2025, …)`; `Season` carries `name`/`start_date`/`end_date`/`is_active` and has no `year`, so every test in the module raised `FieldError` before reaching a request — two lines fixed all 15, and the entry in `PRE_EXISTING_TEST_FAILURES.md` that filed these under "missing `seed_permissions`" was a misattribution, now corrected. (2) `tests_comments` (11 of 13) created a `Shipment` with no `status` against a NOT NULL `status_id`; added a `draft` `ShipmentStatusType` to the `_make_shipment` fixture. (3) `tests_permission_matrix` (1) imported `apps.core.migrations.0016_demote_existing_director`, which the schema-collapse refactor (`932d950`) archived to `backend/_pre_collapse_backup/core/` with all 56 pre-collapse migrations; the rule it guarded (director loses `admin.*`, EM loses `admin.permissions`) now lives in `seed_permissions.PAGE_DEFAULTS`, so it was re-pointed at `seed_permissions --reset` — **not** plain seed, which is `get_or_create` and by design will not repair an already-stale `is_visible=True` row, making a plain-seed retarget a duplicate of an existing test. Verified together in one process: `Ran 51 tests … OK`. Remaining: 15 of 1832 — 6 `tests_boss_analytics` (needs a rewrite onto `HarvestDayEntry`) and 9 catalogued as the new Category 6.
- **Whole-sheet permission sanity sweep + a third silent gap it found: Mergen (`transport`) could never log Greenhouse Departure (fix(p3)).** After two rounds of "fix one role's Sheet permission, discover another role's data doesn't match the fixed mechanism," added `TestEveryRoleCanEditItsOwnSheetRow` (`tests_sheet_perms.py`): walks every `DEFAULT_SHEET_ROWS` entry, resolves its real owning role(s) via `WHO_TO_ROLE` (the who-key→role table `backfill_sheet_row_defaults.py` already uses to seed `SheetRowRoleTrigger`, confirmed with the user 2026-06-02), and asserts that role can edit the field under a **real** `seed_permissions()` run — not a hand-crafted grant, which is what let both prior incidents through their own test suites unnoticed. First run found a third, previously unreported gap: `departed_at` (R21, "Ýük galyndysy ýaşyl jaýdan gitdi" / greenhouse departure) has carried `default_who_key: sheet.who.mergen` since the row was added (2026-04-29, unchanged), and the live `SheetRowSetting.role_triggers` for it already names `transport` alongside `loading_dept_head`/deputy — but `FIELD_DEFAULTS['transport']` never granted the field, only the loading department's. Live and in every test run since day one, transport was silently blocked from a row that's been labeled as theirs the whole time; masked because the loading department's own grant made the cell "work" for most edits. Added `'departed_at'` to `transport`'s field list; `seed_permissions` re-run on the live dev DB, verified against a real `transport`-role account. Sweep test is now green for every role/row pair with a `WHO_TO_ROLE` mapping. 124-test regression sweep clean except the same already-tracked pre-existing failures.
- **Soltanmyrat (`loading_dept_head`) lost Sheet access to his own Harvest Block cell — a regression from the `firm_splits`/`block_sources` junction-permission fix below (fix(p3)).** That fix made `can_edit_sheet_field`/`get_sheet_edit_map` check the `shipment_block_source` junction resource for `block_sources` instead of `'shipment'` — correct in general, but `seed_permissions`' `FIELD_DEFAULTS` had `loading_dept_head`'s R8 grant sitting under `'shipment'` (`'block_sources'` in that role's field list) with **no matching `shipment_block_source` grant anywhere** — the opposite convention from `document_team`'s `firm_splits` setup, which the mechanism fix was modeled on. So the mechanism was right and the seed data was wrong for this one role: Soltanmyrat's cell went from working (old, less-correct check happened to match his old-shaped grant) to blocked (new, correct check has nothing to match). `warehouse_chief` and `loading_dept_head_deputy` had no grant at all either way — same latent gap, not yet reported. Moved the R8 grant to `'shipment_block_source': ['*']` for both `loading_dept_head` and `warehouse_chief` (`loading_dept_head_deputy` inherits via its existing deep-copy of the head's dict); the stale `('loading_dept_head'/'loading_dept_head_deputy', 'shipment', 'block_sources')` rows were deleted from the live dev DB, and `seed_permissions` was re-run to insert the three new junction grants. New `TestJunctionFieldSeedDataGrantsRealRoles` (`tests_sheet_perms.py`, 4 tests, 44 in the module) seeds via the **real** `seed_permissions` command rather than a hand-crafted grant — the gap that let this regression through `TestJunctionFieldDelegation` unnoticed, since that suite only ever tested the delegation mechanism in isolation. Verified RED against the un-fixed `FIELD_DEFAULTS` (git stash) before fixing, and against Soltanmyrat's real account (`soltanmyrad`) via `manage.py shell` after. Regression swept clean otherwise (92 tests across `tests_shipment_sheet`, `tests_role_lifecycle`, `tests_permission_matrix`) — only the same already-tracked pre-existing failures.
- **`document_team` (Sulgun) could not edit Export Firm on the Sheet despite holding the permission — two separate bugs, same root cause (fix(p3)).** `firm_splits` (R9, Export Firm) and `block_sources` (R8, Harvest Block) are junction-table rows — their permissions are scoped to `shipment_firm_split`/`shipment_block_source`, per `RESOURCE_FIELDS` in `permission_registry.py` — but two independent layers of the stack field/resource-perm-checked `'shipment'` instead. **(1) The Sheet's edit gate** (`can_edit_sheet_field` / `get_sheet_edit_map`, `apps/core/permissions.py`) field-perm-checked resource `'shipment'` for both rows. `'firm_splits'`/`'block_sources'` can never appear in a `'shipment'` grant (they aren't in that resource's field list), so the check was unconditionally `False` for every role except the three hard-coded bypasses (`admin`, `director`, `export_manager`) — regardless of grants on the junction resource itself, e.g. `document_team`'s real `shipment_firm_split: ['*']`. New `_JUNCTION_FIELD_DELEGATES` map (mirrors the existing `_VIRTUAL_FIELD_DELEGATES` pattern for `transit_days_temp`, but crosses resources instead of staying within `'shipment'`) resolves these two sheet rows to their real `(resource_code, field_name)` before the field-perm check, in both `can_edit_sheet_field` (inline PATCH gate) and `get_sheet_edit_map` (the batch map behind `can_current_user_edit`), so the two never disagree. Also fixes the mirror-image leak the same bug caused: a role holding a **wildcard** `'*'` grant on `'shipment'` (e.g. `warehouse_chief`) was incorrectly treated as able to edit `firm_splits` too. 4 new tests in `tests_sheet_perms.py` (`TestJunctionFieldDelegation`, 40 in the module). **(2) The actual save, `POST /shipments/{id}/firm-splits/` (`set_firm_splits`) and its `block-sources` sibling** (`set_block_sources`), fixing (1) surfaced this: both actions inherit `ShipmentViewSet`'s class-level `DynamicResourcePermission(resource_code='shipment')`, which maps `POST → shipment.can_create`. `document_team` correctly has `shipment.can_create = False` (must not create new shipments) but full CRUD on `shipment_firm_split` (`can_edit=True` — "Sulgun manages firm splits") — a role/resource mismatch identical in shape to bug (1), one layer down. So opening the now-editable cell and hitting Save 403'd with DRF's generic *"You do not have permission to perform this action."* New `junction_write_permission(resource_code)` factory in `apps/core/permissions.py` (same closure-factory style as the existing `write_permission`/`firm_write_permission`) checks the junction resource's own `can_edit` instead — wired into `ShipmentViewSet.get_permissions()` for both actions, following the exact precedent already set there for `set_sales_report` (a POST that writes a different resource than the ViewSet default). 3 new tests (`SheetJunctionEndpointResourcePermissionTests` in `tests_shipment_sheet.py`): `document_team` can now POST firm-splits, a role with neither `shipment.can_create` nor the junction grant still 403s, and `block_sources` was proven fixed generically (not a firm-splits-only special case) using a synthetic grant on `transport`. Both fixes verified against Sulgun's real account and live permission rows via `manage.py shell` before landing. **Full regression sweep** (`tests_sheet_perms`, `tests_shipment_sheet`, `tests_shipment_swap`, `tests_shipment_field_audit`, `tests_role_lifecycle`, `tests_permission_matrix`, 160 tests): only the already-tracked pre-existing failures remain (5 `SheetJunctionEndpointTests` quota-balance 400s, 3 `tests_shipment_swap` 403s, 1 `tests_permission_matrix` stale-migration-name error) — each verified unchanged by this fix, per `docs/PRE_EXISTING_TEST_FAILURES.md`.
- **A quota issuance stamped to the wrong season blocks firm assignment for everyone (fix(p3) + data(p3)).** New `fix_quota_issuance_seasons` management command, third sibling of `backfill_season_fks` (fills NULLs only) and `fix_local_sell_plan_seasons` (repairs the sell plan): it re-stamps each `QuotaIssuance` to the season its own `issue_date` falls in. An issuance carries the season that was **active when it was entered**, and quota for a new season routinely arrives before an admin opens that season — so it lands in the old one, and under D11 (quota never crosses a season boundary) the active season is left with **no quota at all**. With expiry now applied to the balance, the Sheet's firm-split gate then refuses every firm: the drift stopped being a reporting skew and became an operational block. `--dry-run` first, idempotent, and it busts the FIFO + balance caches after writing (both are season-keyed, so 60 s of stale reads would look exactly like the command doing nothing). One guard the sell-plan sibling has no need for: it **never writes across a closed season** in either direction, since that would rewrite frozen history behind the D10 write-freeze — such rows are skipped and named. Dates no season covers are skipped too, never guessed (`QuotaIssuance#34` used to sit in the July-2026 hole, FINDINGS_BACKLOG S2). 6 tests in `tests_season_backfill.py` (18 in the module). **On the live DB it found nothing to re-stamp** — the one mismatched row, issuance #35 (HG, 1,234,000 kg, 2026-08-22), had been deleted by the owner as test data minutes earlier, along with #33 and #34. It stays as the tool for the next occurrence. **Data, same session:** the 26 `QuotaUsageRecord` rows in season 2026-2027 (ids 992-1017, 1,686,664 kg across 22 firms) were deleted at the owner's instruction — all created by `admin` on 2026-08-21 during the role/permission testing, all with `shipment` NULL and values like 1 / 3 / 23 / 123 / 231 / 1234 / 12345, so nothing real referenced them. The active season now holds zero issuances and zero usage, i.e. a clean slate; **until real quota is entered on `/export/quota` with the switcher on 2026-2027, no firm can be added to a truck's splits.**
- **Picking a driver fills the shipment's phone from the registry, when the registry has one (feat(frontend)).** New `driverPatchFields()` in `components/DriverSelect.tsx` holds the rule in one place: a pick always writes `driver_id` + `driver_name`, and adds `driver_phone` only when the registry row carries a non-blank number. Z_TIRWEB supplies no phones while 80 of the 146 shipments hold one an operator typed by hand, so an unconditional write would only erase that; a phone entered in the Fleet Admin Drivers tab is newer information and does replace what is on the shipment. Clearing the driver leaves R28 untouched — it is its own cell with its own history. All three surfaces inherit this via `DriverSelect`, whose `onChange` now emits `(id, name, phone)`, and the Sheet's undo snapshot carries `driver_phone` only on the patches that actually write it.
- **The Sheet offered ~20 export firms as having quota when exactly one did (fix(p3)).** `compute_firm_quota_balances()` — the source behind the `firm_splits` dropdown's `⚠ no quota` gate and the matching `POST /shipments/{id}/firm-splits/` block — summed **every** kg issued in the season minus every kg committed, and never looked at `QuotaIssuance.validity`. A June leftover therefore still read as quota on 2026-08-23, two months after its window closed: the live DB shows 21 firms with a positive season-to-date balance while only **HG** holds a live allocation (issuance #35, 2026-08-22, `this_month`). It now drops lapsed allocations from `issued_kg` **and** `used_kg`, reusing the `quota_expiry_date()` / `_allocs_by_firm()` / `_fifo_consume()` trio the dashboard's *expired unused* column already runs on — a second expiry rule here is precisely how the two screens would start disagreeing about the same firm. `today` defaults to `timezone.localdate()`, the same source `build_quota_dashboard()` uses, so both flip on the same day; the lapse test is `expiry < today`, so quota is still spendable **on** its expiry date. **Consequence, by design:** the gate is a hard block on both ends, so a firm whose quota has lapsed cannot be *added* to a firm split until new quota is issued — firms already on a split are exempt (`existing_firm_ids`), so existing splits stay editable. Measured on the live DB (2026-08-23): season **2025-2026** goes 21 firms with a positive balance → **1** (HG, 1,215,000 kg of issuance #35). Season **2026-2027**, which became the active one mid-day, shows **0** firms with quota — and did so before this change too (it holds usage but not a single issuance, so every balance was already ≤ 0 there). See the *Not fixed* note below: that is the season-stamp gap, not the expiry rule. Two side effects worth naming: FIFO charges usage to the oldest allocation, lapsed ones included, so August kg draw from a June row and `remaining_kg` is optimistic by that much (deliberate — the alternative counts the same kg twice, here and as expired-unused); and per-allocation accounting floors at 0, so the over-committed AB (386,100 used vs 325,000 issued) reads `0` rather than `-61,100`, which blocks either way. `aggregate_quota_issued_for_season()` was deleted with its last caller. 7 new tests in `tests_quota_firm_balances.py` (16 in the module): lapsed-only, the expiry-date boundary in both directions, `next_month` validity, usage charged to the lapsed row first, usage spilling into the live row, over-use flooring at 0. Fixtures in `tests_quota_season_d11.py` and `tests_shipment_sheet.py` that granted quota on hard-coded past dates now pin `today` or issue on `timezone.localdate()`. 328 backend tests across the quota/season/sheet suites pass; the 5 pre-existing `SheetJunctionEndpointTests` firm-split failures were verified unchanged against the same suite without this change. **Not fixed (data, needs an owner decision):** all 25 issuances — #35 included, dated 2026-08-22, inside season 2026-2027's range — are stamped **season 1**, while season 3 (2026-2027) is now the active one. Under the active season no firm holds quota at all, so the firm-split gate refuses **every** firm until an issuance is stamped there. This is the `QuotaIssuance` row already recorded as S1 in `docs/FINDINGS_BACKLOG.md`, now with an operational consequence attached; re-stamping is a live-data write and was deliberately not performed here. Note that #35's `validity` is `this_month`, so re-stamping alone buys until 2026-08-31.
- **The season you meant to close lost its Close button the moment you opened its successor (fix(frontend)).** `SeasonsPage` gated the Close action on `record.status === 'ACTIVE'`, which made the ordinary end-of-season sequence impossible: opening next year's season demotes this year's to UPCOMING, so the season an admin actually meant to close lost its Close button **permanently**, while the newly opened season gained one — in the row that had just jumped to the top, since the grid default-sorts by `is_active`. The only Close button on screen belonged to the season that must not be closed, and two wrong seasons were closed that way on 2026-08-22. The restriction was UI-only: `close_season()` refuses just an already-closed season, and `SeasonViewSet.close` adds nothing beyond `season.can_edit`. Now gated on `status !== 'CLOSED'`, matching the Edit and Delete gates on the same row, and **Open renders before Close** so the benign action keeps the leftmost slot on the one row that shows both. The existing test asserted UPCOMING shows "never Close" — it pinned the defect as if it were a guarantee, and is rewritten; a second test pins the button order.
- **Driver Logo code surfaced in the API and both driver UIs (feat(frontend)).** `logo_ref` and `driver_logo_code` are now returned by `DriverSerializer` as read-only — the import owns them and `driver_logo_code` is the key the duplicate retirement runs on, so an editable field would be silently reverted on the next run. Fleet Admin's Drivers tab gains a sortable Logo-code column that its client-side filter also searches. `DriverSelect` appends the code to an option's label only for names that actually repeat, so the two `BATYROW BAYRAMMYRAT` rows (ids 30/31, distinct codes, genuinely two people) are pickable apart while the other 150 rows stay uncluttered; the label remains a plain string so `optionFilterProp="label"` keeps working and the code becomes searchable on the rows that show it.
- **"Expired unused" quota counted kg that had in fact been used (fix(p3)).** The Firm breakdown column and its KPI card summed the **whole** lapsed allocation, so quota that was issued, exported against, and only then ran out of validity was reported as waste — the opposite of what the label promises. `aggregate_quota_expired()` now counts `kg_quota − consumed`, where `consumed` comes from the FIFO walk (a firm spends its oldest allocation first). That walk was two copies of the same loop; it is now one `_fifo_consume()` shared by `compute_fifo_usage()` (season-scoped, feeds the Issuances tab's `used_kg`) and the dashboard, so the two surfaces can no longer disagree about which allocation a kg came from. **The published figure drops** wherever quota was used before lapsing — smaller numbers than yesterday are the fix, not missing data. Two scoping traps came out of it: the usage total is read through the same date window as the allocations it is consumed against, and it is filtered by `product_type` — `aggregate_quota_used()` is product-agnostic by default (that is the `used_kg` KPI's historical definition and was left alone), while production holds 16 pepper usage rows that would otherwise have drawn down tomato quota. `aggregate_quota_used()` gained an optional `product_type` for that one call. 8 new tests in `tests_quota_dashboard_expiry.py` (20 in the module): partial use, full use, over-consumption clamping at 0, FIFO order pinned by a live allocation issued after the lapsed one, drafts ignored, other-product usage ignored, out-of-window usage ignored, KPI-vs-rows reconciliation. **Known defect surfaced, not fixed:** the `used_kg` KPI itself still sums every product, so the tomato dashboard includes those 16 pepper rows.
- **Domestic Sales totalled its weight column as text (fix(frontend)).** `DomesticSale.weight_kg` is a model `DecimalField`, so it arrives as `"1500.00"` while `IDomesticSale` declares `number` — the page's `rows.reduce((s, r) => s + r.weight_kg, 0)` concatenated instead of adding, the same failure as the quota Total row. Coerced in `useDomesticSales`'s `queryFn` (`weight_kg`, `price_per_kg`), which is the hook's only consumer. The per-row `Number(...)` patches already sitting on `price_per_kg` at the usage sites are what made this findable. `SalesReportPage`'s expense total was checked in the same sweep and is **not** affected — `toSavedRow` already coerces `amount_local`.
- **Driver registry: Logo accounting identifiers imported, and the one genuine duplicate driver retired (feat(p3) + data(p3)).** `logo_ref` and `driver_logo_code` were skipped by the original import as TIR-app internals; they are the driver's identity in the Logo accounting system and are populated on all 152 rows. `driver_logo_code` is the reliable same-person key that names cannot be: ids 99 `SALAROW TOYLY` and 113 `TOYLY SALAROW` share `195.02.S008` and are one person with the words swapped, while ids 30/31 carry the identical name `BATYROW BAYRAMMYRAT` on distinct codes and are two people. `_deactivate_duplicate_drivers()` runs at the end of every import and retires any row whose code duplicates a lower-id one, leaving 151 of 152 active. It deactivates rather than deletes, since Z_TIRWEB still holds the row and a delete would return on the next import while `is_active` — absent from the upsert defaults — survives; rows with a blank code are skipped. Neither column is unique-constrained, because the source itself violates that and a constraint would make the import fail instead of deduplicating. Migration `transport/0005`.
- **Driver registry picker on ShipmentDetail and the edit drawer, closing the asymmetry with Sheet R27 (fix(frontend)).** `driver_name` was still `inputType: 'text'` in `shipmentEditConfig`, so `driver_id` was written only from the Sheet — and retyping the name in the drawer left `driver_id` pointing at the previously selected person, making the link wrong rather than absent. New `ShipmentDriverSelector` mirrors `ShipmentTruckSelector`: it writes `driver_id` + `driver_name` in one `useShipmentPatchMulti` call, returns early when the pick is unchanged (no PATCH, no audit row for a no-op), and on clear sends `{driver_id: null, driver_name: ''}` so the id and the name never drift apart. `DRIVER_NAME_FIELD` is exported and added to `excludeKeys` beside `TRUCK_PLATE_FIELD`, so the transport group renders it once standalone and the completeness chip still counts it; `driver_phone` stays an ordinary text row. Gapy-Satys keeps the plain text field on all three surfaces. The option list, filtering and inline "+ Add" were extracted into `components/DriverSelect.tsx` (frontend/CLAUDE.md's STRICT self-fetching-control rule, now that there is a second consumer); its `onChange` emits `(id, name)` because every consumer writes both columns, and `SheetDriverSelectEditor` was refactored onto it, dropping its local just-created-name ref. Known limitation carried over from the truck picker: the "+ Add" guard matches names exactly, so transliteration variants (`Abayev`/`Abayew`) can still produce a duplicate registry row.
- **The quota Firm breakdown mixed two seasons in one row (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** Every figure on that tab is anchored on the season-clamped date window `/quota-dashboard/` is given — except **"Expired unused"**, which the browser computed from its own `useQuotaIssuances()` fetch. That hook reads the **global** season switcher (`useSelectedSeason()`), while sales/issued/used follow the quota page's **own** season dropdown, so moving one selector and not the other left half of every row describing a different season. With all 25 issuances on the live DB stamped to `2025-2026`, the 2026-2027 breakdown showed the prior season's expired quota — and with the switcher on 2026-2027 the column read `0` everywhere instead, so neither position was right. `expired_kg` is now computed server-side by `aggregate_quota_expired()` over the same `fetch_issuances()` list `weekly_flow` already consumes, and shipped on each `per_firm` row plus as a `kpis.expired_kg`; `QuotaDashboard` no longer fetches issuances at all. `quota_expiry_date()` is a port of the frontend's `computeExpiry()` (`next_month` and `this_and_next` share an expiry — end of the following month — differing only in when the quota *starts*). The **second half of the same bug** is closed with it: `QuotaPerFirmTable`'s Total row summed `expiredPerFirm` across every firm the browser had fetched while the column rendered only firms present in `per_firm`, so the footer could not be reconciled against the visible rows — it now sums the rendered rows. `build_quota_dashboard()` stays date-driven per §4.7; `today` was added purely so tests can pin expiry. 12 new tests (`tests_quota_dashboard_expiry.py`) covering both the service and the endpoint, 78 quota/dashboard tests pass, 433 frontend tests pass, `tsc` clean. **Not fixed, reported:** `expired_kg` counts the full allocation rather than the unused remainder despite the column label — faithful to the frontend it replaces.
- **A deleted season left every season-scoped query 404ing, unrecoverably by refresh (fix(frontend)).** Deleting season `2026-2027` (id 2) and recreating it as id 3 left an open tab on `?season=2`; `harvest-plans/` and `day-entries/` — and every other `SeasonScopedMixin` endpoint — then 404'd, because `resolve_season()` raises `NotFound` on an id that no longer exists (correct: an empty list for a season the user picked would be a lie). The client could not recover on its own — `useSelectedSeason()` resolves `URL ?? store ?? active` with the URL winning, and `useSeasonParam()`'s store→URL effect sees the dead id as "not the default" and writes it back into `?season=` on every load, so a refresh reproduced the broken state. The only escapes were hand-editing the address bar or the header switcher, which hides itself when fewer than two seasons are selectable. New `useSeasonFallback()` (`frontend/src/hooks/useSeasonFallback.ts`), mounted once in `AppLayout` beside `useSeasonParam()`: once the `['admin-seasons']` list has **settled** (`isSuccess && !isFetching`) and the selected id is absent from it, it switches to the active season and toasts `season.stale_season_reset`. Inert while the list is refetching (a season the user just created is legitimately absent from a 60s-stale cache — switching away would read as a failed create), inert for roles without `season.can_view` (a missing id is indistinguishable from one they may not list), and inert during the no-active-season gap. A `handledRef` set guards the intermediate render where the store already holds the active season but the URL still holds the dead one: `useSwitchSeason()` writes both in one handler, yet zustand's external-store subscription forces a re-render before the router flushes, and the URL wins — without the ref that render fires a second switch and a second toast (observed, not theorised). No extra network cost: `useSeasons()` is the query `SeasonSwitcher` already runs in that layout. 6 new tests; 430 frontend tests pass, `tsc` clean.
- **Quota dashboard's Total row printed a wall of concatenated digits in the "Expired unused" column (fix(frontend)).** `useQuotaIssuances` typed `kg_quota`/`used_kg` as `number`, but they come off a DRF `DecimalField` with `COERCE_DECIMAL_TO_STRING` at its default — i.e. strings (`"100000.00"`). Every `+` on them concatenated instead of adding, twice over: `perFirmExpired[firm] = (… ?? 0) + a.kg_quota` built `"0100000.00…"` per firm, then `QuotaPerFirmTable`'s `reduce((s, v) => s + v, 0)` glued those together — hence the leading `00`. `fmtWeight` passed the result through unharmed because `String.prototype.toLocaleString` ignores its arguments; in **ton** mode the same cell rendered `не число` instead, since `Number(garbage)` is `NaN`. Fixed at the fetch boundary — `useQuotaIssuances` now maps every allocation through `Number(...) || 0`, which makes the existing type declaration true rather than restating it as `string`. The Firm-breakdown column that showed the symptom has since moved server-side (see the season fix above), so the remaining consumer of these two fields is the **Issuances** tab, whose *Issued* / *Used* columns had been printing raw `100000.00 kg` rather than `100 000 kg`. `COERCE_DECIMAL_TO_STRING = False` was deliberately **not** set — repo-wide blast radius for one display total.
- **Join in the Sheet was dead for `admin` and `boss`, and silently greyed out for a whole class of valid pairs (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** Three frontend-only divergences from `_validate_join`, all in the join path: **(1)** `SheetToolbar.canJoin` carried its own `['export_manager','director']` literal and was never widened when the endpoint took `boss` (2026-08-15), so the Sheet's Join button simply did not render for `admin` or `boss` while the *same* users could join the *same* two drafts from the Shipment List or a Detail page. (`git log -L` confirms this list was **never** wider, so it is a standing divergence rather than a regression — it produces the "works on the shipment page, not in the Sheet" symptom only for an `admin`/`boss` session.) **(2)** `isSupplyDraft()` additionally demanded `country === null || created_by_role ∈ SUPPLY_ROLES`, a rule the API never had: a supply draft that had blocks *and* a country typed in, or one created by `export_manager`/`boss` instead of the loading dept, classified as neither destination nor supply, so `detectJoinDirection` returned `ambiguous` and the Join button stayed disabled on a pair the backend would have accepted. It is now the backend's rule verbatim — a draft with ≥1 block. **(3)** `toggleJoinSelection` silently ignored a third click once two columns were selected, so a mis-picked pair could not be corrected without leaving join mode — the column the operator clicked simply never highlighted. It now slides the window (drops the oldest, keeps the newest pair). **(4)** The three role gates (`SheetToolbar`, `joinDraftsGate`, `ShipmentDetailHero`) were three copies of the same list; they are now one `canUserJoin()` / `JOIN_ROLES` in `joinHelpers.ts`, so they cannot drift apart a fourth time.
- **An approved local sell plan was still editable by `export_manager`, `document_team` and `admin` (`fix(p3)`).** `perform_update` now refuses every PATCH on an `approved` row for every role with `409 plan_approved_locked`, evaluated on the pre-save instance and **before** any field comparison — so a PATCH of `export_firm` or `week_number` is refused too. The lock rule itself moved onto the model as `locked_day_fields(*, is_approver)`; no field change, no migration.

### Added
- **Test coverage for the Sheet's role-block grouping, the one part of this branch that had none (test(p3)).** `role_group` shipped with a new field, migration 0063, a `/sheet/` payload key and an admin write path, all covered only by the frontend's `sheetRoleBlocks.test.ts` — nothing server-side. Added `SheetRowSettingAdminTests`' new sibling `SheetRowRoleGroupTests` (PATCH round-trip + version bump, an unknown role code rejected 400 by `choices`, clearing back to blank, viewer 403, and the payload key for a **default** row, an unset row (null, the frontend's fallback branch) and a **custom** row — the last being the whole point of the feature, since `sheet.who.custom` maps to no role) and a new `tests_sheet_row_role_group.py` covering migration 0063's `RunPython` pair (backfill maps every default row, creates nothing, is idempotent, skips a row that already carries a value, leaves custom rows alone) plus a **drift guard** on the who-key → role table, which now exists in three hand-synced copies (`WHO_TO_ROLE`, the frontend's `WHO_KEY_ROLE`, and 0063's frozen `_WHO_SLUG_TO_ROLE`): the test parses the `.ts` literal and fails the moment the live backend and frontend copies disagree — the exact drift class behind both August Sheet-permission incidents. 21 new tests; `tests_sheet_settings_admin` + the new module 55/55. Coverage map and what is still untested: `docs/QA_COVERAGE_2026-08-27.md`.
- **The quota block reached the destination-draft modal, which had been a hole straight through it (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** A firm with no live quota was tagged, disabled and 400'd on the Sheet's R9 cell and on `POST /shipments/{id}/firm-splits/` — but **draft creation wrote `firm_splits` straight through**, so the Sheet's own *Create destination draft* modal could put exactly the firm the Sheet refuses onto a brand-new truck, with no warning on either tier. Backend: `_create_draft_shipment` now runs the same `compute_firm_quota_balances` check **inside** its atomic block, immediately before the split rows and the quota-usage sync that counts them (so two concurrent creates cannot both draw a firm's last kg), raising the byte-identical `400 {"error": "<firms> has no remaining quota and cannot be added to the split."}` — and the shipment header rolls back with it. There is **no** "already on the split" exemption here: every firm on a new draft is newly added. Frontend: `ExportFirmSelect` gained an opt-in **`checkQuota`** prop (tag + disable + the quota-page link), set by the draft modal and deliberately **not** by `ContractCreate`/`ContractSaleList`, which pick a firm for a different reason and must stay unfiltered. The predicate and the link are now shared rather than duplicated — `utils/quotaFirms.ts`'s `firmHasNoQuota()` and `components/QuotaPageLink.tsx` — and `SheetCellEditor` was refactored onto both, which also moves its permission gate inside the link. The currently-selected firm is never disabled, so a pre-filled blocked firm cannot wedge the field. **`tests_shipment_join.py`'s `test_loading_dept_head_draft_persists_firm_splits` now allocates quota first** — it was creating a draft with an unfunded firm and is the one existing test the new rule invalidated. 4 new backend tests (`tests_draft_quota_block.py`, incl. the rollback) + 5 new frontend tests; 36/36 join tests, 450 frontend tests, `tsc` clean. **Still not gated, deliberately:** `POST /shipments/{id}/join/` (it merges splits that already exist) and pepper, since every call site hardcodes `product_type='tomato'`.
- **The `Decimal`-arrives-as-a-string rule, written down (docs).** New section in the `api-contract` skill after three separate screens hit it. `COERCE_DECIMAL_TO_STRING` is at its DRF default, so a `DecimalField` (model or explicit) ships as a JSON **string** while a `SerializerMethodField` returning a `Decimal` ships as a JSON **number** — two fields of the same row can differ, which is exactly why `kg_quota` broke and `used_kg` did not. The rule: keep the TypeScript type `number` and make it true by coercing in the hook's `queryFn`, never at the usage site. Includes the failure signature — `+` concatenates while `-`, `*`, `/` and `<` coerce, so sorters and ratios keep working and only sums break; `fmtWeight()` passes the string through because `String.prototype.toLocaleString` ignores its arguments; ton mode turns it into `NaN`; and `tsc` cannot see any of it because the lie is on the wire.
- **The Sheet's export-firm dropdown now offers a way out of the quota dead end (feat(frontend)).** A firm with `remaining_kg <= 0` is tagged `⚠ no quota` and rendered `disabled` in the R9 `firm_splits` multiselect — correct, but a dead end: nothing in the dropdown said where quota comes from. When the list holds at least one such firm, the footer beside *Done* now shows an **`Open quota page →`** link to `/export/quota` (`sheet.firm_no_quota_link`, tk/ru/en). It is a plain anchor with `target="_blank"` rather than an in-app `navigate()`, because a same-tab route change unmounts the Sheet and drops the selection the operator was in the middle of making; `mousedown` `preventDefault` on the footer (which keeps the Select from committing early) does not cancel an anchor's click default, so the editor stays open behind the new tab. Gated on `canSeePage(user, 'export.quota')` — a single call, not the route's two-code array, because `canSeePage` already returns true for a holder of the `export.quota.local_sell` child page and so matches the route's OR logic exactly. Shown only when a no-quota firm is actually listed, so the common case is unchanged. The existing `sheet.firm_no_quota_error` toast was deliberately left without an action link — one entry point, not two. 3 new tests in `SheetCellEditor.test.tsx` (link renders / hidden when every firm has quota / hidden without the page permission); 433 frontend tests pass, `tsc` clean.
- **The Join UI now names the unmet requirements instead of "invalid pair" (feat(frontend)).** New `explainJoinBlockers(selected)` in `joinHelpers.ts` mirrors `_validate_join`'s gates in the same order and returns what is still missing, keyed by `shipment_code`: *select exactly 2* / *two different columns* / *`{code}` is not a draft* / *both columns have supply blocks* / *neither column has supply blocks* / *`{code}`: fill in the Country* / *`{code}`: fill in the Customer*. Rendered as a warning list in the Sheet's `JoinActionBar` (as soon as one column is picked, not only at two) and inside `JoinDraftsModal`'s previously opaque *ambiguous* alert. The two gates a client cannot evaluate — season open and caller role — are deliberately excluded and still arrive as the 400 toast, so the list never claims a requirement is met when the server may disagree. New `join_blockers.*` block (7 keys) in tk/ru/en. 12 new cases in `joinHelpers.test.ts` (25 passing).
- **The Sheet groups every field into one contiguous block per role (`feat(p3)` + `feat(frontend)`).** Rows are ordered by their existing `default_who_key` — the repo's single source of truth for "who fills this in", already shown in Col B — with a labelled band row above each block and a highlight on the blocks the current user can edit. The Sheet is transposed, so a role's "block of columns" is a contiguous run of **rows**. View order only, applied client-side in `ShipmentSheet.tsx`: the 13-row identity prefix stays pinned, and a user with a personal row order keeps it with **no** bands rather than a striped sheet.
- **Role blocks now group by role, not by person — and admins can manage the mapping (`fix(p3)` + `feat(p3)` + `fix(frontend)` + `feat(frontend)`).** Three defects reported the day after ship, plus a follow-up feature. **(1)** `default_who_key` names a specific person (Sirin, Gadam, Soltanmyrat…), not a role — several people share one role, so grouping by the raw who-key fragmented one department into several blocks and labelled each with a first name. New `WHO_KEY_ROLE` map in `sheetRoleBlocks.ts` resolves a who-key to its `ROLE_CHOICES` code before grouping/banding (9 blocks → 6: `loading_dept_head`, `transport`, `document_team`, `finansist`, `sales_rep`, `export_manager`). **(2)** Bands were not appearing at all for real users: every admin-added custom row shares the generic `sheet.who.custom` key, and with more than one on the sheet that key reappeared in a second, non-adjacent run and tripped the "same owner in two places" contiguity gate — silently suppressing every band on the whole sheet. Custom rows (and the empty `sheet.who.none`) are now transparent to grouping: never moved, never counted, never able to break contiguity for the real rows around them. **(3)** Writing the tests for (2) caught a real counting bug: a band's `rowCount` badge was computed from index distance, so an ungroupable row sitting inside a run's visual span inflated the count by one; now counted as actual matching rows. **(4)** A saved personal row order (`user_preferences.row_order`) permanently disables grouping for that user by design — and both the real `admin` and `export_manager` accounts already had one from ordinary drag-and-drop use before this feature shipped, so the two accounts most likely to test it saw no change at all. New **"Reset to role blocks"** button in the Sheet settings modal, shown only when the signed-in user has a personal order, PATCHes `row_order: []`. **(5), 2026-08-27:** `SheetRowSetting.role_group` (migration `0063_sheet_row_role_group`, backfilled from the same who-key→role table already confirmed with the user 2026-06-02 for `SheetRowRoleTrigger` seeding) is now an admin-editable override — Shipment Settings → Sheet Rows gained a "Role Block" column (distinct from the pre-existing "Trigger Roles" column, which grants edit access past a lock, not visual grouping) — so reassigning a row's block, or grouping one specific custom row, no longer needs a code deploy. The backfill also **corrected Aganazar's role**: the first version of `WHO_KEY_ROLE` guessed `sales_rep` from the one tail row he owns (`sales_report_date`); the confirmed decision is `export_manager`. **(6), 2026-08-27, same day:** the owner pointed at a live screenshot — `vehicle_condition`, `documents_status`, `transport_docs_given_at` and 4 others had a Role Block *set* in the admin tab but still rendered unbanded, because they were pinned by hardcoded **position** (`IDENTITY_ROW_COUNT = 13`), not because they needed to be. Shrunk to a field-key set, `PINNED_FIELD_KEYS = {shipment_code, country, customer, city, import_firm}` — the 5 fields an operator actually needs visible while scrolling; everything else (including the 7 rows above) now groups normally, growing `transport`/`document_team`/`export_manager`/`loading_dept_head` and reordering which block leads (`transport` now first, via the released `vehicle_condition`). Required filtering instead of slicing (`groupRowsByOwner` — the 5 pinned fields aren't contiguous in server order) and bumping `DEFAULT_FROZEN_ROW_COUNT` 13→5 **with a `localStorage` key version bump** (`ygt-sheet-freeze-v2`→`-v3`), or the live frozen-row setting would keep dominating the band start-index for anyone who'd ever touched the freeze picker. **(7):** found and fixed a pre-existing, unrelated bug while verifying (6) in-browser — `SheetGrid.tsx`'s row key was `row_number`, which is not unique (5 real collision pairs in the live data, e.g. `is_gapy_satys`/`firm_contracts` both 47); switched to `field_key`, the model's actual unique identity. 23 frontend unit tests (`sheetRoleBlocks.test.ts`, up from 19); 508/508 frontend suite tests pass, `tsc` clean; full backend suite (`apps.export apps.core apps.greenhouse`) 1753 tests, 42 pre-existing failures — 0 new (`tests_sheet_settings_admin` 35/35, `tests_shipment_sheet` 30/35 with the same 5 pre-existing `test_firm_splits_*` failures verified earlier); (6) and (7) confirmed live in a browser session, not just unit-tested.
- **Quota dashboard — "Firm Quota" tab (`feat(p3)` + `feat(frontend)`).** Answers "which firms hold how much quota right now": per-firm live remaining quota (the same figure the Sheet's firm-split gate blocks on), active issuance count and nearest expiry, served by `GET /api/v1/export/quota-firm-summary/`. Season-scoped and deliberately **not** period-filtered — quota expires in about a month, so a date window would be misleading. No serializer, no model, no migration.
- **`CanViewFleetMap` on `GET /transport/live-positions/` (`feat(p3)`).** The seller is now denied live truck positions server-side by a role deny-list. Deliberately not a new `transport.map` page_code: an unseeded page_code reads as DENY for all 14 other roles, so that route would have needed a data migration in the same release to avoid hiding the map from everyone.

### Added
