- **End-to-end role lifecycle test suite (`test(p3)`).** `apps/export/tests_role_lifecycle.py` — 31 tests walking a shipment from creation through all 11 lifecycle edges to completion and deletion, each step fired by the role that owns it, plus the Peregruz fork both ways, wrong-role refusal at every step, the four roles that own no edge, the privileged bypass, the draft join guard, API create/edit with their role refusals, soft-delete/restore/idempotency, admin-and-draft-only hard delete, and cancel gating. The step->role map is taken from `TRANSITIONS`, the only authority — `ShipmentStatusType.required_role` and `.step_order` disagree with it in three places and are deliberately not used. **The walk runs through `transition_to()` rather than `POST /transition/`, because the endpoint turns out to be unreachable for the roles that own the steps:** `DynamicResourcePermission` maps POST to `shipment.can_create`, which is 0 for document_team, sales_rep, warehouse_chief, transport and finansist, and `get_permissions` has no relaxation branch for `transition` — though it already carries three such branches for the same clash elsewhere, each commented as "would wrongly block" the owning role. The process survives in production only because auto-advance fires off an ordinary PATCH, where `can_edit` is 1. `TransitionEndpointReachabilityTests` asserts that behaviour as it stands today and will go red the moment it is fixed, which is the signal to move the walk back onto the endpoint. Also recorded: the live permission matrix has drifted from `seed_permissions` in two places — `warehouse_chief` lost the `can_create` its `export.drafts` grant exists for, `document_team` gained an unasked-for `can_delete`. Findings are documented in `docs/ROLE_ACCESS_AUDIT.md`, not fixed.
- **A test login for every role, and `docs/TEST_ACCOUNTS.md` as the single credential source (chore + docs).** 10 of the 15 roles had no usable login — `accountant` had **zero accounts of any kind**, and the other nine (`boss`, `director`, `document_team`, `finansist`, `greenhouse_manager`, `loading_dept_head`, `loading_dept_head_deputy`, `seller`, `weight_master`) existed only as real staff whose passwords are hashed and must not be reset. New `apps/core/management/commands/seed_test_users.py` creates one `t_<role>` account per uncovered role with a shared password, and is **structurally unable to touch a real account**: it only ever `create`s usernames it prefixes with `t_`, never calls `set_password` on an existing row, and skips any username already present. `--dry-run` previews and `--delete` removes the whole `t_*` set, so the change is one command to undo. Run against live `YIGIT_PLATFROM_NEW`: 10 rows created, each confirmed with `check_password`, user count 63 → 73. `docs/TEST_ACCOUNTS.md` pairs the logins with the role → visible-pages matrix read from the live `core_role_page_permissions` (280 visible rows of 645), one line per role on what it actually gates — which is what makes the credential list testable rather than just a login list. **Two silent traps surfaced while verifying:** the username `document_team` carries role `export_manager`, so anyone testing document_team gating with it gets wrong results and no error; and `admin`'s role is `admin`, not `export_manager` as `CREDENTIALS.md` claimed since AD-15 introduced the role. `CREDENTIALS.md` was left in place but headed with a superseded banner rather than deleted, so the two files cannot disagree. **The legacy passwords were then checked against their hashes, and two are wrong:** `export_manager`/`em123` and `document_team`/`dt123` both fail, which means `export_manager` had no working login either and the count of uncovered roles was really 11, not 10 — `t_export_manager` was added and the whole set re-verified (74 users, 15 of 15 roles with a confirmed login). `wc123`, `tr123`, `sr123` and `admin123` do authenticate. Verification went through `check_password`, never the login endpoint, because `django-axes` locks by username + IP and a wrong guess would have locked a real account. **Known gap:** `t_greenhouse_manager` owns no `GreenhouseBlock`, so the weekly plan and block screens render empty for it — assigning a block would mean editing live reference data.
- **Sheet R27 picks a driver from the registry, mirroring R23's truck picker (feat(frontend)).** `input_type` stays `'text'`; `SheetCellEditor` special-cases `field_key === 'driver_name'` and renders `SheetDriverSelectEditor` for a **non-gapy** shipment — one searchable select over the 152 imported drivers (**active-only**: the admin tab passes `include_inactive=true`, the picker must not), inline `+ Add`, portaled to `document.body` past the cell's `contain: layout paint`. Commit sends **`driver_id` + `driver_name` in a single `patchMultiMutation`** with undo capture, the same shape `saveTruck` uses for head/trailer/plate. **`driver_phone` (R28) is never written from it** — separate cell, separate comment thread and history, 80 operator-typed values, and a registry with no phone for anyone, so the only possible effect would be blanking them. **Gapy-Satys keeps the plain text input**, extending the truck cell's HARD RULE: a local buyer's own truck arrives with the buyer's own driver, and inline-adding those would bury a 152-row company registry. Inline-added names are upper-cased to match the registry's own casing. `IDriver` moved from `useFleetAdmin.ts` to `useFleet.ts`, joined by `useDrivers()`/`useCreateDriver()`.
- **Removed the dead `driver_id` → coordinator-list mapping (fix(frontend)).** `TaskCardEditor.helpers.ts` bound `driver_id` to `optionsSource: 'transportUsers'`, which resolves to `optionsByCategory('transport_responsible')` — the 6-row transport-coordinator list, correct for `vehicle_responsible` and copy-pasted onto `driver_id`. Selecting there wrote an id of 1–6 into a column meaning `Z_TIRWEB.drivers.id`, now populated with ids 5–158, so 5 and 6 were ambiguous. Dormant while the column was NULL everywhere — the Sheet picker above would have made it live. It turned out to be **unreachable**: `FIELD_MAP` is keyed off `TaskRule.target_fields` and no rule targets `driver_id` (all target `driver_name,driver_phone,truck_plate`), and its `labelKey` `shipment_edit_drawer.field.driver` was absent from tk/ru/en. Deleted rather than rewired.
- **Drivers tab on Fleet Admin — the driver registry is now manageable from the UI (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** The 152 rows imported from `Z_TIRWEB` had no API and no screen. Backend adds `DriverSerializer` + `DriverViewSet` + the `drivers` route, mirroring `TrailerViewSet`: `GET/POST /api/v1/transport/drivers/` and `PATCH /api/v1/transport/drivers/{id}/`, unpaginated, `?search=` over `name`/`phone`, list is `IsAuthenticated` and active-only unless `?include_inactive=true`, create/update gated to `CanEditShipment`. **`destroy` is deliberately absent** — `Shipment.driver_id` is a loose `BigIntegerField` with no FK to raise on a dangling reference, so rows are deactivated rather than deleted — and `id` is read-only because it is the `Z_TIRWEB.drivers.id` that shipments point at. Frontend adds `FleetDriversTab.tsx` as its own component rather than a third block inside `FleetAdminPage` (already 380 lines against a 150-line-per-component rule): a `ProTable` with 50-per-page pagination, a client-side name/phone filter in the toolbar (the full list is already in memory, so a keystroke costs no round trip), active-first column sorting, and a create/edit modal. Truck/trailer tabs keep their unpaginated basic `Table` — 91 and 74 rows don't need either. A blank phone posts as `null`, never `''`, matching what the import means by "no phone known". The modal's OK/Cancel are driven from our own i18n because Ant Design ships only `ru_RU`/`en_US` here and `App.tsx` falls back to `en_US` for Turkmen — the two older tabs still render English footer buttons in a `tk` session. 12 new `fleet_admin.*` keys in tk/ru/en. **`boss` was also added to all three Fleet Admin gates.** `/admin/fleet` has no `page_code` — it is gated by an inline `roles` array in `App.tsx` and `AppLayout.tsx` mirroring the backend `SHIPMENT_EDITOR_ROLES`, a layer the permission matrix never consults, and `boss` was in none of them. The route sat in `BOSS_MENU_GROUPS` yet the link never rendered for a real boss account, and `CanEditShipment` would have 403'd every write. Same shape as the weekly-plan and `/join` widenings. `SHIPMENT_EDITOR_ROLES` gates **only** `apps/transport/views.py` (GPS device-link override + TruckHead/Trailer/Driver CRUD), so the widening reaches nothing further. `AppLayout.menuGroups.test.tsx` was structurally unable to catch this — its `fakeUser` fixture sets `is_superuser: true`, taking the role filter's bypass branch — so the new guard renders a **non-superuser** boss and asserts `/admin/fleet` is present (confirmed red without the fix). **Still open:** the shipment-level `driver_id` dropdown continues to offer the 6 `transport_responsible` coordinators rather than these drivers.
- **Driver registry loaded from `Z_TIRWEB` — 152 drivers, ids 5–158 (feat(p3) + data(p3)).** `transport.Driver` had existed since the fleet-map slice but held **zero rows** and was wired to nothing; the one-time `import_tir_fleet` pipeline read only `truck_heads` and `trailers`. Added `TirClient.get_drivers()` (`SELECT id, full_name, phone, is_active FROM drivers`) and `_import_drivers()`, upserting by preserved source `id` exactly as `TruckHead`/`Trailer` do. **No schema change was needed**: `Shipment.driver_id` is already a raw `BigIntegerField` pointing into `Z_TIRWEB`'s id space (alongside `truck_head_id`/`trailer_id`/`trip_id`, kept loose so `export ↛ transport` holds), so preserving ids is the whole contract. Following the TruckHead/Trailer convention, `name` is the **only** field in the upsert `defaults`: `phone` is excluded because Z_TIRWEB holds none for any driver and never will, so including it would give a re-run exactly one possible effect — nulling whatever an operator typed; `is_active` is excluded so a manual deactivate survives a re-run. `import_fleet()` was split into `_import_truck_heads` / `_import_trailers` / `_import_drivers` to stay inside the 20-line-per-function rule. **Deliberately not done:** no backfill of `Shipment.driver_id` (NULL on all 146 shipments) and no change to the operator-entered `driver_name` (85/146) / `driver_phone` (80/146) — name matching against the source reached only 30 of 82 distinct names (transliteration variants such as `Abayev`/`Abayew` for one person, typos, two drivers in one cell, and junk test rows), and the truck-plate route via `trips` reached only 29 of 80 head plates, so any backfill would have left a mixed-truth field. **Open defect recorded, not fixed:** `TaskCardEditor.helpers.ts` binds `driver_id` to `optionsSource: 'transportUsers'`, which does not resolve to platform users but to `optionsByCategory('transport_responsible')` — the seeded 6-row transport-coordinator list, correct for `vehicle_responsible` and copy-pasted onto `driver_id`. Selecting there writes an id of 1–6, now ambiguous with real `Driver` ids 5 and 6; harmless only because the field is NULL everywhere. Tests: `test_tir_import` 4/4 (driver mock mirrors production's empty phones).
- **Pomidor Dükany page — planned vs achieved production per greenhouse block (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** New route `/export/pomidor-dukany` porting the analysis from `Pomidor Dükany 2025-2026.xlsx` and sera-butce-web. **Achieved = `ShipmentBlockSource` + `DomesticSale`, not `HarvestDayEntry.actual_value`**: `plan_value` is a harvest plan, but the nightly `rollup_actuals` sums exports only and never reads `DomesticSale`; the rollup value and an export sum are also the same rows; and the rollup is an unscheduled cron that had filled 26 of 3,519 cells against 2.3M kg of shipment data, so blocks would have read 0%. `actual_value` is kept as a muted `rollup_kg` diagnostic with a `rollup_days` count so "never computed" is distinguishable from "computed as zero". Per block: Meýilleşdirilen / Ýerine ýetirilen / variance / achievement % (colour-banded ≥90/≥60), Meýdan m² with plan and actual kg/m², and the Içerki vs Daşarky split, plus grand totals and an overall-achievement KPI. Period modes Hepdelik | Aýlyk | Möwsümleýin × granularity Döwür | Jemleýji (cumulative to a chosen day), with a block multi-select — all four modes resolve to one `date_from`/`date_to` query via the pure, tested `resolveRange()`, keeping calendar logic out of the service. Backend: `services/pomidor_dukany.py` + `GET /api/v1/export/production-analysis/`, three grouped aggregates, top-level blocks only (sub-blocks would double-count area and weight), soft-deleted shipments excluded but archived ones kept, 800-day range guard. `GreenhouseBlock.area_m2` was already populated but read by nothing outside the block admin page — kg/m² is its first analytical use. New page code `export.pomidor_dukany`; admin/director/export_manager/boss inherit it from `_ALL_PAGES`, so a plain `seed_permissions` run suffices with no migration. 22 i18n keys in tk/ru/en. Not built: year-over-year comparison, and per-block row totals on the weekly plan grid itself.
- **"Join drafts" button in the Shipment List bulk-action bar merges two selected drafts (auto-detected supply→destination) via the existing join endpoint (feat(p3) + feat(frontend), Phase B).** Tick exactly two draft rows; a "Join drafts" button appears (visible to `export_manager`/`director`/`boss` and absent when the season is read-only). Opening the modal auto-detects which draft is the destination (kept) vs the supply (deleted) and shows a preview; confirming merges via the existing `POST /export/shipments/{target}/join/` endpoint. Ambiguous pairs (cannot tell which is the destination) show an error instead. New `join_drafts.*` i18n block (9 keys) added in tk/ru/en.
- **"Join supply" button on a destination draft's Detail page — opens a modal listing supply drafts and merges the chosen one via the existing join endpoint (feat(p3) + feat(frontend), Phase A).** A "Join supply" button in the Detail hero's action cluster (next to Promote/Transition/Cancel) on any destination-draft Detail page, opening the new `JoinSupplyModal` (visible to `export_manager`/`director`/`boss` and absent on non-drafts, drafts with existing blocks, or supply drafts themselves). Lists all draft supply candidates with their block count and total weight; clicking one confirms a merge via the existing `POST /export/shipments/{target}/join/` endpoint (target = destination, source = selected supply). Page reloads and the button disappears since the shipment now has blocks. Backend rejection surfaces its error message. New `join_supply.*` i18n block (9 keys) added in tk/ru/en.
- **"Create supply draft" button + modal on the Shipment List — a supply draft can now be created outside the Sheet, with variety captured at creation (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** New `SupplyDraftModal` (`frontend/src/components/shipment/SupplyDraftModal.tsx`), opened from a button next to "New Shipment" (same `canCreate` gate). Collects total weight (`weight_net`), a multi-select of blocks with no per-block weight (`block_ids`), variety, harvest status, export code and notes; posts to `POST /export/shipments/` with `is_draft: true, skip_forecast_check: true`. `ShipmentCreateSerializer` gained `weight_net`, `block_ids` (deduped — a repeat returns a clean 400) and `harvest_status`. Such a draft has `block_sources` immediately (weights null), so it is joinable right away via the existing Sheet Join — no per-block weights required first; those are filled in later by the pallet-manifest close or a future Detail editor.
- **Generated documents now print database-filled values in red, and the office can retune page layout without a developer (feat(p4) + feat(frontend)).** Two features ported from `data/sera-butce-web`, whose two documents the office rated above ours. Comparing them first showed the documents themselves are at parity — our `contract_kz.docx` carries the same 8 bilingual sections plus a 9th, and our invoice already emits the Упаковочный лист as page 2 — so what was copied is operator experience, not content.
  **(1) Red highlighting.** Every value from the DB renders `C00000`, boilerplate stays black, so a blank field is obvious before printing. On by default; `?highlight=0` (a checkbox in every download modal) gives an all-black copy for the customer or a customs file. **docxtpl's `RichText` was tried and rejected**: it needs the `{{r tag }}` prefix syntax (rewriting every tag in all 8 templates), and because `{{r }}` replaces the whole run it discards the template's bold/size/font, which would push presentation into the currently-pure context builders. Worse, with plain `{{ }}` it emits **invalid OOXML** — `<w:r>` nested inside `<w:t>` — that python-docx parses without complaint, so the existing `assertNotIn('{{', text)` smoke test would have shipped a Word-corrupt file silently. Instead `document_highlight.wrap_context` sentinel-wraps each non-blank value (`U+E000`/`U+E001`) before the render and `colorize` splits the runs afterwards, each new run a deep copy of the original `<w:r>` inserted via `addnext`. **Zero template files and zero context builders changed**, and every run property survives — which is what makes it safe on `cmr_*_docx`, whose run properties position each line on the pre-printed form. Blank values are deliberately left unwrapped (a wrapped empty string would emit a stray red run and would break any future `{% if %}`). The xlsx CMR overlay colours cells directly — every cell it writes is a filled value.
  **(2) Page-layout adjustments.** New `DocumentLayoutSetting` (one row per document type, shared by all users) holding `font_scale_pct` 80–120, `line_spacing` 1.00–2.00, and four `margin_*_delta_mm` −10…+15, applied after rendering. **Deltas and a percentage, never absolutes**, for two measured reasons: `contract_kz.docx` has two sections with deliberately different top margins (0.51cm page 1 for the letterhead, 2.5cm after) that a single absolute knob would flatten; and an absolute base font size is nearly a no-op because most runs carry an explicit `<w:sz>` overriding the Normal style (713 of 879 runs in `contract_kz`, 70 of 83 in `invoice_ru`) — only a run-by-run scale moves the text, and it preserves each template's size hierarchy. Scaled sizes snap to the nearest half-point (Word's `<w:sz>` grid); truncating would drop up to 0.5pt per run, visible at these 8pt sizes. A left/right delta also rescales every `autofit=False` table **by the table's own current total**, since `_col_widths` pins them to exactly the 17.5cm text area — but `contract_kz`'s hand-authored tables legitimately start *wider* than their section (18.89cm vs 16.68cm), so scaling by ratio rather than fitting to the page avoids silently redesigning a legal document. **Margins are consequently a weak lever on the contract** (its body table already renders past the margin); font scale and line spacing are the real ones, and the popover says so inline. **The four CMR keys are excluded** from layout entirely (`registry.supports_layout()`, plus a 400 from the API) — their geometry registers onto pre-printed paper. `GET /contracts/document-layouts/` synthesises defaults for untouched keys; `PATCH /contracts/document-layouts/{key}/` upserts with ADR-0006 version conflict → 409, gated by a **scope-derived** resource code (`contract_kz` → `contract`, everything else → `sale`) because generation itself is split across those two codes — one fixed code would let someone retune a document they cannot generate. The lookup is **deliberately uncached**: one indexed row against a 10–30s LibreOffice render buys nothing, and a staleness window would sit inside the exact loop the feature exists for.
  **Frontend:** `CmrDocumentsButton` / `PacketZipButton` / `InvoiceDocumentsButton` were ~90% identical, so the shared `DocumentOptionsModal` + `useDocumentDownload` were extracted first — otherwise the checkbox and the layout gear would each have landed three times. `applyDocumentOptions()` sends **only the opt-out** (red is the server default), so every pre-existing URL renders exactly as before. New `DocumentLayoutPopover` + `useDocumentLayouts` follow `sheet/SheetRowStyleControls`' drag-mirror pattern (mirror locally on drag, save once on release) and open a `Modal.confirm` on 409. 16 `document_layout.*` + 2 `documents.*` i18n keys in tk/ru/en.
  **UX change worth noting:** the CT-1 / FITO / customs letters used to download on one click and now open the options modal, because those are exactly the copies that go to the customs and phytosanitary authorities and most need the clean-copy toggle.
  **Tests:** +29 backend (contracts 184 → 213) and +8 frontend (302 → 310). The pre-existing suite **could not** have caught either feature failing — `p.text`/`cell.text` discard all run properties and the `assertNotIn('{{')` check passes on corrupt OOXML — so the new tests assert at the XML level (no nested `<w:r>` and no sentinel survivors in **any** zip part, not just `word/document.xml`) and check colour in both directions, since over-colouring is as wrong as under-colouring. The three render test classes moved from `SimpleTestCase` to `TestCase`, as rendering now reads the layout row. Also fixed a pre-existing typo in `test_seller_director_tk_falls_back_to_ru_when_blank` (asserted on the mock `c[...]` instead of the result `out[...]`, from `d7bc0ab`) which made that test permanently red.

### Changed
- **Weekly Plan role predicates extracted to `WeeklyPlanGrid.roles.ts` and covered by tests (refactor(frontend) + test(frontend)).** Five inline predicates in a 913-line component were untestable without a render harness, so the Aug 2026 boss widening changed four of them with no safety net. Moved verbatim into the pure `planGridCapabilities({ role, isReadOnly })`; no behaviour change. `hasBlockPermission` deliberately stayed in the component — it keys on `managed_block_ids`, not on role. 12 new tests pin the two asymmetries a reader trips on (`director` edits the harvest grid but not an actual; a closed season beats every role including admin) and that `canEditActual` currently narrows to `admin` alone. Frontend suite 397/397, `tsc` clean.
- **Draft Shipments (`/export/drafts`) and the Assignment Board (`/export/assign`) removed from the boss sidebar (refactor(frontend)).** Both were in `BOSS_MENU_GROUPS`' `nav.group_prep` since `421068f`'s "surface orphaned routes" sweep; the owner asked for them gone. That group now holds `/export/weightmaster` alone. `STAFF_MENU_GROUPS` is unchanged — both pages remain for `export_manager` / `loading_dept_head`. Sidebar-only: the routes still resolve, and the boss's page permissions and the `POST /shipments/{id}/assign/` grant are untouched, since the planned process page mounts both screens as tabs. `AppLayout.menuGroups.test.tsx` now asserts boss 46 keys / staff 48 with the difference pinned to exactly those two, so a future orphaned-route sweep fails the test instead of silently re-adding them.
- **The join endpoint now allows `boss` and superusers to join drafts (feat(p3), Phase B).** `POST /export/shipments/{id}/join/` was gated to `apps.core.roles.PRIVILEGED_ROLES` (`admin`/`export_manager`/`director`) only, so the Sheet/Detail/List Join actions 403'd for `boss` despite the UI showing the button. Widened at the call site — same style as `/assign` (boss) and `/cancel` (superuser bypass); the frontend gates now mirror `{admin, export_manager, director, boss}` + superuser.
- **`ShipmentBlockSource.weight_kg` is now nullable (migration `0062`) — blocks can be recorded before they are weighed (feat(p3)).** Supports the new supply-draft-creation path above.
- **Join preserves a supply draft's declared total weight when its blocks are unweighed (fix(p3)).** `_execute_join` previously recomputed `target.weight_net` from `Sum(block_sources.weight_kg)`, which nulls/understates the total when the source's blocks haven't been weighed yet. Now captures the source's `weight_net` before it's deleted and uses it as a fallback when any moved block has `weight_kg IS NULL`.


### Fixed
- **Weekly plan: `boss`'s cell shows one value — the plan (fix(frontend)).** A manual `actual` is an override, not an entry: it stamps `actual_source='admin_override'` and `rollup_actuals` then skips that block-day permanently, so leaving the auto-computed actual in a plan-entry cell was both clutter and a trap. New `planOnly` prop on `HarvestCell` (passed for `role === 'boss'` only) renders the plan alone on every day via a single branch above the `past_actual`/`today_actual`/`future_plan` mode switch; `canEditActualForEntry` returns `false` for the same role so the capability isn't live without a UI path. Boss keeps the reason prompt when overwriting a filled plan, and retains backend override authority via `is_admin_like` — it just has no button on this screen. `admin`/`director` rendering is unchanged and pinned by a control test.
- **Weekly plan: cells stayed uneditable right after "Initialize Week" until a page reload (fix(frontend)).** `initialize_harvest_week` creates the seven Mon–Sun `HarvestDayEntry` rows along with the plans — those rows are the grid's editable cells — but `useInitializeWeek`'s `onSuccess` invalidated only `['harvest-plans']`. `['day-entries']` kept its stale empty cache, so `entriesByBlockDay` was empty and every cell rendered as a dead `—` span with no click handler; `showInitialize` (which compares `dayEntries.length` to the expected count) also kept the button on screen. Now invalidates `['day-entries']` too. Affects `admin` and `director` identically — surfaced when `boss` gained access to the Initialize button. Regression test in `usePlanning.invalidation.test.tsx`.
- **`boss` can now write the weekly harvest plan — every write on `/export/plan` returned 403 for him (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** His permission matrix already granted `['*']` on every resource; the denials came from hardcoded `role == 'admin'` compares in `apps/greenhouse/views.py` and `harvest_day_service.py`, a layer the matrix never consults. New `ADMIN_LIKE = {admin, boss}` + `is_admin_like(user)` (honours `is_superuser`) in `apps/core/roles.py`; `_check_plan_permission`, `initialize_week`, the four grant/revoke late-edit actions and `set_plan_value` / `set_forecast_value` / `set_actual_value` / `admin_override` all route through it. `HARVEST_DAY_WRITE` gained `boss` (gates `POST /export/harvest-forecast/`) and `POST /export/tasks/generate-weekly-plan/` was widened at the call site to `PRIVILEGED_ROLES | {'boss'}`. Boss inherits admin's override contract (reason required over a filled cell, `last_override_*` stamped), so `WeeklyPlanGrid.tsx`'s override-reason prompt widened in lockstep via a new `isAdminLike` flag — `isAdmin` (admin|director) stays as-is because it feeds `canEditTrucks`, whose `TRUCK_WRITE` gate has no boss. User management and the permission matrix stay admin-only (AD-15); the remaining un-widened `role == 'admin'` sites elsewhere are a separate sweep.
- **`tests_shipment_join` was red: permissions were never seeded (fix(p3)).** ~28 of the file's 34 tests 403'd at the DRF resource-permission matrix, because every non-superuser POST hit it with no permissions ever seeded for the test roles. Fixed by adding `call_command('seed_permissions')` to `setUpTestData` in all 9 `TestCase` classes in `backend/apps/export/tests_shipment_join.py`; no join logic changed. `tests_shipment_join.py` is now 34/34 green.
- **The join endpoint's 403 error message now names the `boss` role (fix(p3)).** `POST /export/shipments/{id}/join/` was gated to `{export_manager, director}` in its docstring and the serializer message. Error message updated to `{export_manager, director, boss}` to align with the intended gate wording (boss was not yet actually admitted by the gate at this point — see the Phase B entry above).
- **Production rebuild failed — `celery-worker`/`celery-beat` still built from a `./backend` context (fix(docker)).** `0a09304` widened the *backend* service's build context to the repo root (`context: .` + `dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile`) so `docs/how_works/` could be baked in, and rewrote every `COPY` in `backend/Dockerfile` to be repo-relative. The two celery services — added earlier in `9059e38` — kept `context: ./backend` + `dockerfile: Dockerfile`, so from their narrower context `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/` and `docs/how_works` do not exist: `failed to compute cache key: "/docs/how_works": not found`. Because Compose builds all four images in one bake, **the whole bake aborted — `backend` and `frontend` never finished either**, so `update.sh` failed outright rather than degrading to "fleet map stops polling". Both celery services now use the same repo-root context as `backend`. `docker-compose.prod.yml` declares no `build:` block for them, so the base-file fix propagates to production. Not refactored to a shared `image:` — three tags over one BuildKit-cached layer stack costs nothing, and cross-service build ordering is a bigger change than the bug warrants.
- **Boss Dashboard margin/Route P&L reported ~100% margin on every route (fix(p3)).** `_aggregate_summary` and `_aggregate_route_pnl` summed only the legacy `SalesReport.transport_cost_usd / market_fee_usd / other_expenses_usd` columns — which the rich report path never writes — so `cost = 0` and `margin = revenue` for every report filed through the UI, while `total_expenses_local` on the same row held the real figure. New `report_cost_usd()` expression resolves per row: `total_expenses_local / exchange_rate` when the Kurs is set, else the legacy USD columns (historical rows). Rich reports saved without a Kurs still contribute 0 — converting them would mean inventing a rate. +4 tests.
- **A rejected sales-report save left a phantom report that could auto-complete the shipment (fix(p3)).** `set_sales_report` ran `get_or_create` *before* validation with no enclosing transaction (`ATOMIC_REQUESTS` is off), so any 400/500 committed an empty `SalesReport`. Bare row existence already satisfies the `satyldy → tamamlandy` trigger (`target_fields='sales_report'`, `ALL_FIELDS_FILLED`), so the next unrelated `Shipment.save()` could complete the truck with no financial data — and the row also hid the shipment from `overdue`, `my-sales-reports?needs_report=true` and the compliance backlog. Now `get_or_create` + validate + save share one `transaction.atomic()`. Does not clean up rows already committed by past failures.
- **Malformed nested sales-report rows 500'd instead of 400'ing (fix(p3)).** `partial=True` on the parent propagates to nested children via DRF's `root.partial`, so `required` never fired and a row missing `quantity_kg`/`price_local`/`amount_local` reached `bulk_create` as a NOT NULL violation. Both child serializers now check presence explicitly in `validate()`. Unreachable from the UI (the payload builder filters incomplete rows) — this closes the direct-API surface. +2 tests.
- **Sales report was editable on a soft-deleted/archived shipment (fix(p3)).** Detail actions bypass the soft-delete filter in `get_queryset()`; `set_sales_report` now re-checks `deleted_at`/`is_archived` and returns 403, matching `set_column_color`. +1 test.
- **SalesReportPage silently deleted expense rows on save (fix(frontend)).** The save is replace-all, and rows were seeded as one-row-per-*active*-category keyed by category PK — so (a) a second row of the same category (per-city `NAKLIYE`, distinguished by `label_raw`) collapsed to the last one and (b) any row whose category had since been deactivated was never seeded at all. Both were deleted on the next save. Seeding now emits every saved row (duplicates kept, grouped under its category in template order) plus a blank row per unused active category, with deactivated-category rows appended so they round-trip. Two identical-looking rows can now render for a duplicated category — preserving the data beats deleting it; a proper per-row editor is follow-up.
- **SalesReportPage's Save was live before the expense-category template loaded (fix(frontend)).** Rows seed only once categories arrive, so a Save clicked in that window posted `line_items: []` / `expenses: []` and wiped the report. The page now holds on the categories + countries queries and blocks with an error panel if the category fetch fails.
- **Sales-report totals drifted by cents from the stored server totals (fix(frontend)).** The server quantizes each line to 0.01 then sums; the UI summed raw `qty × price`. New shared `sumLineAmounts()` / `roundMoney()` in `salesReportUtils` mirror the server's order of operations across `SalesReportPage`, `SaleTab` and `SalesReportPanel`. The UI still recomputes rather than rendering `total_sales_local` — that swap is follow-up.
- **A `0` expense saved from ShipmentDetail was deleted by the next SalesReportPage save (fix(frontend)).** The page filtered `amount_local > 0` while `SalesReportPanel` had no such guard. Now filters on `!== null` only, so an explicit zero round-trips; untouched template rows (null) are still skipped.
- **Sales-rep worklist and Overdue list went stale after saving a report (fix(frontend)).** `useSaveSalesReport` invalidated only the shipment-detail key. Now also invalidates `['shipments','my-sales-reports']` and `['shipments','overdue']` by prefix.
- **Fleet truck PATCH no longer re-matches (and could wipe) the GPS device on an unrelated field edit (fix(transport)).** `TruckHeadSerializer.update()` re-ran `device_for_plate()` whenever `plate_number` was *present* in the PATCH body, not when it *changed* — so the new admin Edit modal (which always submits the plate) would re-match on a capacity/owner/status edit and, if `device_for_plate()` returned `None`, silently clear a working `traccar_device` link. Now guarded on `new_plate != instance.plate_number`; an unchanged plate is left untouched, changed-plate matching preserved. +2 tests; `apps.transport` suite green (71).
- **Shipment↔truck GPS link: map viewport stuck on device switch + resolver false-match risks (fix(transport)).** `ShipmentTruckLocationCard`'s `MapContainer` only reads `center`/`zoom` at mount (react-leaflet v4) — overriding a shipment to a different device moved the pin but left the viewport on the old truck's location. Added a `Recenter` child using `useMap()` that pans the live map on every position/device change. `resolve_device_for_shipment()` also now (1) rejects any shipment plate containing a Cyrillic letter before normalizing — `normalize_plate()` strips Cyrillic, which could shrink a homoglyph plate into a token that collides with an unrelated Latin `Truck.plate` — and (2) matches only `Truck.objects.filter(is_active=True)`, so a retired truck's GPS can no longer surface. 3 new backend tests, all `apps.transport` tests green.
### Changed
- **The Boss Dashboard wrote the greenhouse block list three times; it is now one table (feat(frontend)).** `ProductionResults.tsx` rendered the 15 blocks **twice** — one table for `scope=daily`, one for `scope=seasonal`, stacked inside a single card — and `ExportMarketByBlock.tsx` rendered them a third time below. 45 block labels where 15 do, and comparing a block's harvest against its export meant holding a row in your head across two scrolls. Both files are **deleted** and replaced by `BlocksTable.tsx` (card + orchestration), `BlocksTableRows.tsx` (the four row-level components), `BlocksTableCells.tsx` (the `CellGroup` / `Num` / `SeasonBar` primitives) and `BlocksTable.helpers.ts` (`mergeBlockRows`, `sumTotals`, the shared grid template and style constants) — one row per block with Günlük / Aýlyk / Möwsümleýin plan-vs-actual and Daşarky Bazar kg + share as column groups to the right. **No backend change and no extra requests**: the page already issued all three calls, and all three aggregators build their rows from the identical `GreenhouseBlock.objects.all().order_by('code')`, so the merge is a client-side 1:1 join on `block_code`. It still left-joins on the daily response and zero-fills a block missing from the others — the shared row set is a property of today's backend, not a contract the frontend can enforce, and a visible zero beats an `undefined` that takes the card down. **The four groups do not share a time window and two of them ignore the period switcher** — Günlük is always `date.today()` and Aýlyk always the current calendar month, both fixed inside `_aggregate_production`, while Möwsümleýin and the export columns honour the selected period. That was already true; stacked tables hid it, one row would have made it misleading, so the group headers are load-bearing rather than decorative. `monthly_*` is read from the daily response only, since the backend derives it from `today` regardless of `scope`. **Both drill-downs survive** by making the *cell group* the click target rather than the row: harvest cells open `/export/plan?block=`, Daşarky Bazar cells open `/export/shipments?block_source=`, with hover highlighting only the group under the cursor — a row-level `onClick` (what both deleted files did) could only have kept one. i18n: the group headers reuse `production.scope_daily`/`scope_monthly`/`scope_seasonal`, three keys that were defined in all three languages and **consumed by nothing**; 6 new `blocks_table.*` keys added, 10 now-dead keys removed from tk/ru/en. The `Içerki Bazar / Sowgatlyk` footnote and the CRITICAL comment forbidding domestic/gift columns moved across verbatim. The heatmap tiles are untouched — a different instrument, elsewhere on the page. 14 new frontend tests (9 helpers + 5 render); the export-click test was verified RED against a single row-level handler. Suite 247 → 261, `tsc` clean. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-boss-blocks-table-merge-design.md`.
- **The debt-aging card is removed from the Boss Dashboard (feat(frontend)).** `DebtBreakdown.tsx` deleted along with its `useBossDebt` hook and the `IBossDebt*` types; `RevenueChart` now takes the full row where the 2-column grid used to be. The dead i18n keys `boss_dashboard.section.debt` and the four `boss_dashboard.aging.*` buckets are dropped from all three languages — `boss_dashboard.kpi.debt` stays, since the hero KPI strip still shows a debt tile. **Backend untouched:** `GET /api/v1/export/boss/debt/` and its tests remain, so the card can be re-mounted when P4 Contracts supplies real aging data. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **The invoice NET now drives the quota, and quota usage is shown per truck instead of a date × firm matrix (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** `ContractSale.quantity_kg` and `ShipmentFirmSplit.weight_kg` are the same number by design — the firm's official export weight (AD-016) — but were kept in step in only one direction: applying a `PackingTemplate` wrote the share net down onto the split, while editing `quantity_kg` on the sale did not, so the invoice and the quota ledger silently disagreed. Two of the 18 linked sales on the dev database had drifted (`0807003/26`: split 7,000 / 11,000 against invoice 9,000 / 9,000 — identical truck total, different per-firm split, and quota is counted **per firm**). `ContractSaleViewSet.perform_create/perform_update` now call the new `sync_split_weight_from_sale()`, which rewrites that one firm's split (others keep their weights), re-runs quota and busts the FIFO/balance caches. A firm with no split on the truck is skipped rather than added — putting a firm on a truck is a separate decision with its own no-remaining-quota hard block. The direction is forced by the module graph: `export` may not import `contracts`, so quota cannot read the sale and the helper lives on the contracts side. New `sync_split_weights_from_sales` management command (`--dry-run`) repairs rows that drifted before this existed. Coverage caveat: only 9 of 90 shipments with firm splits have a sale carrying `quantity_kg`, so the split stays the only source for the rest. **On the frontend the date × firm matrix is deleted** (`QuotaUsageGrid`, its helpers and `QuotaUsageCellDetail`) and replaced by `QuotaUsageByShipment`: one row per truck — code, date, firm count, total — expanding into the per-firm rows with inline-editable kg. Quota is consumed per truck, so the truck is what an operator reconciles against; the matrix could not show which shipment a number came from at all. Records with no shipment (575 of 711) collapse into one bucket pinned last rather than being dropped. Manual entry moved with the matrix: typing into an empty cell used to POST a row, so the new `QuotaUsageCreateModal` is reached from an **Add manual row** button in the flat list. 7 new backend tests + 11 frontend; zero regressions.
- **Quota usage counts automatically — the draft→approve step is removed (feat(p3) + feat(frontend), AD-16 amended).** Usage rows are created `status='approved'` by both `QuotaUsageViewSet.perform_create` and `quota_sync.sync_draft_quota_usage_for_shipment`, so exported kg reaches FIFO, the firm balances and the dashboard the instant a truck has firm splits. `POST /quota-usage/approve/` is **deleted** (405); `perform_update`/`perform_destroy` no longer gate on `status`, so `400 Only draft records can be edited/deleted` is gone; the bulk-approve buttons, status filter, status column, Approved-By column and row-selection checkboxes are removed from both usage views, along with 9 now-dead i18n keys per language. `approved_by`/`approved_at` stay **NULL** — `'approved'` now means "counted", not "signed", and stamping the acting operator would be a false signature in the audit trail; the column survives only to carry pre-cutover history. **Not the one-line flip it looks like:** `sync_draft_quota_usage_for_shipment` raised `ApprovedQuotaExistsError` (→400) whenever approved rows existed, a guard whose entire premise was that approved meant a human signature. Once every row is born approved that fires on *every* firm-split edit after the first — it would have 400'd routine work platform-wide. Three call sites carried it (`ShipmentViewSet.set_firm_splits` plus two in `contracts.views`); all three removed, and resync now replaces every row on the shipment rather than defending it. Manually-entered rows carry no shipment and are never in that queryset. Every usage write now busts the FIFO/balance caches, which previously only `/approve/` did. New `approve_legacy_quota_usage` management command (with `--dry-run`) converts the pre-cutover drafts. **Run on dev 2026-08-11: 149 rows / 1,714,452 kg** (the other 2 rows / 18,000 kg had already been regenerated as approved by `sync_split_weights_from_sales`); approved total 19,915,100 -> 21,647,552 kg, drafts 0, row count unchanged at 711, and **no firm's `remaining_kg` moved** — `compute_firm_quota_balances` never filtered on status. **Beta needs the same command after deploy.** Two consequences left standing and documented rather than changed: `POST /shipments/{id}/cancel/` still reports `draft_quota_deleted` (now structurally 0) and `approved_quota_to_reconcile` (now every row) — no kg is stranded because `counted()` already drops rows tied to a cancelled shipment; and **whether `is_gapy_satys` shipments should consume quota at all is an open question referred to the business** (3 shipments / 36,000 kg, all to Kyrgyzstan) — until answered they consume it exactly as before. 9 new backend tests + 7 rewritten; frontend 228/228; zero regressions against a HEAD worktree baseline.

### Added
- **API idempotency — a retried `POST` create no longer produces a duplicate (feat(core) + feat(p3) + feat(p4) + feat(frontend)).** The API had no idempotency mechanism at all: a `grep` for `Idempot` returned only prose and management-command descriptions. The frontend already blocks the trivial double-click (`disabled`/`loading={isPending}` in 80 places across 54 files), so the live failure was the **retry after timeout** — an operator on a public network in KZ/RU presses Save, the request reaches the server and succeeds, the response is lost, and Save is pressed again 30–60 s later (`services/api.ts` sets no axios `timeout`, so that window is wide). Six endpoints now accept an optional `Idempotency-Key` header (`[A-Za-z0-9\-]{8,64}`, else 400): `POST /export/shipments/`, both comment paths (`/export/comments/` and the legacy `/export/shipments/{id}/comment/`), `/export/advances/`, `/export/customs-expenses/` and `/contracts/contracts/`. New `core.IdempotencyKey` model (migration `0034`) with `UniqueConstraint(user, endpoint, key)` and a `Cyrillic_General_CI_AS` `response_body` (comment bodies carry Turkmen/Russian); new `@idempotent` decorator in `apps/core/idempotency.py` — a decorator, not a viewset mixin, because two targets are `@action` methods a `create()`-wrapping mixin never reaches. **The INSERT happens before the view runs**, inside its own `transaction.atomic()`: that is the mechanism, since a `filter().exists()` check lets two concurrent retries both through. `endpoint` is `request.path` rather than a hand-written scope string, so detail actions are discriminated by their pk for free. Outcomes fork three ways — 2xx is stored and replayed **verbatim as the original 201, not a 409** (the axios interceptor special-cases 409 only for `season_closed`, so a 409 would show "failed" on a request that succeeded); a validation 400/403 **frees** the key so a corrected resubmit works; a 5xx or unhandled exception **keeps** it, because `ATOMIC_REQUESTS` is off and these views write across several models before they can fail (`ShipmentViewSet.create` → `_create_draft_shipment()`/`create_shipment()`; advances writes the row then its links), so freeing it would let a blind retry re-run a half-finished create. An in-flight winner returns `409 {"error": "idempotency_in_progress"}`, handled by a new interceptor branch + i18n key in all three languages. **Storage is a table, not Django's cache** — `settings.py` falls back to `LocMemCache` when `RUNNING_TESTS or (DEBUG and not REDIS_URL)` and beta runs `DEBUG=True`, so a cache-backed store would silently no-op in every test and on the one server where this is exercised. Keys expire after 24 h via a new `purge-expired-idempotency-keys` Celery beat task. Frontend: `useIdempotencyKey()` holds the key in a `useRef` so it survives re-renders and is regenerated only on success — **generating it in the axios request interceptor would dedupe nothing**, since every attempt would mint a new UUID. `crypto.randomUUID()` is secure-context only and beta serves plain HTTP at `10.10.11.25:8080` where it is `undefined`, so `newKey()` falls back to `crypto.getRandomValues()`; without that the feature would be dead precisely where it gets tested. **One `useIdempotencyKey()` per mutation, never shared** — `useDrafts.ts` posts to `/export/shipments/` from four distinct mutations sharing a user and a path, so the key is all that separates them; a hoisted key would make two different draft creates collide and the second silently receive the first's response. Wired at 11 call sites. **`block-sources` and `firm-splits` were audited and deliberately left alone**: both are *replace* operations (`write_block_sources(..., replace=True)` deletes first; `firm-splits` runs `.all().delete()` before inserting), so a retry replaces the same set with itself and the `unique_together` constraints are never reached — they are already idempotent. The ~60 action endpoints (`transition`, `approve`, `done`, `restore`, `bulk-*`) are out of scope for the same reason. **Preventive — no backfill or duplicate-hunting command**, as no duplicates were confirmed in the data. 20 backend tests + 10 frontend, including a `TransactionTestCase` two-thread race test that was verified RED against a check-then-create implementation before being accepted.
- **Quota card on ShipmentDetail — which firms burned quota on this truck, and whether it is approved (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** The mirror of the usage list's shipment column: quota is spent by trucks, so the shipment is where operators ask "did this cost quota, whose, and is it signed off?". New `ShipmentQuotaCard` (firm / kg / status / date, total in the footer, empty state when the truck consumed none) reads a new `?shipment=` filter on `GET /export/quota-usage/` — same param, same meaning and the same implementation as `/customs-expenses/?shipment=`, copied verbatim rather than re-derived. **That filter relaxes the D11 season scope**, per Rule A (§4.5): detail pages resolve for any season, so a prior-season shipment opened by direct link would otherwise render an empty quota card while its rows plainly exist. **The relaxation is gated on `can_view_closed()`, not unconditional** — a role holding `quota_usage` but not `closed_season` (`document_team`) stays scoped even with `?shipment=`, or the param would route around the 403 that `/quota-usage/?season=<closed>` returns; that is the exact shape of the 2026-08-07 quota-dashboard date-window bypass, so it is pinned by tests rather than left to the code comment. `resolve_season()` still runs unconditionally, so the close→open gap fails closed and a bad/closed `?season=` still 404s/403s. Read-only; approval stays on the quota screen. 8 backend tests, including the unpermitted-role case and its `?season=<closed>` 403 control.
- **Season lifecycle: close a season (frozen + hidden), open the next one, browse a closed season read-only via a header switcher (feat(core) + feat(export) + feat(contracts) + feat(greenhouse) + feat(frontend) + db, AD-16).** `core.Season` gains `closed_at`/`closed_by` + a filtered unique index enforcing at most one active season; state (`UPCOMING`/`ACTIVE`/`CLOSED`) is derived from those two columns, never stored. New `apps/core/seasons.py` splits `is_active` into `get_active_season()` (write target) and `resolve_season(request)` (per-request read scope, `?season=<id>`) — replacing 9+ ad-hoc `Season.objects.filter(is_active=True)` lookups across `export`/`contracts`/`greenhouse`. `SeasonScopedMixin` applies the resolved scope to every season-bearing viewset (shipments, Sheet, Kanban board, harvest plans, day entries, truck allocations/destinations, local-sell plans, contracts, comments, tasks, quota-usage, advances, customs-expenses, contract-sales, document-packets, clients-report); when no season is active, scoped lists return **nothing**, never everything (D7 — fail closed). New `apps/core/services/season.py`: `close_season()` (freezes + hides, touches no shipment/plan/contract row — D2), `open_season()` (atomic incumbent-deactivate + activate; reopening a closed season is unsupported by design), `close_preview()` (advisory counts for the confirm dialog). Write freeze in two layers — `SeasonNotClosed` DRF permission + `assert_season_open()`/`assert_bulk_seasons_open()`/`assert_season_id_open()` service guards — returning `409 {"error":"season_closed","season","closed_at"}` on every mutating path against a closed season, including `transition_to()`, the Sheet bulk-edit, the two-row Join, and POST-to-collection creates. New `closed_season` admin-configurable permission resource gates who may browse a closed season at all; seeded to `admin`/`director`/`export_manager`/`boss`/`finansist`. `QuotaIssuance` gained a `season` FK for the freeze (later extended to drive the read scope too — see the D11 entry under **Fixed**, which reverses the "consumed FIFO across seasons" reasoning this line originally carried). `/api/v1/auth/me/` gains `active_season: {id,name,status}|null` and `can_view_closed_seasons: boolean`. Frontend: `useSeasonStore` (Zustand) + `useSeasonParam()`/`useSelectedSeason()`/`useSwitchSeason()` mirror the selection to `?season=`; every season-scoped TanStack Query key threads `seasonId`; header `SeasonSwitcher` (hidden when there's nothing to switch to); `useSeasonReadOnly()` drives a persistent `ClosedSeasonBanner` + `disabled` on every create/edit/delete control across Sheet, Detail, Weekly Plan, Assignment Board, and Shipment List; admin `SeasonsPage` gains Close/Open actions with a `close-preview`-backed confirm dialog, and hides Edit/Delete on `CLOSED` rows so the UI can't reopen one. See AD-16 (`docs/ADR.md`) for the design rationale, including the two mid-implementation rulings (D7 fail-closed, D8 archive-coupling reversal).
- **"How the process works" card on the Boss Dashboard, backed by a whitelisted doc-serving endpoint (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** Two tiles — "A shipment's journey" and "BPMN diagram" — each open a process-explainer document in a new tab via `GET /api/v1/export/boss/process-doc/?doc=<slug>` on `BossAnalyticsViewSet` (inherits its `IsBossOrDirector` gate). `?doc=` is user input naming a file, but it is resolved **only** through a hardcoded `{slug: filename}` dict — never a filesystem path built from the request value — so an unlisted slug 404s before any disk access; adding one of the folder's other documents is a one-line dict edit. The documents are served byte-for-byte straight from `docs/how_works/`, never copied into `frontend/`, so that folder stays the single source of truth. 10 tests cover both slugs, an unknown slug, a missing slug, four traversal payloads, a case-variant slug, and — the discriminating case added on review — a request for a real but unlisted file in the same directory (`?doc=walkthrough`), which a naive `Path(dir) / f'{slug}.html'` implementation would serve but the whitelist correctly 404s; a disk read that raises `FileNotFoundError` (curator typo in the dict) now 404s instead of 500ing. **Do not "simplify" this to path-joining** — the traversal tests exist specifically to catch that regression.
- **`ProcessNodeLink` model — BPMN diagram task nodes are now clickable, opening the mapped app screen (feat(p3) + feat(boss)).** The second process-explainer document, `shipment-bpmn.html`, draws a BPMN diagram of the process; after it renders, the page fetches `GET /api/v1/export/boss/process-doc-links/` (same viewset/gate) and upgrades any task block whose id is in the response to a real `<a target="_blank">`. If the fetch fails, 404s, or returns garbage, the diagram is left exactly as rendered — no error, no partial state — so it keeps working offline or with an expired session. The mapping is `export.ProcessNodeLink` (`export_process_node_links`, migration `0059` + data migration `0060` seeding the 20 task nodes, transcribed verbatim from the diagram's own node array). `node_id` is the **join key to that array and is read-only via the API** — changing it silently orphans the row (the block just stops linking, no error anywhere). No create/delete on the admin endpoint: the 20 ids are fixed by the diagram, so a new node needs a diagram change plus a migration, not an admin action.
- **Admin page at `/admin/process-links` to edit the BPMN node→route mapping (feat(core)).** Lists the 20 rows, lets an admin edit `route` / `is_active`; `node_id` and `label` are read-only in the UI even though the API technically permits editing `label`. Gated with `roles={['admin']}` (route guard + menu item) rather than the page-permission matrix — matches the backend's inline `_is_full_admin` check on `ProcessNodeLinkViewSet`, which is **deliberately not registered as a `resource_code`**: doing so would be swept up by the blanket `**{r: _VCRUD for r in _ALL_RESOURCES}` spreads `seed_permissions.py` applies to `director`, `export_manager` and `boss`, silently granting those roles full CRUD access this feature withholds. Route input validates a leading `/` and offers every known app route (`ROUTE_PAGE_MAP` keys) as autocomplete, to guard against dead links from typos.

### Changed
- **The Active toggle is back on the season create/edit form — and now routes through the lifecycle services instead of writing the column (feat(core) + feat(frontend), AD-16).** `dbe9ad8` had removed it because it caused three failures; the domain owner asked for it back, so it was restored with each of the three engineered away rather than reverted. `is_active` is writable on `SeasonSerializer` again, but `SeasonViewSet.perform_create()` always INSERTs the row inactive and then calls `open_season()` when Active was ticked (nothing for the uniqueness validator to collide with, and the incumbent swap stays one atomic transaction with an `AuditLog` row), and `perform_update()` pops the flag out of `validated_data` so `ModelSerializer.update()` never writes it, routing `false -> true` to `open_season()` and `true -> false` to a new `deactivate_season()` service — which stands the season down **without** closing it (`closed_at` stays NULL, status returns to `UPCOMING`), leaving the no-active-season gap D7 already fails closed on. `useCreateSeason`/`useUpdateSeason` now run the same blanket `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` as `useOpenSeason`/`useCloseSeason`, so the write target can no longer move in the database while `/auth/me/` and every season-scoped list keep serving the old season. **A correction to what `dbe9ad8` and AD-16 claimed:** the validator that 400'd the create is a *field-level* `UniqueValidator` DRF 3.17 derives from the `uq_season_single_active` filtered constraint (queryset already `Season.objects.filter(is_active=True)`), not a serializer-level `UniqueTogetherValidator` — `Meta.validators = []` would not have removed it. Declaring `is_active` explicitly on the serializer is what removes it, and is load-bearing. The form's switch defaults **off** on create (adding next year's season must not silently move the write target). `PATCH {"is_active": true}` on a **closed** season is a `400` keyed on `is_active` again (restored `validate_is_active()`, reusing `Season.assert_activation_allowed()`) where it briefly returned `200` having changed nothing; reopening stays unsupported. 12 backend + 3 frontend tests.

### Fixed
- **The quota usage grid silently hid records and under-reported every total on screen (fix(frontend)).** `QuotaUsageGrid` keyed records into a `Map<date_firmId, record>`, but a `(date, firm)` cell holds **many** records — a firm rides several trucks a day, each producing its own `QuotaUsageRecord`. The last one read won and the rest vanished from the grid entirely: the firm column, the row total and the grand total all under-reported, and because `handleCellSave` re-read that same Map, typing into such a cell PATCHed whichever record it happened to keep, leaving the others untouched. The backend never deduped (`services_quota` sums `kg_used` over `counted()` in all three places — dashboard, firm balances, FIFO), so this was the one view in the system disagreeing with the ledger. **37 cells affected on the 2025-2026 season, the worst holding 8 records rendered as 1.** New `QuotaUsageGrid.helpers.ts` owns the rule: `groupRecordsByCell()` buckets into `Map<key, record[]>`, the cell renders `sumKg()`, and `isCellInlineEditable()` makes a multi-record cell read-only — it shows the sum plus a count badge and opens the new `QuotaUsageCellDetail` modal listing each record with its shipment code (linked). `handleCellSave` keeps an explicit guard for that case rather than relying on the render. 11 frontend tests.

- **The close-season confirm dialog now warns about draft quota-usage rows, which closing makes permanently unapprovable (fix(core) + fix(frontend), AD-16).** `close_preview()` returns a fifth counter, `draft_quota_usage`, counted through `usage_season_q()` so no second season-ownership rule enters the codebase. It is deliberately **not** folded into the dialog's body copy: that paragraph promises "nothing is deleted — every record reappears, read-only", which is true of the other four counters and false of these rows (approving is a write to frozen data, and there is no unfreeze). `SeasonCloseModal` renders a separate warning `Alert`, only when the count is > 0, with real `ru`/`tk` translations. The original four keys stay a contract — a test now asserts them explicitly so a rename fails loudly. On the dev database the count is **151** for the open season (15 unlinked drafts + 136 linked to its shipments); measured cost 9.5 ms for the count, 41 ms for the whole preview. 7 backend + 2 frontend tests.
- **A closed season's quota-usage rows with no shipment were fully writable — create, edit, delete AND bulk approve (fix(p3), AD-16 / D11).** Reported by an automated reviewer, reproduced, and it was one verb wider than reported. `freeze_season_of()` resolves `obj.season`, then `obj.shipment.season`; an unlinked `QuotaUsageRecord` has neither (575 of 711 rows on the dev DB are historical imports with no shipment), so it returned `None` — which `assert_season_open()` treats as **open**. Both layers of the write freeze were therefore silent no-ops on those rows: `POST` of a usage row dated inside a closed season returned **201**, a `PATCH` moving one into a closed season **200**, `DELETE` **204**, and `POST /quota-usage/approve/` **200** — each mutating a quota balance the freeze exists to hold. Fixed with a `freeze_season` property on `QuotaUsageRecord` (the model hook `freeze_season_of()` already supports — third user after `ContractSale` and `FinansistAdvance`) that delegates to `season_of_usage()` rather than repeating the date-range lookup, so the row a closed season *lists* is the row it *freezes*; the import is deferred inside the property because `services_quota` imports the models package at load time (a module-level import raises `ImportError` — verified, not assumed). The bulk `approve` action cannot reach that property (a raw id list never calls `get_object()`) and its `assert_bulk_seasons_open(qs, 'shipment__season')` resolved through a NULL FK, matching no season — replaced by `services_quota.assert_usage_batch_seasons_open(qs)`, which applies the shared `usage_season_q()` once per closed season and subsumes the generic call. **Status codes split deliberately:** a row that resolves to **no** season stays a `400` on `usage_date` (a field problem); a row that resolves to a **closed** season is the branch's `409 season_closed` (a state problem). 18 new tests (8 RED before the fix, 3 added in self-review for the approved-row shape, two independent reversions) — and since layer 1 now precedes the `Only draft records can be edited/deleted` check, `PATCH`/`DELETE` on an **approved** unlinked row in a closed season returns 409 where it used to return 400 (the draft-only 400 is untouched outside a closed season).
- **Boss process-explainer documents 404'd in the Docker image (fix(p3), packaging).** `_PROCESS_DOCS_DIR` was `settings.BASE_DIR.parent / 'docs' / 'how_works'` — correct in a repo checkout, nonexistent in the container: `docker-compose.yml` built the backend with `context: ./backend`, so only `backend/` lands at `/app` and `BASE_DIR.parent` is `/`. Both `?doc=` slugs 404'd in the deployed environment, taking the Boss Dashboard "How the process works" card and the whole BPMN click-through with them; the 19 backend tests passed because they run from a checkout. `docs/how_works/` stays the single editable source of truth — the image now gets a build-time snapshot: build context widened to the repo root (`dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile`, COPYs rewritten repo-relative), `COPY docs/how_works /opt/ygt/docs/how_works` deliberately outside `/app` (the dev bind mount `./backend:/app` would shadow it), new `settings.PROCESS_DOCS_DIR` overridable by env (same pattern as `LIBREOFFICE_BIN`), and a new root `.dockerignore` so the widened context excludes `frontend/`, `node_modules/` and the multi-MB root logs. New `ProcessDocsPackagingTests` asserts every whitelisted filename resolves to a real file.
- **`boss` could cancel a shipment through a path that skipped `/cancel/` (fix(p3), data integrity).** `CANCEL_ROLES` was derived as `PRIVILEGED_ROLES | {'admin'}`, so adding `boss` to the export services' `PRIVILEGED_ROLES` also rewrote every `cancelled` edge — though the actual opening was the privileged-role bypass inside `transition_to()`. `POST /shipments/{id}/transition/ {"new_status":"cancelled"}` therefore succeeded and skipped everything `/cancel/` does: open Tasks stayed OPEN, draft `QuotaUsageRecord`s were never cleaned up, and the mandatory reason was never supplied. `CANCEL_ROLES` is now a literal `{admin, export_manager, director}` and the generic transition action rejects `cancelled` with 400 for every role, naming `/cancel/`. No UI regresses — `get_allowed_transitions()` already filtered the cancel edge out.
- **Migration `core/0033` could permanently no-op itself (fix(core)).** Its forward function returned early on `DJANGO_TESTING=true`, but Django records a migration as applied regardless — so a `migrate` run with that variable set in the shell (every documented backend test command in this repo exports it) skipped the one migration whose purpose is to reach a live database, forever. Now gated on `schema_editor.connection.settings_dict['NAME'].startswith('test_')`.
- **Three more pages withheld from `boss` (fix(core)).** `admin.users`, `admin.staff_access` and `feedback.admin_inbox` are each refused by a gate that never consults the permission matrix, so the grant produced dead menu entries — and the feedback inbox silently scoped to his own tickets, reading as "there is no feedback" rather than as an error. Now in a named `_BOSS_DEAD_PAGES` alongside `admin.permissions`, duplicated in `core/0033`, with a test asserting the two lists stay identical. Boss page visibility 41 -> 38 of 42.
- **`export_process_node_links` added to `database/ygt_platform_ddl_v5_1.sql` (db).** The table shipped in migration `export/0059` without a matching block in the DDL of record.
- **Process-links admin page missed three project conventions (fix(frontend)).** A failed fetch rendered an empty table indistinguishable from "no rows" (now an `Alert`, new i18n key in all three locales); plain antd `Table` replaced by `ProTable` per `frontend/CLAUDE.md`; and `useProcessNodeLinks` returned `[]` under `USE_MOCK` with no fixture, so the page was blank in mock mode (`src/mock/processNodeLinks.ts` added).
- **Stored XSS in `ProcessNodeLink.route`, reachable through the boss's BPMN diagram (fix(p3)).** `route` was a free-text `CharField` with no validation, PATCHable by any admin via `/admin/process-links`, and written verbatim into a diagram `<a href>` via `setAttribute()` that the boss clicks (`docs/how_works/shipment-bpmn.html`). A stored `javascript:` value would have executed **in the boss's session** — admin-only gating on the field doesn't contain it, since AD-15 keeps `admin` and `boss` as separate principals and the payload runs in the boss's browser, not the admin's. Closed in two layers: **(1) server** — a `RegexValidator` on the model field (migration `0061`) restricts `route` to blank or an in-app absolute path (`^/([A-Za-z0-9_-]+(?:/[A-Za-z0-9_-]+)*/?)?$`), rejecting any scheme (`javascript:`, `data:`, …) and any protocol-relative `//host` by construction — this is the real boundary, since DRF copies model-field validators onto the generated serializer field, so every write path is covered without a separate serializer check. **(2) client** — `isSafeInAppRoute()` guards the `setAttribute('href', …)` call in the diagram itself, so a row written before the validator existed still can't produce an executable link; this layer alone would **not** have been sufficient, since the API can be called directly, bypassing any client-side check. The admin page's own client-side validation (`ProcessNodeLinksPage.tsx`) is realigned to the same regex — it previously only required a leading `/`, which would have accepted `//evil.example` and produced a confusing 400 from the API instead of catching it client-side. +7 backend tests (`ProcessNodeLinkRouteValidationTests` — 5 rejection + 2 acceptance, asserting against the DB row so a 400 with the value written anyway would still fail; 19/19 green in `tests_process_node_links.py` after the fix) and +2 frontend tests.

### Added
- Boss view/edit mode toggle in the app header — read-only by default, opt-in editing (feat)
- Five previously URL-only pages surfaced in the sidebar: truck forecast, drafts, assignment, domestic sales, prices (feat)

### Changed
- Sidebar reordered by export process phase instead of by module (feat)
- Boss role widened from 3 read-only pages to full access on every page and resource (feat)
- `boss` added to `PRIVILEGED_ROLES` so he can trigger any valid status transition (feat)
- Boss page grant narrowed: `admin.permissions` excluded — `_AdminOnlyPermission` 403s even GET, so the entry was a dead menu item (fix(core))
- Boss resource grant narrowed: `truck_split_default` read-only (Gap 7 / ADR-016) and `sale` delete removed, matching `director` / `export_manager` (fix(core))
- Boss read access widened at three call sites so surfaced pages actually load: `/shipments/overdue/`, the audit log (`AUDIT_VIEWERS`) and `my-sales-reports` (which had silently returned an empty list) (fix(export))
- `POST /shipments/{id}/assign/` opened to `boss` — `/export/assign` is on his process sidebar and its only action 403'd (fix(export))

### Fixed
- **Boss permission widening now reaches existing databases (fix(core)).** `seed_permissions` uses `get_or_create(..., defaults={...})`, and `defaults` applies only on INSERT — every pre-existing database already held all 42 boss page rows and a view-only resource row per resource, so re-running the command changed nothing and a deploy would have produced a half-applied state. Added data migration `core/0033_boss_process_visibility_perms` which `.update()`s the existing rows (with a reverse function restoring the pre-widening 7-page, view-only state) and a test class that drives it against a hand-built pre-state, which the seed-based tests could not.
- **Boss view/edit toggle was structurally inert on the Sheet (fix(frontend)).** `isCellEditable` trusted the backend's per-row `can_current_user_edit` bool, which is emitted for every row and knows nothing about `bossEditMode`, so the `??` fallback to `canEditCell` never fired: inline editing and Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V / Delete over a range stayed fully live for the boss while the header read **Просмотр**. Guard added above the backend-decision read.
- Transition button on Shipment Detail had no `canDo` gate, so the boss could drive a truck through the state machine in view mode (fix(frontend))
- Contract document upload/delete read `resource_permissions` directly, bypassing the toggle — routed through `canDo` (fix(frontend))
- Write controls that the backend always rejects for `boss` are now hidden instead of rendering and 403ing: shipment create (list + sheet toolbar), local sell plan, and the customers / truck-destinations reference-data pages (fix(frontend))

### Added
- **Sheet `truck_plate` cell picks a fleet head+trailer (feat(transport)).** TIR fleet SP3b — the last surface. `SheetCellEditor` special-cases `field_key === 'truck_plate'`: a **non-gapy** shipment gets `SheetTruckSelectEditor` (a two-select overlay — truck head + trailer — with inline "+ Add", mirroring the SP3a/SP3c drawer picker) and saves `truck_head_id` + `trailer_id` + derived `truck_plate = "{head}/{trailer}"` in ONE `patchMultiMutation` with Sheet undo capture (mirrors the R26 `transit_days_temp` virtual cell). A **gapy_satyş** shipment stays a plain text input — no selects, no GPS (HARD RULE). The overlay is **portaled to `document.body`** (`createPortal` + `position:fixed` from the cell's rect) because the cell's `contain: layout paint` + grid scroll-overflow would otherwise clip it — a browser-layout fix jsdom can't verify, so it needs a manual on-screen check. The `"{head}/{trailer}"` compose was extracted to a shared `composeTruckPlate()` util (`utils/truckPlate.ts`) used by both the drawer and the Sheet. 25 frontend tests across the sheet/util/selector files, `tsc` clean. (`processes/fleet-map.md`, `screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated.)
- **Admin fleet management page — `/admin/fleet` (feat(transport)).** TIR fleet SP4: a role-gated (`SHIPMENT_EDITOR_ROLES` — admin/director/export_manager/warehouse_chief/loading_dept_head/loading_dept_head_deputy) admin page with **Trucks** and **Trailers** tabs to list (including inactive), create, edit, and activate/deactivate the `TruckHead`/`Trailer` fleet. Backing the "show inactive" view, both list endpoints now accept **`?include_inactive=true`** (default stays active-only, so the shipment pickers are unaffected). New hooks in `useFleetAdmin.ts` — `useAdminTruckHeads`/`useAdminTrailers` (list-incl-inactive), `useUpdateTruckHead`/`useUpdateTrailer`, and single-call `useAdminCreateTruckHead`/`useAdminCreateTrailer` (create sends the full row in one POST — no create-then-PATCH partial-failure window). `owner_name` exposed as a form field + table column. Route/nav gated to the fleet-editor roles (no `admin.fleet` page_code registered). 6 `FleetAdminPage.test.tsx` tests incl. an `owner_name`-preservation regression on a capacity-only edit; `useFleetAdmin.test.ts` + `tsc` clean. (`screens/fleet-admin.md`, `processes/fleet-map.md`, `reference/api-endpoint-map.md`, `reference/data-model-map.md` updated.)
- **Inline "+ Add" for unknown truck/trailer plates in `ShipmentTruckSelector` (feat(transport)).** TIR fleet sub-project 2 / SP3c Task 2: typing a plate with no matching option in either the truck-head or trailer `Select` shows an "Add ..." button in the dropdown (`dropdownRender`, matching the existing `CitySelect`/`CountrySelect` pattern); clicking it creates the fleet row via `useCreateTruckHead`/`useCreateTrailer` (Task 1) and immediately links it onto the shipment. `truck_plate` is composed from the just-created plate string passed straight through `save()`/`plateFor()`, not looked up from `heads`/`trailers` — that list hasn't refetched yet at that point (the create mutation's invalidation is async), so looking it up would silently write a truncated combined plate. 6/6 `ShipmentTruckSelector.test.tsx` tests, `useFleet.test.ts` still green, `tsc --noEmit` clean. New i18n keys `shipment_edit_drawer.add_truck`/`.add_trailer` (tk/ru/en).
- **`Trailer` fleet endpoints — list/create/update (feat(transport)).** TIR fleet sub-project 2 / Task 2: `GET/POST/PATCH /api/v1/transport/trailers/`, mirrors the `TruckHeadViewSet` pattern from Task 1 minus GPS matching (trailers have no `TraccarDevice` link). `GET` (any authenticated user) lists active-only trailers, `SearchFilter` on `plate_number`, no pagination; `POST`/`PATCH` gated to `CanEditShipment`. 3 new tests (`apps/transport/tests/test_fleet_api.py`), 67/67 `apps.transport` tests green. (`api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **`TruckHead` fleet endpoints — list/create/update (feat(transport)).** TIR fleet sub-project 2 / Task 1: `GET/POST/PATCH /api/v1/transport/truck-heads/`. `GET` (any authenticated user) lists active-only tractors with `has_gps` (search on `plate_number`/`owner_name`, no pagination); `POST`/`PATCH` gated to `CanEditShipment`. New `device_for_plate()` helper (`apps/transport/services/matching.py`) plate-matches a `TraccarDevice` on create, same resolution `_pick_device()` uses. `PATCH` sees inactive rows too, so admins can deactivate/re-activate. 5 new tests (`apps/transport/tests/test_fleet_api.py`), 62/62 `apps.transport` tests green. (`api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **One-time `import_tir_fleet` management command (feat(transport)).** Sub-project 1 / Task 2 of the TIR fleet integration: `TirClient` (read-only pyodbc reader of the external Z_TIRWEB `truck_heads`/`trailers` tables, raises `TirUnavailable` on connection failure or on an unset `TIR_DB_CONN_STR`) + `import_fleet()` service, upserting `TruckHead`/`Trailer` by their **source id preserved** (so `Shipment.truck_head_id`/`trailer_id` stay valid once wired up), auto-linking each `TruckHead` to a `TraccarDevice` by normalized plate. Idempotent — safe to re-run. `TIR_DB_CONN_STR` has no default in `settings.py` (credentials never committed); must be set in `backend/.env` before running the command — see `.env.example`. After import, `DBCC CHECKIDENT` reseeds both tables' identity above the imported max so subsequent app-created rows don't collide (empirically confirmed non-load-bearing since MSSQL auto-advances the identity counter on `SET IDENTITY_INSERT` inserts, kept anyway as a safety net). 3 new tests (`apps/transport/tests/test_tir_import.py`, TIR client mocked — no live DB hit), 53/53 `apps.transport` tests green.
- **Shipment↔truck GPS link (feat(transport)).** `ShipmentDetail` shows the truck's live
  position — `ShipmentDeviceLink` (manual override, transport-owned, `export.Shipment`
  OneToOne) + `resolve_device_for_shipment()` resolver (manual override > auto-match by
  tractor plate > none). New endpoints `GET /transport/shipments/{id}/position/`,
  `PUT|DELETE /transport/shipments/{id}/device/`, `GET /transport/devices/`. New
  `ShipmentTruckLocationCard` on `ShipmentDetail` (mini map, override picker, reset-to-auto).
  Auto-match only covers shipments whose truck has a registered Traccar device (~30/79 as of
  2026-08-01); others show a "No GPS device linked" empty state with a manual picker.
  (`processes/fleet-map.md`, `api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **`celery-worker` + `celery-beat` docker-compose services (feat(transport)).** Deployment follow-up to the Celery poller below — both reuse the `backend` build/image, environment, and volumes; `celery-beat` runs with `--schedule /tmp/celerybeat-schedule` to avoid writing into the read-only prod image. Prod override in `docker-compose.prod.yml` drops the dev bind mount and adds `restart: unless-stopped`, matching `backend`'s prod env-var pattern. (`processes/fleet-map.md` updated with a "Running the scheduler" section, incl. the Windows `-P solo` requirement and a deploy-order warning: `pip install` must run before restarting Django now that Celery is imported on every process boot.)
- **Celery app + beat-scheduled `poll_traccar` task, 120s (feat(transport)).** Supersedes the "1-min poller" line below — that was the manual-cron plan at first ship; live polling is now driven by **Celery beat every 120s**, and the manual `poll_traccar_positions` management command (still 1-shot, still available) is kept only for ad-hoc/debug runs, not as the scheduler. Added standard Django+Celery wiring — `config/celery.py` (`app.autodiscover_tasks()`), loaded from `config/__init__.py`, plus a `CELERY_` settings block reusing the existing `REDIS_URL`/`RUNNING_TESTS`/`TIME_ZONE`: no result backend (fire-and-forget), `CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER = RUNNING_TESTS` so tests never touch a broker, static `CELERY_BEAT_SCHEDULE` (no django-celery-beat) with `CELERY_TASK_TIME_LIMIT = 110` / `CELERY_TASK_SOFT_TIME_LIMIT = 100` and a matching `expires: 110` on the beat entry — all kept under the 120s interval so a slow/hung run is killed before the next tick fires, ruling out two overlapping `poll_traccar` runs hitting the same `update_or_create` rows on MSSQL (deadlock/IntegrityError risk). New `apps/transport/tasks.py:poll_traccar` reuses `sync_devices()`/`sync_positions()` and swallows `TraccarUnavailable` (returns `ok=False`, no retry storm). 2 new tests (`apps/transport/tests/test_tasks.py`); 27/27 `apps.transport` tests green. Docker-compose worker/beat services deferred to a follow-up task. (`processes/fleet-map.md` updated.)
- Fleet Map (transport): live truck positions from Traccar — standalone truck/driver/device registry, `/api/v1/transport/live-positions/`, react-leaflet map page. (Originally shipped with a 1-min cron poller — see the Celery entry above for the scheduler that replaced it.)
- **Brute-force login lockout — escalating ladder (feat(core) + feat(frontend)).** The login endpoint had *no* rate limiting or lockout: `/api/v1/auth/login/` accepted password guesses at any rate.
