Added **django-axes** with a custom escalating cool-off keyed on the `(username, IP)` pair: 3 failed logins → **30 min** block, 3 more → **5 h**, then **1 day**, with a fresh 3 attempts per tier. The ladder tier is tracked in a Redis counter (`apps/core/security_axes.py`), the block length is computed by `escalating_cooloff` (wired to `AXES_COOLOFF_TIME`), and the tier is bumped once per episode via axes' `user_locked_out` signal and cleared on successful login (done explicitly in `LoginView` — JWT login never calls Django `login()`). Real client IP is read from nginx's `X-Real-IP` (`AXES_CLIENT_IP_CALLABLE`). Locked requests return **429** `{error, detail:"locked_out", retry_after}` + `Retry-After`; the login page shows a localized countdown (`login.locked_*` in tk/ru/en). Per-attempt audit trail kept (`AccessAttempt`/`AccessFailureLog`, admin-visible). Disabled under tests by default (`AXES_ENABLED = not RUNNING_TESTS`); 10 lockout tests green (escalation, fresh-3-per-tier, reset-on-success, username+IP isolation), 48 existing cache/auth tests still green. (`processes/authentication.md`, `api-contract.md` updated.)
- `completeness` block on the shipment detail endpoint — required/missing fields derived live from `TaskRule.target_fields`, not a stored field or a new rule table (feat(p3))
- `driver_name` / `driver_phone` / `truck_plate` inputs on Shipment Detail's transport group — previously required by the task system but had no inputs on the page (feat(p3))

### Changed
- **`Season.is_active` is now the write target only — read scope resolves per-request (AD-16).** `?season=<id>` on a season-scoped list endpoint browses a different season (default: the active one); a closed season requires the new `closed_season.can_view` permission, else `403`. At most one season may be `is_active=True` — enforced by a filtered unique index (`uq_season_single_active`), not just application logic.
- **Inside a closed season, the `is_archived` operational/archive split now requires BOTH permissions, not one implying the other (D8, AD-16).** Archived rows of a closed season are visible only to users who also hold `_ARCHIVE_VIEW_ROLES` membership — the original design's "closed-season access implies archive access" was reversed after review found it silently escalated the new `closed_season` grant into a superset of archive-view.
- **`shipment-lifecycle.md` corrected**: the Sheet no longer fetches unconditionally "ALL active-season shipments" — `?season=<id>` overrides the default, subject to the same permission gate as every other scoped endpoint.

### Data
- Backfilled nullable `season` FKs on `Contract`/`WeeklyLocalSellPlan` (by date range; unmatched rows reported, not dropped) and on `QuotaIssuance` (by `issue_date`; originally for the write freeze only, now also the read scope — see the D11 entry under **Fixed**). 24 of 25 issuances matched; `QuotaIssuance#34` (2026-07-06) falls in the gap between the two seasons and is reported rather than guessed.

### Fixed
- **The seventh instance: `harvest-forecast/remaining/` leaked a closed season's pool (fix(p3), AD-16).** Found by the structural sweep the `quota-dashboard` fix prompted — enumerating every `APIView` and non-`ModelViewSet` route rather than grepping for `?season=`. `get_remaining_for_date()` filtered `HarvestDayEntry` on `entry_date` alone; that model's `season` FK is non-null and seasons run Sept→Aug without overlapping, so a `?date=` inside a closed season *was* a closed-season read — available to **any authenticated user**, with no `?season=` parameter for `closed_season.can_view` to hang off. Exactly the shape `harvest-plans/block-summary` was fixed for, and `harvest-forecast` was absent from the design spec's §4 endpoint table, which §4 itself defines as a bug. The read now resolves the season (`?season=` optional, default active; `404` unknown id; `403` closed without permission; `[]` during the close→open gap, D7) and passes it to the service, which scopes both halves of the subtraction. The two **write** validators sharing that service — draft-create `validate()` and the race-safe `assert_draw_within_pool()` — deliberately keep no season predicate: they check a draw against the shipment's own date on a path the write freeze already restricts to an open season, and scoping them would change what a create is validated against rather than gate a read. The draft composer sends no `?season=` on purpose — a draft is always created in the write target, so its pool must be the write target's. All 24 pre-existing harvest-forecast tests stayed green unmodified.
- **`quota-dashboard`'s season gate was bypassable through the date window — now clamped (fix(p3), AD-16 / spec §4.9).** Found by review of the fix below. Routing the endpoint through `resolve_season()` gated *which season the caller names*; it did not gate *which dates the caller asks for*. `_parse_date()` returned `?date_from=`/`?date_to=` verbatim — the resolved season supplied only the fallback — and `build_quota_dashboard()` aggregates on dates alone. So `document_team` (holds `quota_issuance`, not `closed_season`) could send the closed season's own range with **no** `?season=` at all: `resolve_season()` returns the active season, the permission check passes, and the response carries the closed season's aggregates — the exact payload the new 403 exists to withhold. Reachable from the UI too: the page's `RangePicker` has no season bounds and the hook forwards both params verbatim. The resolved season now **clamps** the window (`max(from, season.start_date)` / `min(to, season.end_date)`) instead of merely defaulting it. Clamping rather than pushing a `season` FK into the aggregates is deliberate — it is monotonically restrictive, so no number changes for a window already inside the season, and it needs no ruling on whether `build_quota_dashboard()` should become season-aware (that question stays open). A window wholly outside the season inverts (`date_from > date_to`) and every aggregate reads it as empty: fail closed. **The general lesson, recorded in spec §4.9: on a date-windowed endpoint, a default window is not a bound.**
- **`quota-dashboard` now enforces the closed-season permission — the sixth read path that built its own queryset (fix(p3) + fix(frontend), AD-16).** `QuotaDashboardView` read `?season=` straight off the query string and looked the row up itself, so `resolve_season()` — and with it `closed_season.can_view` — was never consulted. Verified on the live database: `document_team`, `loading_dept_head` and `loading_dept_head_deputy` hold `quota_issuance` but not `closed_season`, so they are correctly 403'd on `/quota-issuances/?season=<closed>` yet could still read that same closed season's aggregates here. Now routed through `resolve_season()`, which also normalises the endpoint onto the contract every other scoped read already follows: `?season=` is **optional** and defaults to the active season (it was the only scoped read where omitting it 400'd), an unknown id returns **404** instead of 400, a closed season without permission returns **403**, and the close→open gap returns the empty payload with its shape intact (`kpis` all zero, `per_firm`/`weekly_flow` `[]`) rather than the just-closed season's numbers — D7 fail-closed, matching `dashboard/summary`. Resolution happens **before** the 60s cache read, so a permitted user's payload can't be served to an unpermitted one, and the cache key carries `season.pk` rather than the raw parameter. Frontend: the dashboard's own season filter hides closed seasons from anyone without the permission and takes its default from the same filtered list, so during the gap an unpermitted user is no longer silently defaulted onto the most recent closed season. `build_quota_dashboard()` itself stays date-driven — the resolved season supplies the default window and the gate, not a predicate on the aggregates; pushing the FK in would change published numbers rather than visibility and needs its own ruling.
- **Season `is_active` is server-set — the Active switch is gone from the season form (fix(p3) + fix(frontend), AD-16).** Confirmed by the domain owner during live testing: creating a season **400'd** whenever one was already active, because the admin form defaulted the Active switch on and DRF derives a `UniqueTogetherValidator` from the `uq_season_single_active` filtered constraint; and toggling Active through Edit left the whole app on stale season state, since `useUpdateSeason` invalidates only `['admin-seasons']` while `useOpenSeason`/`useCloseSeason` invalidate everything including `/auth/me/` — the database was right the whole time, every list just kept sending the old `?season=`. It also bypassed `open_season()`/`close_season()`: no atomic incumbent swap, no `AuditLog` row (the residual the final whole-branch review ticketed). `is_active` is now in `SeasonSerializer.read_only_fields` and the create/edit modal has no Active control, so a new season always lands `UPCOMING` and **Open and Close are the only routes** to changing the write target. Marking the field read-only without a serializer default also removes that auto-derived validator, which is what unblocks the create. `SeasonSerializer.validate_is_active()` was deleted as unreachable — DRF never calls `validate_<field>` for a field absent from `to_internal_value` — leaving `Season.save()`'s `assert_activation_allowed()` as the guard for the ORM, Django admin and management commands, and `open_season()`'s own refusal for the service path. Wire-level change: `PATCH {"is_active": true}` on a closed season now returns **200 having changed nothing** where it returned 400; the outcome is unchanged and stronger, since no value is accepted at all. Seed/import paths are unaffected — `seed_data`'s `update_or_create` and `close_season()`/`open_season()` write at the model layer, never through the serializer. `seasons.is_active` stays in i18n: it is the admin table's Active column title.
- **Quota never crosses a season boundary — D11 reverses D10 (fix(p3) + fix(frontend), AD-16 / spec §4.7).** D10 had exempted `quota-issuances` from read-scoping because issuances were assumed to be consumed FIFO *across* seasons; the domain owner reversed that during user testing. Both halves now stop at the season line. **Display:** `QuotaIssuanceViewSet` is season-scoped on its `season` FK like any other direct-FK endpoint, and `QuotaUsageViewSet`'s scope moved off `include_null_link` (which meant "visible in *every* open season") onto the same per-season predicate consumption uses. **Consumption:** `compute_fifo_usage(product_type, season)` and `compute_firm_quota_balances(product_type, season)` now take the season explicitly — a shipment can only draw on its own season's quota, and leftover issuance expires with the season instead of carrying forward. `QuotaUsageRecord` has **no** `season` FK and **no** `issuance` FK (only `usage_date` and a nullable `shipment`; 575 of 711 rows have no shipment), so its season is *derived* by the new `services_quota.usage_season_q()` — `shipment.season` when linked, else `usage_date` inside the season's range — rather than stored: `freeze_season_of()` reads `obj.season` before `obj.shipment.season`, so an unstamped new column would have silently read a closed-season row as open and loosened the write freeze D10 added. All 711 rows resolve; none is left unassigned. `compute_firm_quota_balances` also switched its issuance side from an `issue_date` range to the `season` FK and now follows the *resolved* season rather than the write target. Both quota cache keys carry the season id (`fifo_usage:<product>:<season>`, `quota_firm_balances:<product>:<season>`) and `invalidate_quota_caches()` enumerates the seasons to bust them. `POST /quota-issuances/` now returns 400 during the close→open gap instead of creating a row stamped with no season, which under read-scoping would be invisible the moment it was saved. Frontend: `useQuotaIssuances` and `useQuotaFirmBalances` thread `seasonId` through their keys and send `?season=`. **Measured against the live dev database: no firm's balance changes.** Consumption totals for the season that holds the data are identical before and after (19,771,100 kg tomato / 144,000 kg pepper); the only ledger difference is that `QuotaIssuance#34`'s allocation leaves the map, and it was already consuming 0 kg. That issuance (25,000 kg, 2026-07-06, firm *Eziz Doganlar*) falls in the gap between the two seasons, keeps `season = NULL`, and is now reachable by direct link only — reported for an owner ruling, not guessed at. **Post-review fixes:** `POST`/`PATCH` on `/quota-usage/` now 400 when the resulting row would belong to no season — D11 *created* that failure mode, since before it `include_null_link` surfaced an unlinked row under the active season whatever its date, and both `usage_date` and `shipment` are writable while the usage grid POSTs no shipment; the grid's month picker also disables months outside every season. `used_kg` on the issuance **detail** route is keyed off the row's own season rather than the request's, which was reporting `0.00` for any issuance outside the active season because detail deliberately bypasses season scoping. And `QuotaUsageViewSet`'s now-dead `season_field`/`include_null_link = True` were removed — the latter advertised exactly the behaviour D11 forbids.
- **`/me/tasks/` is now season-scoped — the My Tasks screen ignored the season switcher (fix(core) + fix(frontend), AD-16 / spec §4.8).** `TaskViewSet` was scoped during the original build, but the My Tasks screen never lists from it: `useMyTasks` calls `/me/tasks/` (`MeTaskListView`, an `APIView` in `apps/core/views_me.py` — not a router registration, which is why the endpoint table missed it), which had no season filter and no `seasonId` in its query key. Switching seasons left the board unchanged. Now scoped on `shipment__season` with `include_null_link`, identical to `TaskViewSet`: weekly-plan/local-sell-plan tasks (no shipment) stay visible under an open season and drop out when a closed season is explicitly selected; `?season=` returns 403 on a closed season without `closed_season.can_view` and 404 on an unknown id; the close→open gap fails closed. `SeasonScopedMixin.apply_season_scope`'s predicate was extracted to a reusable `apps.core.seasons.season_scope_q()` so the `APIView` applies the identical rule rather than a hand-rolled filter that could drift. `useMyTasks`' key is now `['my-tasks', seasonId, role]` and sends `?season=`. `/me/kpi-today/` is deliberately left unscoped (a closed season's tasks cannot be completed, so a filter would only blank the tile).
- **Season lifecycle — four findings from the final whole-branch review (fix(p3) + fix(frontend), AD-16).** (1) `POST /export/advances/` accepted `shipment_ids` and bulk-created junction rows with no season check, so a `finansist` could create an advance linked to closed-season shipments and move that season's `/ledger/` totals — now `assert_bulk_seasons_open()`, placed *before* the parent `FinansistAdvance` INSERT (`ATOMIC_REQUESTS` is off, so guarding only the `bulk_create` would have committed a link-less orphan advance that the read scope treats as "unlinked" and shows anyway). (2) `FinansistAdvanceViewSet` carried no `SeasonNotClosed`, so generic `PATCH`/`DELETE`/`reconcile` still mutated a frozen season's ledger — and adding the permission alone would have been a **no-op**, since `freeze_season_of()` returned `None` for a model whose shipment links live in a junction table. New `FinansistAdvance.freeze_season` property derives the same anchor the read side already derives, so both freeze layers resolve it: **frozen if ANY linked shipment is in a closed season** (the "either side frozen" rule `ContractSale` sets), `None` for a link-less advance so it stays editable, `None` for unsaved instances. Consequence: an advance spanning an open and a closed season is wholly immutable, including unlinking the closed side. (3) **D7 fail-closed applied to the two date-range endpoints that predated it**: `dashboard/summary` substituted a *current-month* range when no season resolved — and since seasons run Sept→Aug, the just-closed season's range still covered today, so any authenticated user read aggregates over frozen rows during the close→open gap; it now returns an all-zero/empty payload with the response shape preserved (which also zeroes its LIVE `in_transit`/`selling`/`active_shipments` counters for the duration of the gap). `team-kpi?period=season` returned `(None, None)` — no lower bound — silently becoming an ALL-TIME window; `compute_team_kpi()` now returns `[]`. `period_window()` is unchanged. (4) Logout cleared the realtime store, the query cache and the IDB prefs but left `useSeasonStore.selectedSeasonId`; since `navigate('/login')` is an SPA transition, the next user on a shared terminal inherited the previous user's season. 28 backend tests + 1 frontend test; fresh-DB suite 1567 tests with a failure list byte-identical to the pre-existing baseline.

### Changed
- **Export contract: buyer director also printed in Turkmen (Latin) in the TK column (feat(p4) + db).** Mirrors the seller fix: added `ImportFirm.contact_person_tk` (Turkmen/Latin spelling, migration `core.0031`, editable on the import-firm admin page). The builder now feeds `buyer_director_tk` from `contact_person_tk` (falling back to the RU form / modal override when blank) and `buyer_director_ru` from `contact_person`/override, so the TK column no longer shows a Cyrillic buyer director. `manage.py check` + `tsc` clean.
- **Export contract: Turkmen dates spelled out (matching the source), not numeric (feat(p4)).** The §2.6 delivery deadline and §8.1 validity date rendered as `30.06.2026 ý.` in the Turkmen column (numeric was a safe stopgap since the source's ordinal-date grammar was inconsistent). Now spelled like the original — `2026-njy ýylyň 30-njy iýunyna` — using the export team's confirmed rule: the ordinal suffix follows vowel harmony of the spelled number's last word (`-njy` after a back vowel a/u/y/o, `-nji` after a thin vowel i/e/ü/ä/ö, so days 6/9/10/16/19/26/29/30 → `-njy`), reusing `amount_words_tk`; months use a locative-form table. RU stays genitive-spelled. (`_tk_ordinal` + `_date_tk_spelled`; +2 tests.)
- **Export contract: seller director printed in the right alphabet per column (feat(p4) + db).** The bilingual contract showed the seller's director from the single Cyrillic `ExportFirm.director` in **both** the Turkmen and Russian columns. Added `ExportFirm.director_tk` (Turkmen/Latin spelling, migration `core.0030`, editable on the export-firm admin page); the template's `{{ seller_director }}` split into `{{ seller_director_tk }}` (TK column, from `director_tk`, falling back to `director` when blank) and `{{ seller_director_ru }}` (RU column, from `director`). `manage.py check` + `tsc` clean. The bilingual contract showed the seller's director from the single Cyrillic `ExportFirm.director` in **both** the Turkmen and Russian columns. Added `ExportFirm.director_tk` (Turkmen/Latin spelling, migration `core.0030`, editable on the export-firm admin page); the template's `{{ seller_director }}` split into `{{ seller_director_tk }}` (TK column, from `director_tk`, falling back to `director` when blank) and `{{ seller_director_ru }}` (RU column, from `director`). `manage.py check` + `tsc` clean.
- ShipmentDetail rebuilt: accordion replaced with an always-open grid of stage cards (nothing collapsed behind a click), a completeness summary driven by `TaskRule`, click-to-edit rows with a persistent save confirmation and a visible error+retry state, and comments moved onto the page (hero "Discussion" thread + per-field comment icon, reusing the Sheet's `CommentsDrawer`) (feat(p3) + feat(frontend))
- **`DetailFieldRow` split into 4 files to get back under the 150-line component limit (refactor(frontend)).** Tasks 4/5/9 had layered the save-state indicator, click-to-edit, and the per-field comment icon onto one 284-line component. Extracted `useDetailFieldAutosave` (the debounce/save state machine), `DetailFieldValue` (the Text/FieldEditor swap + comment icon), and `DetailFieldRowStatus` (the four-state save indicator); `DetailFieldRow.tsx` stays the 150-line composition point. No behaviour change — all 111 existing frontend tests (incl. `DetailFieldRow.test.tsx`'s click-to-edit and blur-race-guard tests) pass unmodified.
- **Placeholder Links card dropped from Shipment Detail (chore(frontend)).** `ShipmentLinksCard` showed three hardcoded rows (Logo Tiger / Trip Management / GPS) with no backing data. Deleted the component and its three orphaned i18n keys from all three locale files.

### Fixed
- **Fleet Map: poller now refreshes device status and auto-registers new Traccar devices; speed converted knots→km/h (fix(transport)).** `poll_traccar_positions` called only `sync_positions()`, so `TraccarDevice.status`/`last_seen` were frozen at the one-time `seed_traccar_devices` run — `is_online` never changed and trucks added to Traccar after the seed never appeared on the map (`sync_positions` skips unknown deviceIds). The poll now calls `sync_devices()` first, then `sync_positions()`, reporting both counts. Also, `sync_positions()` stored Traccar's raw `speed` (knots) straight into `DevicePosition.speed`, which the serializer/popup label as km/h (74 km/h showed as 40) — now converted at sync time (`speed * 1.852`, rounded to 2dp; `None` stays `None`). +4 tests (`test_commands.py`, `test_sync.py`); 25/25 `apps.transport` tests green.
- RouteTimelineRail no longer disappears on mobile (fix(p3))

### Changed
- **Word is now the CMR's default output; Excel dropped from the UI (feat(p4) + feat(frontend)).** The CMR dropdown offers **Word · PDF** per language (was Excel/Word/PDF) now that the office's own Word form backs the document. **PDF renders from the Word form, not the xlsx** — converting the spreadsheet would emit the older overlay layout instead of the office document (cost: ~6s vs ~1.6s; correctness over speed, and PDF was already the slow path). The spreadsheet overlay is **kept wired** at `?fmt=xlsx` (templates, registry entries and `render_xlsx` untouched) for future use — re-add `'xlsx'` to `FORMATS` in `CmrDocumentsButton.tsx` to restore the menu entry. 63 doc-gen tests green (default-is-Word + xlsx-still-served); `tsc` clean.

### Fixed
- **Word CMR now uses the office's own Word form as its template (fix(p4)).** The first Word CMR rebuilt the layout as a Word table derived from the xlsx grid; positions were right but the formatting was not the office document. Replaced with the real form (`data/CMR_RU_template.docx`) — a flat sequence of positioned paragraphs, no tables — kept verbatim as the layout, with each sample value swapped for a Jinja tag and the fixed labels (`Брутто:` / `кг.` / `вес поддона` …) left untouched; the EN variant reuses the same positions with the `CMR EN` sheet's English wording. The form has **two consignor blocks**, so `build_cmr_overlay_values` now exposes `sender1_*` / `sender2_*` per export firm (a 3rd firm is appended to slot 2, never dropped) alongside the joined `sender_name`. `driver_passport` and `truck_model` render blank — `Shipment` has no such columns, so the crew fills those two by hand. Verified by rendering RU + EN against the source form. 62 doc-gen tests green.

### Added
- Team KPI leaderboard page (/team/kpi) — per-user tasks-completed ranking with on-time %, overdue-now, active hours, period switcher (feat)
- GET /api/v1/core/team-kpi/ endpoint — public, 60s cache (feat(core))
- **Word variant for the CMR on the Documents page (feat(p4) + feat(frontend)).** The CMR dropdown now offers **Excel · Word · PDF** per language (was Excel/PDF). LibreOffice refuses xlsx→docx, so the Word output is a purpose-built template rather than a conversion: `build_cmr_docx.py` derives the Word table's column widths / row heights from the **xlsx template itself** (× its 60% print scale) and places each field via the same `_CMR_OVERLAY_CELLS` map, merging each field across the free columns to its right to reproduce Excel's cell-overflow (without the merge, long values like the sender name were clipped to one column). Extracted `build_cmr_overlay_values()` as the single source of the overlay's field values — `build_cmr_overlay` maps it onto xlsx coordinates, the Word template consumes it as a Jinja context — so the two formats cannot drift. New registry keys `cmr_ru_docx` / `cmr_en_docx`; `ShipmentCmrView` maps `?fmt=xlsx|docx|pdf` (default `xlsx`) to the right template. Word renders in ~65 ms, matching the other Word documents; PDF remains the only slow path (LibreOffice). **The `.xlsx` stays the registration reference** for printing onto the pre-printed customs blank — a Word table only *approximates* Excel's print registration, so the Word variant needs a physical test print before it's trusted for that. 62 doc-gen tests green (+1 endpoint test); `tsc` clean. (`processes/document-generation.md` updated.)

### Added
- **"My tasks" role filter for supervisors (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** Supervisors (`export_manager`, `boss`, `admin`, `director`, superusers) receive *every* role's tasks from `/me/tasks/` — `MeTaskListView` skips the `assignee_role` filter for them — so the board was one undifferentiated pile with no way to tell whose work is whose. Worse, it was silently truncated: measured against live data, `count=1213` while `page_size=1000` returns 1000, so 213 tasks were dropped off the tail; `document_team` showed 484 rows in that page against 550 real tasks — 66 invisible. Added an optional `?assignee_role=` to `/me/tasks/` (supervisor-gated; unknown role → 400) and a supervisor-only role `Select` on the My tasks page. Filtering is **server-side** precisely because a client-side filter would slice the already-truncated payload and lie about completeness; fetched as its own query every role fits under the cap (largest is 550). `/me/kpi-today/` accepts the same param under the same gate so the KPI tiles follow the selected role, with the role added to its 60s cache key. The supervisor view is a **superset** of that role's own screen (no `assignee_user` clause), which is the intended oversight semantic. Non-supervisors are unaffected — the param is ignored and their own-role lock is unconditional (explicit security test). **Not fixed / still true:** with no role selected the all-roles view remains truncated at 1000. +6 backend tests (52 in `tests_task_api` green); `tsc` clean. (`screens/self-board.md`, `api-contract.md` updated.)

### Changed
- **nginx edge rate limit on `/api/` — DoS backstop before the workers (feat(frontend)).** The DRF throttle rejects floods, but only *after* a Django worker has already been spent parsing/authenticating the request — under a hard flood the 3 workers burn on rejections. Added an `nginx` edge limit in `frontend/nginx.conf` that drops the flood at the door: `limit_req_zone` at **30 r/s** per client IP with `burst=60 nodelay`, `limit_conn` 30 per IP, over-limit → **429** (matches the app's own 429s). Deliberately generous so normal use never trips it — 30 r/s sustained is far beyond a human, and `burst=60 nodelay` serves a page's parallel API burst (dashboard fires ~15-20 calls on load) instantly. Static assets (`location /`) and WebSocket (`/ws/`) are **not** limited. Also set `client_max_body_size 25m` so no single upload can exhaust memory/disk (was nginx's 1m default — document/feedback attachments need headroom). Zones key on `$binary_remote_addr` = the real client (this nginx faces the LAN directly in prod, same address `X-Real-IP` carries to the app). Note: `nginx -t` was not run at author time (no docker locally) — validate on deploy. Tunable by editing the conf + redeploy.
- **PDF render concurrency limit — worker-starvation guard (feat(p4)).** Each PDF export shells out to LibreOffice and blocks a worker for up to 120s; with only 3 uvicorn workers, a few concurrent PDF requests would stall the *entire* API (every endpoint, for everyone). Added a shared-Redis slot limiter in `document_render.py`: at most `PDF_MAX_CONCURRENCY` (default **2**, settings-overridable) conversions run at once across all workers, enforced with atomic `cache.add` slot keys (self-freeing 180s TTL if a worker dies mid-render). A request that waits >8s for a free slot raises `PdfBusyError` — a `DocumentRenderError` subclass, so the existing views already return it as **503** with no changes. Net effect: PDF load can never consume more than 2 of the 3 workers, so the API stays responsive under a PDF burst; excess PDF requests get a retriable 503 instead of hanging. `.docx`/`.xlsx` exports (no LibreOffice) are unaffected. Verified: slot exhaustion blocks the 2nd concurrent render and frees on release; 62 doc-gen tests green. (Longer-term: move rendering to an async queue — tracked in `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`.)
- **API rate-limit throttle — flood / DoS backstop (feat(core)).** The API had *no* request-rate limit: django-axes caps failed logins but nothing capped the total request rate, so an authenticated client (or a stolen session) could hammer any endpoint and a small burst of concurrent requests could saturate the 3 uvicorn workers. Added DRF `DEFAULT_THROTTLE_CLASSES` with two proxy-aware throttles (`apps/core/throttling.py`): `ProxyAwareAnonThrottle` (default **120/min** per client IP) and `ProxyAwareUserThrottle` (default **1200/min** per user PK). Both reuse axes' `X-Real-IP` resolution (`security_axes.client_ip`) so the anonymous bucket keys on the real per-device address instead of collapsing every user onto the nginx container IP. Rates are env-tunable (`THROTTLE_ANON` / `THROTTLE_USER`), set to `None` under tests so the suite is unaffected, and counters live in the shared Redis cache so the limit holds across all workers. Generous by design — a backstop against abuse, not a limiter of normal use. Verified: `get_ident` reads `X-Real-IP`, requests block past the limit, buckets isolate per IP. `manage.py check` clean.
- **Shared Redis cache backend + login hardening (feat(core)).** Added a `CACHES` default (Django 5.1 built-in `RedisCache`, LocMem fallback for tests/dev-without-Redis) — required so the brute-force lockout counters are consistent across the 3 uvicorn workers. Side effect: the existing 60s API caches (team-kpi, dashboard, me/kpi-today) become **shared** across workers instead of per-process. Also raised the password `min_length` 5 → 8 (new/changed passwords only). Production stops publishing `backend:8000` to the host (`docker-compose.prod.yml`) so nginx's `X-Real-IP` can't be bypassed.
- Team KPI leaderboard redesigned: per-user cards, single-hue ranking bar chart, 14-day trend sparklines; `trend` field added to /core/team-kpi/ rows.
- Task model: added completed_by FK; populated at all five completion sites for per-user attribution (db)
- **Shipment-detail query key centralised — `getShipmentDetailKey()` is now the single source of truth (refactor(frontend)).** Follow-up to the progress-bar fix below. The key `['shipment', String(id)]` was hand-written as a literal at **20 call sites across 11 files** (`useDrafts` ×5, `usePallets` ×3, `useCustomsExpenses` ×2, `useShipments` ×2, `SheetCellEditor` ×2, `useShipmentPatch`, `useTaskActions`, `useSalesReport`, `CommentComposer`, `TransitionButton`, `ShipmentDetail`) with nothing tying them together — TanStack types `queryKey` as `readonly unknown[]`, so a forgotten `String()` or a typo'd `'shipmnet'` compiles clean and fails **silently** at runtime as a view that never refreshes. That is exactly how the progress-bar bug shipped, and `useSalesReport` already carried a comment warning the next reader about it (a comment is not a defence). All 20 sites now call the helper, so the mismatch is no longer expressible. Two sites (`useSalesReport`, `ShipmentDetail`) previously omitted `String()` entirely and were correct only by virtue of their param typing — note the helper is deliberately **not** a drop-in for `String()`: it passes nullish ids through unchanged (`String(undefined)` would key the literal `'undefined'`), which is what keeps a disabled query's key stable; verified all 20 call sites pass non-null values. No behaviour change. `tsc` clean, 80 frontend tests green.

### Fixed
- **My tasks drawer: the progress bar didn't move after filling a field — only after closing and reopening (fix(frontend)).** The bar counts `isFieldFilled(shipment, fk)` against the shipment **detail** cache, which two separate cache misses left stale. (1) `useShipmentDetail` keyed the query on the raw id, and the drawer passes `task.shipment` as a **number** (`['shipment', 42]`), while every invalidation site in the app uses `['shipment', String(id)]` (`'42'`) — TanStack matches key parts type-strictly, so `42 !== '42'` and each invalidation silently no-opped. The drawer was the only numeric caller; `useSalesReport` already documented that this key "must be a string". Close+reopen appeared to work only because `destroyOnClose` unmounts the observer and the remount refetches past the 30 s `staleTime`. Fixed by normalising through a new `getShipmentDetailKey()` — which also repairs the same latent bug in `DetailSlide` and `ShipmentEditDrawerForId`. (2) `SheetCellEditor`'s `junctionMutation` / `customFieldMutation` invalidated only `['shipments','sheet']`, never the detail cache — and `firm_splits` / `block_sources` are `target_fields` on seeded task rules, so the key fix alone would not have covered those tasks; both now invalidate the detail key too. Note the bar now updates one round-trip after the edit (the optimistic path is the sheet cache, not the detail one). +5 frontend tests pinning the TanStack matching semantics in both directions; `tsc` clean.
- **Loading tasks were assigned to `warehouse_chief` — the loading department never saw their own work (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** `tasks.fill_loading_data` and `tasks.trigger_loading_start` both seeded `assignee_role='warehouse_chief'`, a leftover of the May 2026 change that moved Soltanmyrat to `loading_dept_head` (rule 15's own comment already said "filled by Soltanmyrat"). Confirmed against live data: exactly one account holds `warehouse_chief` and it is a placeholder literally named `warehouse_chief`, while the real loading department is `soltanmyrad` + 5 deputies. Nobody noticed because both rules are `ALL_FIELDS_FILLED` and auto-close when `weight_net` etc. are filled by whoever fills them — the wrong role never blocked the work, it just hid the to-do list. Both rules repointed to `loading_dept_head`. **New concept:** `TASK_ROLE_EQUIVALENTS` + `task_roles_for()` in `apps/core/roles.py` — `Task.assignee_role` holds one role, but a deputy acts with identical authority to their head (June 2026 decision), so the head's tasks are the deputies' work. One helper backs all three exact-match sites (`MeTaskListView` visibility, `IsTaskActor` actions, `_compute_kpi` tiles) so they cannot drift — visibility without action-permission would show a card the user cannot touch. Mirrored frontend in `constants/roles.ts` (`taskRolesFor`) for `SelfBoardTaskDrawer`'s editable-vs-read-only decision. Deliberately **not** derived from `MANAGEABLE_BY_ROLE`, which includes `weight_master` (21 users) who must not receive loading tasks (explicit no-leak tests both for visibility and actions). `reconcile_open_tasks_with_rules` now also syncs `assignee_role` (added to `_AXES`) so repointing a rule moves the open tasks; it only touches active states, so completed history is never rewritten. Applied: 2 open tasks moved to `loading_dept_head`; **146 completed tasks remain on `warehouse_chief` as history**. +4 backend tests (115 task tests green); `tsc` clean. (`reference/task-rules.md` updated.)
- **Weekly plan grid: block managers saw an incomplete block set on upcoming weeks (all blocks appeared only on past/closed weeks) (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** Root cause: editable plan cells (`HarvestDayEntry`) exist only after `initialize_harvest_week` runs, which was **admin/director-only** and all-or-nothing. Weeks were initialized ad-hoc, so a not-fully-set-up week showed only the blocks that already had rows. Confirmed against the live DB: past + current weeks carry all 15 active blocks, but **every future week is 0 blocks** (and early-season weeks were partial — e.g. `2025-W40` has a single block, exactly the reported "only my own block"). So a manager opening an upcoming week to plan saw an empty/partial grid while closed weeks looked complete. No code ever *filtered* other blocks — it was missing data. Fix: a new **daily** command `run_weekly_plan_setup` (in `export`, so it can call both greenhouse + export services) runs, for the current **and** next ISO week of the active season, idempotent `initialize_upcoming_weeks()` (all active top-level blocks, `entered_by=NULL`) **and** `generate_weekly_plan_tasks()` — so the weeks managers actually plan always carry every block *and* get their "fill plan" tasks, without an admin remembering to click "Initialize Week"/"Generate tasks" (both manual buttons kept). The 5-min `run_harvest_dispatcher` stays notifications-only. Also: **P3 critical-late** now seeds to **09:00** Monday (was 00:00) — `GreenhouseConfig.get_solo()` seeds `plan_critical_late_at_time=09:00` (field default kept at 00:00 to avoid a migration into the uncommitted core chain); it's a tunable, so the **existing singleton row must be set to 09:00 via the config admin / one-off**. Separately fixed the grid ignoring its own deep-link params: `WeeklyPlanGrid` now reads `?week=&year=` (lands a `weekly_plan` task / heatmap link on the linked ISO week instead of always today) and highlights the `?block=` row (by id or code; never filters). +5 backend tests (MSSQL green); `tsc` clean. **Note:** the auto-setup only helps where the daily cron is scheduled; back-populating far-future or historical partial weeks still needs a manual "Initialize Week". (`processes/weekly-harvest-planning.md`, `operations/cron.md` updated.)
- **Adding a quota issuance didn't unlock the firm on the Sheet — it stayed "no quota"/unselectable (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)).** The Sheet firm-split editor's hard-block reads per-firm remaining quota via `quota-firm-balances`, which is cached at two layers that issuance create/update/delete never busted: the backend `quota_firm_balances:{product_type}` cache (60s TTL — invalidated only by usage *approve*) and the frontend `['quota-firm-balances']` query (60s `staleTime`). So a freshly-issued firm kept `remaining_kg=0` for up to 60s (backend) or until an unrelated refetch (frontend), staying disabled. Backend: extracted `invalidate_quota_caches()` and now call it from `QuotaIssuanceViewSet.perform_create/update/destroy` (and refactored `QuotaUsageViewSet.approve` to reuse it). Frontend: `useCreateQuotaIssuance` / `useDeleteQuotaIssuance` now also `invalidateQueries(['quota-firm-balances'])`. (Note: the Sheet hard-codes `product_type='tomato'`, so a pepper issuance still won't affect it — unchanged, by design.) Backend firm-balances suite green; `tsc` clean. **Follow-up hardening:** consolidated to a single canonical `invalidate_quota_caches()` in `services/quota_sync.py` (the duplicate in `views_quota.py` was removed and now imports it) that busts **both** `fifo_usage:*` and `quota_firm_balances:*` — previously the firm-split paths (`set_firm_splits`, create, cancel/restore) called the quota_sync version which busted only FIFO, so assigning a firm split (which auto-creates *draft* usage and moves committed `remaining_kg`) left the Sheet's balance stale for up to 60s. Also fixed a pre-existing race in `QuotaUsageViewSet.approve`: the cache-bust ran **inside** `transaction.atomic()` (delete-before-commit → a concurrent read could repopulate stale pre-approval numbers for the full TTL); now deferred via `transaction.on_commit(invalidate_quota_caches)`.
- **Sheet comment badge showed a count with an empty thread behind it (fix(p3)).** Deleting a comment soft-deleted only that one row, never its replies. A deleted *root* with live replies left the replies `is_deleted=False` (still counted by `comment_counts`, the per-cell badge) but unreachable in the drawer, which lists only non-deleted roots — so the cell showed e.g. "2" yet opened to an empty thread. `CommentViewSet.destroy` now cascades the soft-delete to the comment's non-deleted replies; data migration `export.0055` clears already-orphaned replies. (+1 test; `comments-tasks.md` + `api-contract.md` updated.)

### Changed
- **Request letters (CT-1 / FITO / Customs) rebuilt to match the office Excel sheets (feat(p4)).** The generated letters were one-sentence stubs; the real `letter CT1` / `fito` / `customs` sheets carry more. Rebuilt in `build_templates.py` (now three dedicated builders, Times New Roman, A4) with the context builders extended: **CT-1** adds the sender + consignee (Отправитель / Грузополучатель) blocks and the Нетто / Брутто / Кол-во мест weights; **FITO** adds the truck line (`1 автомашина: {plate}`), sender/consignee blocks; **Customs (ARZA, Turkmen)** adds the truck table (T/b · plate · product · boxes · gross) and the full four-paragraph legal boilerplate (gümrük Kodeksi articles, the finance-ministry order) with the generate-time `place_loading` inserted, plus the `Telekeçi` signature. New shared `_letter_figures(invoice)` gives per-firm net/gross/boxes/plate (same rule as the invoice line). Renders verified against all three sheets; doc-gen letter test strengthened to assert the parties/weights/table content. 57 doc-gen tests green.
- **Invoice document rebuilt to match the office Excel sheet — two pages incl. the packing list (feat(p4)).** The generated invoice was a simplified developer mock-up that didn't match the `InvoiceRU`/`InvoiceEN` sheets it replaces. Rebuilt in `build_templates.py`: tall bordered seller/buyer boxes carrying the full multi-line bank requisites (docxtpl renders the `\n` blocks natively), the route rows (origin / loading / delivery / transport), the full 9-column line-items table with `ИТОГО`, an italic pallet note, and the seller signature block. Adds the **page-2 `Упаковочный лист` / Packing List** — previously **missing entirely** — which repeats the header/parties/route with a 6-column weights-only table (no price/packing/sum). A4 page setup. Context builders unchanged. Doc-gen tests green. (`processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **CMR switched from docx to an xlsx print-overlay to match the pre-printed official form (feat(p4)).** The office prints truck data on top of the pre-printed official 24-box CMR form, and the source `CMR RU`/`CMR EN` sheets carry the exact registration geometry (A4 @ 60% scale, column/row sizes, merges) that python-docx can't reproduce — so a docx CMR was the wrong vehicle. Now an **xlsx overlay**: geometry-preserving templates `cmr_ru.xlsx`/`cmr_en.xlsx` (built by `build_cmr_xlsx.py` — source sheet stripped to fixed labels, data/formula/helper cells blanked, print area pinned); `build_cmr_overlay(shipment, lang, overrides)` returning a `{cell: value}` map (RU/EN use different coordinate maps); `TemplateSpec.engine` (`docx`|`xlsx`); `render_xlsx` (openpyxl cell-fill); `generate()` branches on engine and `render_pdf` takes the source extension. **CMR native download is now `.xlsx`** (was `.docx`); `fmt=pdf` unchanged. Old `cmr_ru.docx`/`cmr_en.docx` + `build_cmr()` removed. Known simplification: multiple firms join into one sender box (exact for single-firm trucks; per-cell firm1/firm2 split is a future refinement). Contract (KZ) unchanged — its `1-Contracts` sheet is a data table, not a document layout, so there's no Excel format to match. 57 doc-gen tests green (CMR endpoint/content-type + xlsx smoke updated). (`processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **Firm-split quota is now a hard block — a firm with no remaining quota can't be added to a truck (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** The Sheet R9 `firm_splits` editor previously only *warned* (soft toast, saved anyway); it now **prevents selection** on both tiers. Frontend (`SheetCellEditor`): no-quota firms (`remaining_kg <= 0`, incl. firms with no allocation) render **`disabled`** with the `⚠ no quota` tag; only firms not already on the split are disabled so an existing over-committed firm can still be removed; commit strips any that slipped through and shows an error toast. Backend (`set_firm_splits`): re-checks via `compute_firm_quota_balances('tomato')` and rejects a **newly-added** no-quota firm with `400` before writing any rows (existing firms exempt). i18n key `sheet.firm_no_quota_warning` → `sheet.firm_no_quota_error` × tk/ru/en. Not covered: the two-column join / `create_draft` path. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated; `tsc` clean, `manage.py check` clean.)
- **Unified Packing Template — one gross-net row (whole truck + firm shares), replacing PackingPreset + SplitTemplate + the derivation (feat(p3) + feat(p4) + feat(frontend) + db).** Consolidates the two-catalog + hidden-derivation model into **one** explicit template that mirrors an Excel `gross net` row. `export.PackingTemplate` (parent, whole-truck packing → CMR) + `export.PackingTemplateShare` (each firm's explicit net/gross/boxes/pallets → that firm's Invoice); migration `export.0054` **deletes** `PackingPreset` + `SplitTemplate` and repoints `Shipment.packing_preset` → `packing_template`. Nothing is derived — the review-flagged numerator/denominator bug is gone with the derivation. Nested-shares CRUD API `/api/v1/export/packing-templates/` (read any auth; write admin/director/export_manager); `seed_packing_templates` (5 full rows). The `shipment-packing` endpoint now **applies a template**: `scope:'template'` copies each share onto the firm's `ContractSale` packing fields (editable per truck) and sets the firm weights from the share nets via the **quota-safe** `set_firm_splits` path (so `kg_used = weight_kg` stays correct); `scope:'firm'` edits one firm's values; `scope:'swap'` exchanges two firms' weight + packing. Invoice reads the sale's explicit packing (fallback shipment); CMR reads the template whole-truck (BRUT = gross with pallet). Frontend: the Sheet `packing` popover now picks one template → per-firm **editable** numbers + Σ-check + swap; a single `/admin/packing-templates` page (whole-truck fields + `Form.List` shares editor) replaces the two old admin pages; `PackingPreset*`/`SplitTemplate*` types/hooks/components/pages removed; `packing_template.*` / `sheet.packing.*` / `nav.admin_packing_templates` i18n × tk/ru/en. 131 backend tests green (doc-gen, apply/edit/swap endpoint, nested-shares catalog API, sheet suite) + frontend `tsc` clean. (`docs/how_works/packing.md` rewritten; supersedes the two entries below.) **Post-review hardening:** `scope:'swap'` now preserves the other firms on a 3+ firm truck (a bare two-firm map deleted the rest — HIGH); `scope:'template'` wrapped in one `transaction.atomic()` and returns `no_sale_firms` for firms with no linked `ContractSale`; `swap` returns `packing_swapped`.

### Added
- **Sales report is now a sales-rep task from departure (step 4), and closing the lifecycle keys on the report itself (feat(p3)).** Two cooperating TaskRules replace the old date-only close. **(1) Step-4 reminder:** a new `tasks.submit_sales_report` rule (`sales_rep`, `MANUAL_DONE`) is generated the moment a truck departs (`yola_chykdy`), so the rep sees "fill the sales report" on the board from step 4 — matching the "Needs report" worklist window. It **must** be `MANUAL_DONE`: a field-based (auto-resolving) task on step 4 would gate `is_step_trigger_satisfied` and freeze the truck at step 4 until the report is filled weeks later; `MANUAL_DONE` tasks are exempt, so it stays a non-gating reminder. **(2) Retargeted close:** the `satyldy → tamamlandy` trigger moved from the standalone `sales_report_date` date field to the `sales_report` OneToOne (report existence) — the shipment completes when the actual (rich) report exists, not when a separate date is picked. **Close link:** the engine never auto-resolves `MANUAL_DONE`, so `set_sales_report` now calls new `close_sales_report_task(shipment, user)` (`services/task_rules.py`), which marks the reminder DONE and runs `shipment.save()` so the report-existence trigger resolves + auto-advances — resolving on satyldy *entry* for the common early-fill path (report saved mid-transit) and immediately for late-fill at satyldy. **Backfill:** new `python manage.py backfill_sales_report_tasks` (run after `seed_task_rules`) creates the reminder for departed shipments predating the rule and advances `satyldy`-with-report shipments to `tamamlandy` (`--dry-run` / `--limit` / `--skip-advance`). 6 new tests green (non-gating step-4 advance, close-on-fill, idempotent close, early/late satyldy advance); `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change). (`processes/sales-report.md` + `reference/task-rules.md` updated.) **Deploy:** run `seed_task_rules` then `backfill_sales_report_tasks` (preview with `--dry-run` first).
- **Sales-report block breakdown + weekly-plan actuals from shipment-code date (Phase 3 of the weightmaster→finance feature) (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** Closes the loop from weightmaster pallet data to both the sales report and the weekly plan. **(A) Sales report:** the Processing tab now shows the per (block × variety) loaded-weight breakdown (e.g. `MIDELICE, block F, 9143 kg`) via the Step-2 `BlockBreakdownCard` (reads `GET /block-breakdown/`, live from pallets) — read-only, so the finansist verifies instead of retyping the numbers they used to hand-key from the paper ticket. **(B) Weekly plan:** `rollup_actuals_for_date` now buckets shipments by the day encoded in the numeric `shipment_code` (`DDMMNNN/YY`) via new `parse_shipment_code_date()`, instead of `loading_started_at` — which is null on 32/142 shipments and can drift a calendar day. `shipment_code`-DDMM equals `shipment.date` 100% of the time in prod (0/139 mismatch), so this is the reliable "date from the code" the operators asked for, and it makes the 32 null-loading shipments visible to the plan. The rollup also folds sub-block sources (F1/F2) into their parent (F) so nothing is lost regardless of block_sources grain. Verified end-to-end on real data (2026-06-13 → 5 entries, 90,500 kg). 8 rollup tests rewritten (code-date bucketing, null-loading counted, wrong-date excluded, sub-block→parent fold) + 22 tests green across the feature; `tsc` + `manage.py check` clean. (`processes/weekly-harvest-planning.md` updated.)
- **Weekly-plan truck allocation: selectable export destinations + over-allocation warning (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db).** Two features on the `TruckAllocationTable` embedded in `WeeklyPlanGrid`. (1) The manager now **chooses which destinations** appear as allocation rows for a week instead of the grid auto-showing every active `TruckDestination`. The choice is persisted per (season, week, year) in the new `export.weekly_destination_selections` table (`WeeklyDestinationSelection` model + `WeeklyDestinationSelectionViewSet` with a `set/` replace-action, gated by `truck_allocation` write roles). When a week has no saved selection, the grid falls back to `TruckDestination.is_default` (new bool; data migration seeds Russia/Kazakhstan/Gapy-Satys `True`, leaves Kyrgyzstan and others pickable-but-not-default; admin-toggleable from the Truck Destinations page). (2) A new **Planned Trucks** row = per-day sum of the selected destinations' splits; when planned > capacity (`round(dayKg / 18500)`) the day cell turns red and a week-level `Alert` lists the offending days — **non-blocking** (plans are estimates). New hooks `useTruckDestinationSelection` / `useSetTruckDestinationSelection`; `plan.*` + `truck_dest_admin.*` i18n × tk/ru/en. Migrations `core.0027`/`core.0028`/`export.0056` applied; `tsc` clean; `makemigrations --check` clean. (`processes/truck-allocation.md` + `reference/api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **"Generate contract" — bilingual TK/RU export-contract .docx/PDF from a Contract (feat(p4) + feat(frontend)).** A "Generate contract" button on the Contract detail page produces the two-column Turkmen/Russian sale agreement (the `NNN/YY-…-EXP` document), extending the existing doc-generation framework: new registry key `contract_kz` (scope=`contract`, the first bilingual-in-one-file template), `build_contract_context`, and `GET /api/v1/contracts/contracts/{id}/agreement/?fmt=docx|pdf&buyer_director=&delivery_deadline=` (an `@action` on `ContractViewSet`, gated by the `contract` resource view perm). The template `contract_kz.docx` was cloned from a real signed contract to preserve the exact legal layout/logo; only variable fields are Jinja tags. **Seller parametrized:** renders from `contract.export_firm` (bilingual name — bare, the template supplies the `HJ`/`Хозяйственное общество` form; address; director with its `Директор` title stripped; `bank_details_*` blob collapsed to one line) so it's correct for **all** export firms, not a hardcoded Ýigit. **Buyer (fidelity A):** the flat `name_company`/`address`/`bank_details` blob (no new columns — a structured-bilingual-buyer option was considered and rejected as duplication) + bilingual country name; `buyer_director` + `delivery_deadline` (§2.6) are generate-time overrides; validity (§8.1) ← `Contract.end_date`. **KZ-gated:** the template's clauses reference Kazakh customs (§4), so the endpoint 400s a non-KZ buyer and the button is disabled (tooltip) unless `import_firm_country_code == 'KZ'` (exposed on the contract detail serializer). **Amount-in-words** is spelled in both languages by a new hand-rolled `services/amount_words.py` (no `num2words` dependency; RU thousands gender/plural, TK dropped-`bir`), tested against both real contract amounts (7830, 566892) + edge cases. Dates: RU spelled (`30 июня 2026`), TK numeric (`30.06.2026 ý.`) — TK ordinal-date morphology is inconsistent even in the source. Frontend `ContractAgreementButton.tsx` (modal: director + deadline + docx/pdf); `contracts.generate.*` i18n × tk/ru/en. Backend `manage.py check` clean; template render verified end-to-end for a non-YGT seller (seller/buyer/dates/amount-words all correct, no hardcoded data leaked); `tsc` clean. (`processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **Contract generator: buyer director from the firm + optional signature-block stamps (feat(p4) + feat(frontend) + db).** Two follow-ups on the export-contract generator. **(1) Director from data:** the buyer's director now defaults to the firm's stored `ImportFirm.contact_person` ("Director's Full Name"); the modal pre-fills it (editable) and only truly asks when the firm has none — the `buyer_director` param overrides per generation (`import_firm_director` exposed on the contract detail serializer). **(2) Stamps:** the template was cloned from a real signed contract and carried both original parties' seals as embedded images — these are now **stripped** (drawing elements + media files + relationships removed) so a generated draft never shows someone else's seal. A new **"With stamps"** checkbox (`?stamps=1`, off by default) stamps the section-9 signature block with each firm's **own** uploaded seal + signature: new `ExportFirm.director_seal`/`director_signature` FileFields (migration `core.0029`, mirroring ImportFirm's) for the seller, `ImportFirm`'s existing fields for the buyer, uploadable on each firm's admin page (`ExportFirmSerializer` + `MultiPartParser`). Builder emits a `StampImage` marker (deferred FieldFile ref — builders stay I/O-free) only when stamps are on **and** the image exists; `render_docx` reads the bytes → docxtpl `InlineImage` (missing/unreadable file → blank, never breaks the doc). Placeholders `{{ seller_seal }}`/`{{ seller_signature }}`/`{{ buyer_seal }}`/`{{ buyer_signature }}`; `contracts.generate.with_stamps*` i18n × tk/ru/en. Verified: stamps off → 0 images, on with 3 uploads → 3 placements, on with none → blank. `manage.py check` clean; `tsc` clean. (`processes/document-generation.md` updated.)

### Changed
- **Parent-grain block_sources + (block × variety) rollup from pallets (Phase 2 of the weightmaster→finance feature) (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** `close_pallet_manifest()` now rolls pallet **net** weights up into `ShipmentBlockSource` at **parent-block grain** — sub-blocks F1/F2 merged into F — replacing the draft's allocated split with the measured pallet weights. This makes the weekly-plan rollup (`rollup_actuals_for_date`, keyed on parent blocks) *able* to match these sources; it does not by itself land the actual, because the rollup still filters on `loading_started_at` (usually null post-AD-1) — the day-source fix that actually populates it is Phase 3. Not yet run close→rollup→`HarvestDayEntry` end-to-end. New choke-point service `services/block_sources.py::write_block_sources()` normalizes+merges at every write site (shipment **create**, **set-block-sources**, **manifest close**), because the weekly plan (`HarvestDayEntry`) is keyed on top-level blocks and a sub-block source silently missed `rollup_actuals_for_date`. New `GET /shipments/{id}/block-breakdown/` (`compute_block_variety_breakdown()`) returns the per (block × variety) net-weight breakdown that feeds the sales-report block section (Step 3) — surfaced as a `BlockBreakdownCard` on PalletManifest (`useBlockBreakdown`, `pallet.bb_*` i18n × tk/ru/en). Backfill command `normalize_block_sources [--apply]` (dry-run default) fixes 13 legacy shipments with sub-block rows (OD/OG → O, incl. merging OD+OG → O). 13 tests green (normalize/merge, close writes parent grain, breakdown + endpoint); pre-existing unseeded-perms test failures unaffected; `tsc` + `manage.py check` clean. (`api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **Weightmaster Excel import → Pallet Manifest (Phase 1 of the weightmaster→finance feature) (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).** New `POST /shipments/{id}/pallets/import-weightmaster/` (multipart `file`) parses the weightmaster loading-detail `.xlsx` and returns a **dry-run preview** `{rows, warnings, summary}` — it does NOT save. Parser `services/weightmaster_import.py` reads the fixed template (header row 1; data rows 2..N until col A blank), resolving each pallet's `crate_type` (by `CrateType.weight_kg`), `variety` (by `TomatoVariety.name`, case-insensitive), and `sub_block` (by `GreenhouseBlock.code`); harvest date parsed from the `DD,MM,YYYY` text cell; load code (e.g. `10AP116`) extracted from KODLAMA. **Nothing is silently dropped** — an unresolved crate/variety/block returns a null id plus a warning carrying the raw text, for the user to fix in the grid before saving via the existing pallets upsert. `summary.code_mismatch` warns (does not block) when the file's load code ≠ `shipment_code`. Frontend: "Upload weightmaster Excel" button on `PalletManifest.tsx` loads preview rows into the editable grid (`weightmasterRowToEditableRow`), shows a warnings alert, and surfaces "Filled by: {name}" (from `Pallet.created_by`). Hook `useImportWeightmaster`; `pallet.*` import i18n × tk/ru/en. 8 tests green (parser resolve/warn/stop-at-blank/non-template + endpoint role-gate/preview/save) + `tsc` clean. (`api-endpoint-map.md` updated.) Verified end-to-end against the real `weightmaster_report_10AP116.xlsx`: 33 pallets, 0 warnings, net 18557.008 kg, harvest 2026-04-10.
- **Pallet-manifest write authority unified on `PALLET_WRITE_ROLES` (fix(p3)).** The pallet fill/close endpoints listed `weight_master` + `warehouse_chief` as allowed in-body, but `DynamicResourcePermission` maps their POST to `shipment.can_create` — **False** for both roles, so it silently 403'd the very roles that own the manifest (Artykow Maksat). `get_permissions()` now drops the DB-perm gate for the write paths — `manifest_close`, `import_weightmaster`, and `pallets` **POST only** (GET `/pallets/` keeps its `can_view` gate) — same pattern as `set_sales_report`, making the in-body `PALLET_WRITE_ROLES` allowlist the sole authority. Added roles `loading_dept_head` + `loading_dept_head_deputy` to that allowlist (per request). Grants no role beyond the allowlist; fixes the latent weight_master/warehouse_chief block.
- **Split Template catalog — reusable firm-split divisions (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db).** Complements the packing presets: where the whole-truck config gives the truck totals, a **`SplitTemplate`** says how the truck's weight divides among the firms — the Excel section labels (`10000/8000`, `14000/3000`, `11300/6700`, `4100/9700/4200`, …). `export.SplitTemplate` stores `weights` as a comma-separated CharField (MSSQL-safe, no ArrayField) with `part_count`/`total_kg` derived; migration `export.0053`; seeded by `seed_split_templates` (9 standard splits). CRUD API `/api/v1/export/split-templates/` (read: any auth; write: admin/director/export_manager). In the packing panel a **"Split into firms"** picker lists templates matching the truck's firm count; picking one sets each firm's weight via the **quota-safe** `set_firm_splits` endpoint (`useApplyFirmSplit`, so draft quota sync runs), and each firm gets a weight dropdown to **swap** shares (total preserved — poka-yoke). New `/admin/split-templates` management page (reuses the `export.packing_presets` page-perm), `SplitTemplateSelect`, `split_template.*` / `sheet.packing.split*` / `nav.admin_split_templates` i18n × tk/ru/en. 13 backend tests green (catalog API incl. weights validation/normalisation) + frontend `tsc` clean. (`docs/how_works/packing.md` updated.)
- **Packing Preset catalog — the digital "gross net" sheet (feat(p3) + feat(p4) + feat(frontend) + db).** The Excel `gross net` sheet is a pick-list the document team selects from **before loading** (real weighbridge numbers don't exist yet) to fill the Invoice + CMR. Digitized as `export.PackingPreset` — one preset = one packing config (`name`, `product_type` tomato/pepper — "Bulgar" is Turkmen for bell pepper, so those rows are just `pepper` — `net_kg`, `gross_kg`, `box_count`, `pallet_count` `Decimal(5,1)` for 16.5 half-pallets, `pallet_weight_kg`, `is_active`, `sort_order`; migration `export.0052`). **Poka-yoke split model:** you pick **one whole-truck config** on the shipment (`Shipment.packing_preset`, migration `export.0052`) → it fills the **CMR**, and each firm's Invoice packing is **derived** from it by that firm's weight share (`services/packing_split.py`), so per-firm values always sum to the truck — an inconsistent split (10000+10000 on an 18000 truck) is impossible. NET per firm = the firm's own weight (never `Shipment.weight_net`, ADR-023). Any derived gross/box/pallet can be **overridden** per firm (`ContractSale.gross_kg`/`box_count`/`pallet_count`/`pallet_weight_kg`, migration `contracts.0010`). BRUT = gross **with** pallet so `gross_without_pallet = gross_kg − pallet_weight_kg` (also fixed a pre-existing CMR fallback direction bug). Catalog holds **whole-truck configs only** (a half-truck can't be mis-picked as a whole truck). New CRUD API `/api/v1/export/packing-presets/` (read: any auth; write: admin/director/export_manager) + `seed_packing_presets` (whole-truck configs, reusing `TruckSplitDefault` for net caps). Frontend: the **unified per-truck packing panel** (`ShipmentPackingPanel`, export Sheet synthetic `packing` row → popover) — pick one whole-truck config, each firm shows its derived net/gross/boxes/pallets with editable overrides and a live **Σ-check** banner that turns red on mismatch; backed by `GET|POST /api/v1/contracts/shipment-packing/` (derive + override, `.update()` writes). Plus `PackingPresetSelect`, a `/admin/packing-presets` management page, `packing_preset.*` / `sheet.packing.*` / `sheet.row.packing` i18n × tk/ru/en. Deliberately **no historical import** — the sheet is a template catalog with no key to join its rows to sales. 53 backend tests green (doc-gen incl. derive + override regressions, packing endpoint incl. consistency/poka-yoke, catalog API) + frontend `tsc` clean. (`docs/obsidian/reference/packing-preset-model.md` (new), `contracts-contract-sale-model.md`, `processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **Link shipments to an advance from the UI** (feat(frontend)).
  Two paths, both wiring up backend support that previously had no UI:
  - **New Advance modal** — a self-fetching, search-driven multi-select (`ShipmentMultiSelect`)
    that sends `shipment_ids` on create (`FinansistAdvanceCreateSerializer.shipment_ids`).
  - **Existing advance** — the row-expansion "Linked shipments" panel now has an attach control
    (`ShipmentSelect` + allocated amount) calling `POST /advances/{id}/link-shipment/` and a
    per-row unlink button calling `DELETE /advances/{id}/unlink-shipment/{shipment_id}/`
    (hooks `useLinkShipmentToAdvance` / `useUnlinkShipmentFromAdvance`).

  Previously the modal never sent `shipment_ids` and there was no link/unlink UI, so every
  UI-created advance was unlinked and the Sheet's R24 "Resminama pul berildi" cell
  (`has_doc_advance`, read-only, driven by `Exists(FinansistAdvanceShipment)`) could never
  flip to ✓. All three mutations invalidate `SHEET_QUERY_KEY` so the R24 cell updates
  immediately. i18n in tk/ru/en. (`advances-reconciliation.md` updated.)
- **Task Engine guard: system check for renamed-field drift** (fix(p3)).
  New `apps/export/checks.py` (`check_task_rule_fields`, registered under `Tags.database` via
  `ExportConfig.ready()`) fails `manage.py migrate` / `check --database` when any active
  `TaskRule.target_fields` or `condition_field` names a Shipment attribute that no longer exists.
  Root cause it prevents: migration `0042` renamed `cargo_code` → `shipment_code` in the
  Sheet/Comment/Notification tables but missed `TaskRule.target_fields`, so the stale
  `fill_loading_data` rule referenced a dead field and the task could never auto-complete (hung
  silently with all real data filled). The check mirrors the resolver's `hasattr(Shipment, ...)`
  access and skips cleanly when tables aren't migrated yet. Live data already repaired via
  `seed_task_rules` (rule re-seeded, 11 stale tasks reconciled, 9 auto-closed).
- **Admin can permanently delete a draft shipment from the detail page** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)).
  New `POST /api/v1/export/shipments/{id}/hard-delete/` (`ShipmentViewSet.hard_delete_draft`) —
  admin/superuser only, refuses any non-draft shipment with 400. Shares the quota cleanup +
  audit + cascade with `bulk-delete` via the extracted `_hard_delete_targets(user, targets)`
  helper. Surfaced as a "Delete draft" danger button on `ShipmentDetailHero` (visible only when
  `status_code === 'draft'` and the user is admin/superuser); the `useHardDeleteDraftShipment`
  hook `removeQueries` the detail key (no 404 refetch flash) and the component navigates to
  `/export/shipments`. i18n in tk/ru/en. 2 new backend tests. (`api-contract.md`,
  `api-endpoint-map.md`, `shipment-lifecycle.md`, `quota-management.md` updated.)
- **Weekly "fill the local sell plan" task for the seller role** (feat(p3) + db + feat(frontend)).
  New non-shipment `Task` kind `local_sell_plan` (`export.0051`), mirroring the weekly harvest-plan
  task: one shared role-wide task per ISO week (`assignee_role='seller'`, `assignee_user=null`,
  `scope_year`/`scope_week`). Created as a side effect of `initialize-week` and by a cron-backstop
  command `generate_local_sell_plan_tasks` (defaults to the current ISO week). Auto-resolves
  (lazily, on the `/me/tasks/` read path) once every `WeeklyLocalSellPlan` row for the week is
  submitted/approved **or** is a zero-total draft (a firm with nothing to sell can never leave
  draft, so an all-zero draft must not block); a rejected row or a non-zero draft keeps it open.
  Service `apps/export/services/local_sell_plan_tasks.py`; the task card reuses `PlanTaskCard`
  (SelfBoard now routes any non-shipment task to it via `isPlanTask`); the card link deep-links
  the Quota → Local Sell grid to the task's week (`?week=&year=`). 7 new tests.
  (`docs/obsidian/processes/comments-tasks.md` updated.)
- **Local Sell plan: `admin` can now initialize/approve from the UI** (fix(frontend)).
  `LocalSellPlanGrid` gated the "Initialize Week"/approve actions on `isManager` which omitted
  `admin`, so an admin saw an empty grid with no way to seed it — even though the backend
  (`LOCAL_SELL_APPROVE`) grants admin those rights. Added `admin` to the frontend check.

- **Responsible block manager shown on the Weekly Plan grid** (feat(frontend) + feat(p3)).
  `WeeklyHarvestPlanSerializer` now returns `block_manager_names` (string array of the block's
  active `BlockManagerAssignment` users — full name, falling back to username), prefetched
  N+1-safe via `active_manager_assignments`. The grid renders the names under the block name
  (and under the block-code tag in the transposed view).
  (`docs/obsidian/processes/weekly-harvest-planning.md` updated.)

### Changed
- **Weekly-plan tasks are now per-block, not per-manager** (feat(p3) + db + feat(frontend)).
  A `weekly_plan` Task previously covered *all* of a manager's blocks at once and only
  completed when every block's whole week was filled — so filling block K but not L (or
  leaving the unmeasured Sunday cell blank) left the task stuck OPEN. Added `Task.scope_block`
  FK (`export.0050`, nullable, `SET_NULL`); the generator now creates one task per
  active `(manager, block)` pair and each resolves independently. Completion now checks
  **Mon–Sat only** — Sunday is not measured, so a blank Sunday cell no longer blocks (explicit
  `0` still counts as filled). `TaskListSerializer` exposes `scope_block` + `scope_block_code`;
  `PlanTaskCard` shows the block code; the plan grid's day-entry mutation invalidates `my-tasks`
  so a filled block's task flips to done on the next board refresh rather than the 60 s poll.
  Cleanup replaced 30 stuck/duplicate legacy null-block tasks (W25+W27 2026) with proper
  per-block tasks. Tests updated for per-block + skip-Sunday semantics.
  (`docs/obsidian/processes/comments-tasks.md` updated.)

### Added
- **Fix Sales Report Phase 1 — backend foundation** (feat(p3) + db). Replaced the static
  `ExpenseCategory` TextChoices field on `SalesReportExpense.category` with an FK to a new
  admin-managed `ExpenseCategory` model (`export_expense_categories` table), so admins can
  add custom expense types without a code release. Wire format: `expenses[].category` is now
  an integer PK (was a string code like `"NDS"`); read response adds `category_code`,
  `category_display` (English label), `logo_code`. The 21 original codes were seeded by
  migration `0049_seed_expense_categories` with names from the i18n JSON (tk/ru/en). New CRUD
  endpoint `GET|POST|PATCH|DELETE /api/v1/export/expense-categories/`; write roles:
  `admin`/`director`/`export_manager`. `Country.currency` added to `core.countries`
  (migration `core.0024`), seeded KZ→`KZT`, RU→`RUB`; the sales-report view now defaults
  `currency` from `shipment.country.currency` on first create, falling back to `'KZT'`.
  The old `ExpenseCategory` enum is renamed `ExpenseCategoryEnum` and kept as a seed reference
  only. Serializer test added: admin-added (non-seed) category accepted without a code release.
  (`docs/obsidian/reference/sales-report-model.md` created; `api-contract.md` updated.)
- **Fix Sales Report Phase 2 — full-page report split into Sale + Processing** (feat(frontend)).
  Replaced the sales-report drawer (for the `SalesRepReports` worklist flow) with a routed
  full page `SalesReportPage` at `/export/sales-reports/:shipmentId`, modelled on the Excel
  "Export Hasabat" sheet. Two tabs over one `SalesReport`: **Sale** (sales rep — line items +
  every active expense category **pre-listed** as a fixed row, blank rows dropped on save) and
  **Processing** (export manager — Kurs/exchange rate, loaded vs. sold weight + the derived
  "Tapawutly" difference, per-block kilograms from `block_sources`, USD totals/net income).
  Currency auto-fills from the shipment's destination country (`Country.currency`, now exposed on
  `CountrySerializer`) and stays editable; `useExpenseCategories` hook + `FixedExpensesTable`
  added. The old `SalesReportDrawer`/`SalesReportPanel` remain for the ShipmentDetail +
  OverdueReports surfaces (now PK-compatible). One save persists both tabs (both panes stay
  mounted; `weight_rejected_kg` derived client-side).
- **Fix Sales Report Phase 3 — expense-template admin screen** (feat(frontend) + feat(p3)).
  New page `ExpenseTemplatePage` (route `/admin/expense-template`) — ProTable CRUD over
  `/export/expense-categories/` to manage categories, their tk/ru/en names, `sort_order`,
  `is_active`, and `logo_code` (the future LOGO-ERP account ref; no sync wired yet). Gated by the
  new **non-admin** pageCode `export.expense_template` (AD-15: admin/director/export_manager
  inherit it, restricted-set roles do not), seeded via `seed_permissions` (15 rows,
  get_or_create — no existing grants touched). `code` is immutable after create; deleting an
  in-use category returns 409 (centralised `ProtectedError` handler) instead of 500;
  `?is_active=` filtering enabled on the viewset.

### Changed
- **Sheet rows scoped to a sales rep's own customers** (feat(p3)). The Shipment Sheet (`GET /api/v1/export/shipments/sheet/`) now filters its `results` to shipments whose `customer.sales_rep` is the requesting user when that user's role is `sales_rep` (and they are not a superuser) — a rep only sees columns for customers assigned to them; shipments with a null customer are excluded; a rep with no assigned customers gets an empty list. The filter also covers the `?shipment=<id>` drawer path, so a rep cannot open an unowned shipment. Management (`admin`/`export_manager`/`director`) and every other operational role (loading/transport/document_team/finansist/etc., who work by status phase not customer) see all rows unchanged; the global config (`rows`/`row_settings`/`users_index`) is identical regardless. Mirrors the ownership rule already used by `/export/shipments/my-sales-reports/`. 3 new tests; full sheet suite (38) green. (`api-contract.md`, `screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated.)

### Added
- **Interactive end-to-end export BPMN** (docs). New self-contained, offline `docs/diagrams/export-process-bpmn.html` mapping the full export value chain across 8 role swimlanes: weekly harvest planning → quota acquisition → contract → cargo/forecast-pool draft (+ two-row join) → assign/firm-splits/quota-sync/contract-link/doc-gen → TM customs → loading → transit & border → destination, sales & report. The 13-step lifecycle (steps 0–12) is rendered as BPMN tasks coloured by phase with the `has_peregruz` exclusive gateway; every node is click-for-detail (role, action, fields with Sheet rows, trigger field, side-effects, source refs) and sub-process modals cover planning, quota, contract bridge (ADR-023), draft join, doc generation, auto-advance cascade and cancel. Source-verified against `apps/export/services/shipment.py` (TRANSITIONS), `seed_task_rules.py` (real per-status trigger fields), the greenhouse planning + quota_sync + harvest_forecast services and the contracts module. Pan/zoom, hover-trace, phase legend. Linked from `docs/obsidian/processes/shipment-lifecycle.md`.

### Fixed
- **sales_rep got 403 when saving a sales report** (fix(p3)). The `sales-report` action self-gates with `PRIVILEGED_ROLES | {'sales_rep'}` in its body, but `ShipmentViewSet.resource_code='shipment'` meant `DynamicResourcePermission` ran first and mapped the request to a `shipment` permission. The frontend always **POSTs** (`useSaveSalesReport`, get-or-create on the backend), and `sales_rep` has `_VE` on `shipment` (view + edit, **no create**), so the POST was rejected as `shipment.can_create=False` with DRF's default `"You do not have permission to perform this action."` before the body's own role gate ran — yet the report's true resource is `sales_report`, where `sales_rep` does hold create rights. `get_permissions` now returns `[IsAuthenticated()]` for `set_sales_report`, making the body's role gate the sole authority (granting `shipment.can_create` was rejected — it would also unlock whole-shipment POST). New `TestSalesReportPostPermission` (3 tests: rep POST→200, pre-departure→400, unprivileged role→403); 28 sales_rep tests green.
- **Transport couldn't edit the "Ýol güni we temperatura" (transit-days + temperature) Sheet cell** (fix(p3)). This is the virtual row `transit_days_temp` (R26) — one input editing two real fields (`transit_days` + `transport_temp_c`). The inline edit gate `can_edit_sheet_field` delegates the virtual key to the real `transit_days` field perm, but the batch `get_sheet_edit_map` (which feeds the frontend's `can_current_user_edit`, the flag that enables/disables the cell) did **not** delegate — it field-perm-checked the virtual key `transit_days_temp`, which no role holds in `RoleFieldPermission`, so it always returned `False`. Result: transport saw the cell as read-only even though a PATCH would have succeeded. Hoisted `_VIRTUAL_FIELD_DELEGATES` to module scope and made `get_sheet_edit_map` delegate virtual keys exactly like `can_edit_sheet_field`, so the display gate and the write gate can never disagree. New `TestVirtualFieldDelegation` (3 tests); 67 sheet-perm/sheet tests green.
- **loading_dept_head (Soltanmyrat) couldn't edit the "Ýyladyşhanadan çykdy" (greenhouse-departure) Sheet cell** (fix(p3)). `can_edit_sheet_field` AND-composes the SheetRowSetting trigger gate with the per-role `RoleFieldPermission` allowlist. The `departed_at` row's `role_triggers` already named `loading_dept_head`/`loading_dept_head_deputy`, but neither role was ever granted the `departed_at` field permission — only `warehouse_chief` (his deputies) had it. Migration 0013's docstring claimed the head should match the deputies' fields, but `departed_at` was the one field that diverged. New migration `0026_grant_departed_at_loading_dept_head` grants `departed_at` to `loading_dept_head` and `loading_dept_head_deputy` (idempotent, clears the perm cache); `seed_permissions` updated so a `--reset` keeps it. Both gates now pass for Soltanmyrat and his deputies.
- **Sheet drag-to-reorder columns left a column rendered twice until refresh** (fix(frontend)). Reordering shipment columns by dragging a header sometimes left the dragged column displayed in two places; a page refresh cleared it. Cause: the column headers are virtualized (only the visible+overscan headers are mounted), but `SortableHeaderWrapper` applied dnd-kit's per-item sort `transform` on top of the virtualizer's absolute `left`. `horizontalListSortingStrategy` assumes *every* sortable item is mounted, so its "make room" transforms collided with the virtualizer's positioning and left a stale, displaced header behind on drop (cleared only by remount/refresh). The data itself was never duplicated (`filtered` and the backend both dedupe by id) — it was a render artifact. Fix: stop applying any dnd-kit `transform`/`transition` to in-list headers (the virtualizer owns their position) and render the dragged column via a `<DragOverlay>` clone instead, with the source header fading in place — the canonical dnd-kit + virtualization pattern. Trade-off: sibling columns no longer animate to "make room" mid-drag (the floating clone + drop is the feedback).
- **Task-modal field edits reverted as you moved to the next field** (fix(frontend)). In the board task modals (Self board + Shipment board), filling a driver field (or any sheet-backed target field) made the previous field's value "disappear" the moment its editor closed.
