The drawer renders from the single-shipment sheet cache `['shipments','sheet','row',id]`, but `useShipmentPatch`'s `applyOptimistic` / `reconcileFromServer` / `rollback` only ever touched the full-season `['shipments','sheet']` key (and `invalidateExceptSheet` deliberately skips every `sheet` key), so a saved cell was never written back to the cache the drawer reads — `getCellValue` re-read the stale empty value on close. The three cache helpers now also patch the single-shipment row cache (guarded so no phantom entry is created when it isn't mounted). 4 new regression tests (14 total in `useShipmentPatch.test.ts`).
- **Newest tasks silently dropped off the SelfBoard once a role's backlog exceeded 200** (fix(p3)). `/me/tasks/` used `StandardPagination` (max 200) and the board fetched a single `?page_size=200` page, but the board renders ALL of a user's tasks (active columns + done-today + history) from that one fetch. With the list ordered by `deadline ASC` and done-task history counted in, a role past ~200 tasks (e.g. transport at 309) had its freshest tasks — the ones just created and needing action — sort past the cutoff and never reach the board (real case: assign-driver task for `29JN122/26` ranked 307/309). New `TaskBoardPagination` (`page_size=1000`, `max_page_size=2000`) on `/me/tasks/`; the `useMyTasks` hook now requests `?page_size=1000`. Other list endpoints keep the 200 cap. 46 task-API tests green.
- **Task board listed tasks on soft-deleted shipments** (fix(p3)). The `/me/tasks/` (SelfBoard) and `/api/v1/export/tasks/` list endpoints never excluded tasks whose parent shipment had been soft-deleted (`deleted_at IS NOT NULL`), so the task board showed work items for shipments that no longer exist — and their target-field editors were dead (the sheet endpoint already hides deleted shipments). Both querysets now filter `Q(shipment__isnull=True) | Q(shipment__deleted_at__isnull=True)`, keeping non-shipment plan tasks (weekly_plan / local_sell_plan) while dropping deleted-shipment tasks. The Shipment Kanban `board` action already scoped to live shipments, so it was unaffected. 46 task-API tests green.
- **Contract sale's shipment code was always null** (fix(p4)). `ContractSaleListSerializer.get_shipment_code` read `getattr(shipment, 'code', None)`, but the model attribute is `shipment_code` (it only maps to db_column `code`) — so it always returned `None`. The contract **Sales** tab's `if (record.shipment && record.shipment_code)` therefore never rendered the shipment code/link even for linked sales (showed `—`). Now returns `obj.shipment.shipment_code`; verified end-to-end (API returns the code, the Sales tab renders it as a link to `/shipments/{id}`). 20 sale-API tests green.

### Added
- **`import_2sales_june_sales` command — June sales → existing-shipment bridge (Slice 3, scoped)** (feat(p4)). Imports June-2026 rows from the `2-Sales` sheet as `ContractSale` bridges to **existing** shipments (does not create shipments). Matches each row's `(truck_plate + date)` to a shipment within a ±21-day window (the invoice date lags loading by 1-2 weeks), normalising the plate (upper, no spaces); resolves the contract by the canonical `(export_firm, contract_year, seq)` identity parsed from the row's contract string (links if present, else creates a one-time contract with that number); ensures the firm split exists; then `update_or_create`s the `(shipment, export_firm)` bridge carrying the row's invoice number/date, weight and amount. Dry-run by default, `--commit` to persist, idempotent, skipped-row CSV. Scope note: of 73 June rows, only those whose truck **and** buyer exist in the DB link (the rest are logged); on the current DB that was 12 — the remaining trucks/buyers must be loaded first.

### Changed
- **Shipment firm-split contracts in the Sheet — UI (Slice 4b of ADR-023)** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). A **dedicated "Contracts" row** in the export Sheet (new `firm_contracts` row in `DEFAULT_SHEET_ROWS`, `readonly`/synthetic — no backing shipment column). Each shipment's cell shows a contracts icon that opens a **popover** (`ShipmentFirmContractsPanel`): per firm split it shows the linked contract (number + framework/one-time tag) or — when unlinked — the (firm, buyer) pair's framework contracts in a `Select` with a **Link** button **plus** a **Create one-time** button (always shown), and a $10K bank/cash tag. Contracts are never auto-created — the operator links a framework or creates a one-time per firm. Backed by `useShipmentFirmContracts` (GET) / `useLinkFirmContract` (POST → invalidates the panel query + `['shipments','sheet']`) against the Slice-4a endpoint; a "set the buyer first" hint shows when the shipment has no import firm; cell shows `—` until firms are added. `SheetCell` special-cases the field (popover, no cell editor → no auto-close issue); `getCellValue` returns empty for it. New `sheet.row.firm_contracts` label + `sheet.firm_contracts.*` i18n × tk/ru/en; `IShipmentFirmContract*` types. 32 sheet tests green; frontend type-check + lint clean.
- **Shipment firm-split ↔ contract bridge — backend (Slice 4a of ADR-023)** (feat(p4) + db). The contracts-side machinery to link each of a shipment's firm splits to a contract. `ContractSale.invoice_number`/`invoice_date` made **nullable** (a bridge sale is created before a person fills the invoice at document time); `unique_together (contract, invoice_number)` replaced with a **filtered** `UniqueConstraint` (so the many NULL-invoice bridge rows coexist on MSSQL), plus a new filtered `UniqueConstraint (shipment, export_firm)` = the one-sale-per-truck-firm bridge identity (migration `contracts.0009`). New service `services/shipment_firm_contracts.py`: `framework_contracts_for_pair` (active framework contracts of a seller↔buyer pair), `link_split_to_contract` (mode `framework` → link to a chosen contract; mode `one_time` → auto-create a one-time contract via `next_contract_no`, no passport — then `update_or_create` the `ContractSale` bridge by `(shipment, export_firm)`, copying the split's weight→`quantity_kg` and amount→`total_usd`, invoice left NULL), and a `$10K` `money_warning` (bank/cash, non-blocking). Endpoint `GET|POST /api/v1/contracts/shipment-firm-contracts/` (gated by the `sale` resource): GET lists each split with its framework options + currently-linked contract + money hint; POST links/creates. Architecture: this lives in `contracts` (which may read `export`) — the export firm-split code never calls contracts (dependency direction). 9 new tests + full contracts suite (85) green on MSSQL; `makemigrations --check` clean. Frontend Sheet UI (show framework / "create one-time" per firm) is Slice 4b.
- **Contract type (framework / one-time) + deal-passport on the contract (Slice 2 of ADR-023)** (feat(p4) + db). Formalized the long-existing `Contract.contract_type` free-text field into choices **`FRAMEWORK`** (multi-use, has deal-passport + quota) vs **`ONE_TIME`** (ad-hoc), renaming the legacy DB value `'EXPORT'` → `'FRAMEWORK'` (migration `contracts.0008`, `RunPython` data update + `import_contracts_master` updated to write `FRAMEWORK`). Added `Contract.passport_sdelka` (the bank deal-passport is a property of the framework contract, not of each truck sale) and backfilled it from any non-empty `ContractSale.passport_sdelka` (`bulk_update`, batch 500); `ContractSale.passport_sdelka` stays for the per-sale document value. Both fields are exposed on the contract list/detail/create serializers (`contract_type` keeps its model `choices` validation). Frontend: the create modal's contract-type input became a **Select** (Framework / One-time, default Framework) and gained a `passport_sdelka` field (with a "framework only" hint); the contract detail page shows type + passport; `contracts.type.{framework,one_time}` + `contracts.create.field.passport_sdelka`/`_hint` i18n × tk/ru/en; `IContract.contract_type`/`passport_sdelka` types. Migration applied on MSSQL (43/43 → FRAMEWORK, 0 legacy EXPORT). 3 new tests + full contracts suite (73) green; `makemigrations --check` + frontend type-check/lint clean. one-time contracts will be **auto-created** when a firm-split's (seller, buyer) pair has no framework contract — Slice 4.
- **Contract auto-numbering, per-seller / per-year (Slice 1 of ADR-023)** (feat(p4) + db). `Contract` gains `seq` + `contract_year` int fields and a **filtered** `UniqueConstraint(export_firm, contract_year, seq)` (filtered so the many non-standard numbers with NULL seq/year coexist — MSSQL otherwise allows a single NULL). New `apps/contracts/services/contract_number.py`: `parse_contract_number` / `next_contract_seq` / `build_contract_number` / `next_contract_no` — the counter is per **seller** (export firm) per **calendar year**, NOT per buyer-pair (confirmed against the 1-Contracts sheet: a seller with two buyers keeps one continuous sequence; the buyer is not in the `{seq}/{YY}-{SELLER}-EXP, {DD.MM.YYYY}` string). `ContractCreateSerializer.contract_number` is now optional: blank → auto-generated inside `transaction.atomic()` (filtered unique index = race backstop); supplied → kept verbatim with seq/year parsed out; the model `UniqueValidator` is preserved via `extra_kwargs` so a duplicate supplied number still 400s. Migration `contracts.0007` adds the fields, backfills seq/year from existing `contract_number` (regex parse, `bulk_update(batch_size=500)`, non-standard `-P`/`TAT-`/legacy left NULL), then adds the constraint — applied on MSSQL, all 43 live contracts backfilled, 0 `(firm, year, seq)` collisions (read-only pre-check). 11 new tests + full contracts suite (73) green; `makemigrations --check` clean. Frontend: the contract-create modal's `contract_number` field is no longer required — left blank it shows an "auto-generated per seller/year" hint and the field is omitted from the payload (`contracts.create.field.contract_number_placeholder`/`_hint` i18n × tk/ru/en); `IContractCreatePayload.contract_number` is now optional. The **season** field was also removed from the create modal — a contract is always for the current season, so the serializer defaults `season` to the active `Season` server-side (`season` now optional in the create payload + serializer `extra_kwargs`). Auto-numbering is the primitive for the Add-shipment flow (Slice 4); `Contract.kind` framework/one_time is Slice 2.
- **Terminology + ADR-023: a "2-Sales" row is a FirmSplit, not a truck** (docs). Docs/comments-only (no migration, no behaviour, no data change). Fixed the misleading "one truck dispatched" framing in `contracts.ContractSale` (module + class docstrings + `shipment` FK comment), `export.ShipmentFirmSplit` docstring, and the `import_invoices_from_2sales` docstring — a 2-Sales row is **one export firm's share of a truck** (1 truck → 1..3 rows, split under the $10K rule). Added **ADR-023** recording the `Shipment → FirmSplit (1-3) → Contract` hierarchy, the bridge invariant `(shipment, export_firm)` between `ShipmentFirmSplit` ↔ `ContractSale`, the two distinct identifiers (`shipment_code` = `DDMM+seq/YY` vs `contract_no` = `seq/YY-FIRM-EXP`), and the chosen path B (bridge, not merge); Slices 1-5 deferred. (`docs/obsidian/reference/contracts-contract-sale-model.md` updated.)
- **Renamed the P4 "Invoice" record to "Contract Sale"** (refactor(p4) + refactor(frontend) + db). The model name `Invoice` collided with the actual *invoice document* the platform generates (the `invoice_ru`/`invoice_en` templates), which made the contracts UI confusing. The **record** is now `ContractSale` everywhere; the generated **document** stays "Invoice" (unchanged). Backend: model `Invoice`→`ContractSale`, table physically renamed `contracts_invoice`→`contracts_contract_sale` via a hand-authored **`RenameModel` + `AlterModelTable`** migration (`contracts.0004`, `sp_rename` — **data-preserving, not delete+create**, so the ~1,961 live rows on beta survive); reverse accessors `*.invoices`→`*.sales` (`contracts.0005`, state-only); serializers `Invoice{List,Detail,Create}Serializer`→`ContractSale{…}Serializer`, `InvoiceViewSet`→`ContractSaleViewSet`, route `/api/v1/contracts/invoices/`→`/api/v1/contracts/sales/` (incl. the `…/sales/{id}/document/` action). Permission rename: resource_code `invoice`→`sale`, page code `contracts.invoices`→`contracts.sales`, with a guarded/idempotent data migration (`contracts.0006`) updating existing `RoleResourcePermission`/`RolePagePermission` rows (verified on the live DB: 4 resource + 15 page rows migrated, zero stragglers). **Kept** the fields `invoice_number`/`invoice_date`/`last_invoice_number` (they name the invoice document's number/date) and all doc-gen (`build_invoice_context`, `SCOPE_INVOICE`, `InvoiceDocumentsButton`). Frontend: type `IInvoice`→`IContractSale`, hook `useInvoices`→`useContractSales` (query keys `['invoices']`→`['sales']`), pages `InvoicesTab`→`ContractSalesTab` / `InvoiceCreate`→`ContractSaleCreate` / `pages/invoices/InvoiceList`→`pages/sales/ContractSaleList`, route `/invoices`→`/sales`, nav + tab labels and the `sales.*`/`sales_list.*`/`nav.sales.*`/`contracts.detail.tab.sales` i18n × tk/ru/en. The shipment firm-split's unrelated `invoice_number` field is untouched. 62 contracts tests green on MSSQL; frontend type-check clean; `makemigrations --check` clean across all apps. (`docs/obsidian/reference/contracts-contract-sale-model.md`, `screens/contract-sale-list.md`, `screens/contract-detail.md`, `processes/document-generation.md`, `processes/permissions-system.md`, `00-index.md` updated.)

### Added
- **Inline editing on the firm detail pages (no more edit drawer)** (feat(frontend)). Editing an existing Export or Import firm no longer opens a drawer — every field is editable in place in the detail view. New reusable `InlineEdit` component: a value renders as text with a faint edit icon; clicking turns it into an `Input` (or `TextArea` for multiline addresses/bank details), committing on blur/Enter and discarding on Escape, and only PATCHes when the trimmed value actually changed. Each field saves itself via a single-field partial PATCH (`useUpdateFirm` / `useUpdateImportFirm`). Structured fields get inline controls too: Active and Gapy-Satyş type as a `Switch`/`Select`, and (import firms) Country/City as the self-fetching `CountrySelect`/`CitySelect` — changing country clears city in the **same** PATCH so a stale city can't persist. The drawer + full form is kept **only for creating** a new firm; the header "Edit" button is gone (Delete stays). Read-only for users without edit permission. New `common.click_to_edit` i18n key × tk/ru/en. Type-check + lint clean.
- **Smart search on the Export & Import firm admin lists** (feat(frontend)). Both firm lists had sortable columns but no search box. Added a toolbar search (ProTable `toolbar.search`, filters as you type) that matches across **code, short name, and full name(s)** — export firms search `code`/`name_short`/`name_tk`/`name_ru`/`name_en`, import firms search `code`/`name_short`/`name_company`. Matching is diacritic- and punctuation-insensitive via the shared `normalizeSearch`/`buildSearchBlob` utils (same forgiving search used by the firm `Select`s), so "ygt", "YGT H.J." and "ygt hj" all hit. Import-firm search runs within the active Our/Gapy Satyş tab (tab counts stay full). Reuses the existing `common.search` i18n key. Type-check + lint clean.
- **Country filter on the Import Firms list** (feat(frontend)). A toolbar `Select` (searchable, clearable) narrows the list to one country. Options are derived from the firms actually present (unique, alpha-sorted by name) so only countries with at least one firm appear; the filter composes with both the search box and the active Our/Gapy Satyş tab. New `import_firms_admin.filter_country` i18n key × tk/ru/en. Type-check + lint clean.
- **Contract document attachments (PDF upload / inline preview / delete) — P4** (feat(p4) + feat(frontend) + db). Contracts can now carry their signed contract scans/annexes as downloadable PDFs. New `contracts.ContractAttachment` model (`contracts_contract_attachment`, migration `0003`) mirroring the proven `feedback.FeedbackAttachment` pattern — `FileField(upload_to='contracts/%Y/%m/')` + `original_filename`/`mime_type`/`size_bytes`/`uploaded_by`/`uploaded_at`, `contract` FK `CASCADE`. **PDF-only** validation in a new `contracts/services/files.py::validate_contract_document` (≤20 MB, `.pdf` extension, `%PDF-` magic bytes; max 20 per contract). API: three `@action`s on `ContractViewSet` — `POST …/contracts/{id}/attachments/` (multipart `files`, validates all before persisting, `can_create`-gated), `DELETE …/attachments/{att_id}/` (`can_delete`), and `GET …/attachments/{att_id}/download/` (`can_view`). Unlike feedback's direct `/media/` URL, downloads stream through the **authenticated** endpoint (`FileResponse`, `Content-Disposition: inline` so the browser previews the PDF) — contract documents are legal/financial records and must stay behind auth. Detail response nests read-only `attachments[]` metadata (no file URL); the queryset gains `prefetch_related('attachments__uploaded_by')` on retrieve only. The status/FK filter in `ContractViewSet.get_queryset` is now **scoped to the `list` action** — detail + attachment actions resolve by pk across every status, so documents stay reachable after a contract is completed/closed (when they're most needed — settlement/disputes). Frontend: new **Documents** tab on the Contract detail page (`DocumentsTab`) with an antd `Upload` (PDF accept) + a file list with inline View (opens the authed download URL in a new tab — the same-origin httpOnly cookie rides along) and Delete. Write controls gate on the user's **resource** permissions (`resource_permissions.contract.create`/`.delete` — contract has no field-level perms, so `editable_fields` is empty for all roles), with the backend enforcing the same. `useUploadContractAttachments`/`useDeleteContractAttachment`/`contractAttachmentUrl` hooks, `IContractAttachment` type, `contracts.detail.tab.documents` + `contracts.documents.*` i18n × tk/ru/en. 8 new backend tests (upload 201 + nested-in-detail, non-PDF magic-byte 400, no-files 400, inline download, download-on-completed-contract, delete 204, read-only role 403) — full contracts suite (62) green on MSSQL; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/contract-detail.md` + `docs/obsidian/reference/contracts-contract-model.md` updated.)
- **Export firm "Short Name" field, separate from the document "Code"** (feat(core) + feat(frontend) + db). Export and documents use different short labels for the same firm: documents key off the firm `code`, but the shipment list/sheet should show a separate operator-facing short name. `core.ExportFirm` gains a nullable `name_short` (CharField, Cyrillic collation), mirroring `ImportFirm.name_short`; migration `core.0023` adds it and **seeds it from `code`** for all existing firms (`bulk_update`, batch 500). Exposed as a writable field on `ExportFirmSerializer` (admin CRUD) and read-only on `ExportFirmReferenceSerializer`. The shipment **display** now reads `name_short` (falling back to `code` when blank): `ShipmentListSerializer.export_firms_display` (comma-joined) and the sheet firm-chip `SheetFirmSplitInlineSerializer.firm_code`; documents keep using `code`. Frontend: new "Short Name" field on the Export Firm detail/edit page + the OptionLists quick-create, `IExportFirm.name_short` type, `firms_admin.name_short` i18n × tk/ru/en; the existing `code` field (which had been mislabelled "Short Name") was relabelled to **"Code" / "Код" / "Kody"** (label only — column unchanged). 32 shipment-sheet tests green on MSSQL; type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-list-vs-sheet.md` + api-contract updated.)
- **Export/Import firm bank details now render multi-line** (fix(frontend)). Bank details are entered in a multi-line textarea but the read-only `Descriptions` view collapsed the newlines into spaces (HTML whitespace collapsing). Wrapped the values in `white-space: pre-line` on both the Export and Import firm detail pages so each bank line shows on its own row.
- **Move an import firm between "Our" and "Gapy Satyş" from the list** (feat(frontend)). The Import Firms admin list splits firms into "Our Firms" vs "Gapy Satyş" tabs by `is_gapy_satys`, but changing a firm's type previously required opening its detail page and editing in the drawer. Added an inline action column (edit-permission gated) with a confirm-guarded button that PATCHes `is_gapy_satys` to the opposite value via the existing `useUpdateImportFirm` hook — the firm moves to the other tab on success (query invalidation). New `import_firms_admin.{actions,move_to_gapy,move_to_our,confirm_move}` i18n keys × tk/ru/en. Type-check clean.
- **Sales Rep Reports page + customer-based ownership** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). A dedicated worklist so a `sales_rep` enters sales reports for only *his* shipments, scoped by **customer** — a rep handles specific buyers and sees only their shipments (the platform had no per-shipment sales-rep owner; `vehicle_responsible` is free text and rep "work" was only phase-scoped). Ownership is one rep per customer: `core.Customer` gains a nullable `sales_rep` FK → `core.User` (migration `core.0022`), surfaced as writable `sales_rep` + read-only `sales_rep_name` on `CustomerAdminSerializer` (with `validate_sales_rep` rejecting non-sales_rep users). `SalesRepCoverageViewSet` — `GET /api/v1/export/sales-rep-coverage/` (every sales_rep user + their `customer_ids`) and `PUT …/{user_id}/` (replace-all in `transaction.atomic()`: assigns listed customers to the rep and clears the ones he dropped; validates target is a sales_rep + customers exist); gated to superuser/PRIVILEGED_ROLES. New `my_sales_reports` action on `ShipmentViewSet` (`GET …/shipments/my-sales-reports/?needs_report=`) returns step-4+ (departed) non-deleted shipments filtered to `customer__sales_rep=request.user` — management roles bypass and see all (oversight), a rep with no customers gets an empty list; annotates `has_sales_report` via the existing `Exists` pattern, N+1-safe `select_related('customer')`. Page codes: `export.sales_reports` (sales_rep + management) and `export.sales_rep_coverage` (admin/director/export_manager — deliberately a non-`admin.` prefix so management roles inherit it without breaching AD-15). Frontend: `SalesRepReports` page (`/export/my-reports`) with two tabs — **Needs report** (default) then **All my shipments** (report-status column) — opening the shared `SalesReportDrawer` (extracted from `OverdueReportDrawer`, now a thin re-export shim so the Overdue page is unchanged); the rep↔customer link is managed in **both** the Customers admin page (a Sales Rep field per customer) and the `SalesRepCoveragePage` grid (customer multi-select per rep); hooks `useMySalesReports` / `useSalesRepCoverage`; nav + `ROUTE_PAGE_MAP` + `ProtectedRoute` wiring; `sales_reports.*` / `sales_rep_coverage.*` / `customers_admin.*` / `nav.*` i18n × tk/ru/en. 25 backend tests (coverage replace-all incl. reassign-away, customer scoping, `needs_report` toggle, management bypass, no-customer empty, gate 403, non-sales-rep target 400) pass on MSSQL; AD-15 invariant test green; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/sales-report.md` + `docs/obsidian/reference/api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **Audit Log: filter by user** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). The audit table had `model_name`/`action`/`object_id` filters but no way to see "everything user X did". Backend: `AuditLogViewSet.get_queryset` now honours `?user=<id>` (filters `user_id`, mirrors the existing `object_id` int-coercion guard). Frontend: a new reusable self-fetching `UserSelect` (search-driven `Select`, emits the primitive user id) backed by the **mentionable endpoint** — deliberately not the admin user-CRUD list, which is admin/EM-only, so `director` (an `AUDIT_VIEWERS` role that can reach the page) can also populate it. Added to `AuditLogPage` filter row + cleared by "Clear"; `IAuditLogFilters.user` + `?user=` wired through `useAuditLog`. New `audit_log.filter_user` / `placeholder_user` i18n keys × tk/ru/en. Type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/reference/api-endpoint-map.md` updated.)
- **Export firms column on the Shipment List** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). The ShipmentList column manager had no export-firm option because a shipment can be split across several firms (the data lives in the nested `firm_splits[]` junction, excluded from the flat list). Added a flattened scalar `export_firms_display` to `ShipmentListSerializer` (and inherited by `ShipmentDetailSerializer`) — a comma-joined string of export firm codes (e.g. `"YGT, HJ"`, null when no splits). The list/overdue querysets gain `prefetch_related('firm_splits__export_firm')` (one extra query per page, not per row — N+1-safe). Frontend: new opt-in (hidden-by-default) column with `shipments.export_firms` i18n key × tk/ru/en, `IShipmentListItem.export_firms_display` type, and mock updates. Type-check clean.
- **CT-1 / phytosanitary / customs request letters — P4** (feat(p4) + feat(frontend)). Three more documents on the generation framework: the CT-1 certificate-of-origin request (`ct1_ru`), the phytosanitary certificate request (`fito_ru`), and the customs-clearance ARZA letter (`customs_tk`, Turkmen) — each **single-language** per its real source form. Short authority letters: static addressee/body boilerplate baked into each `.docx`, only named fields injected. New builders `build_ct1_context` / `build_fito_context` / `build_customs_context` (`services/document_context.py`); `fito`/`customs` resolve the destination country via a new `_country_name` helper (shipment.country → buyer-firm.country fallback, reads `Country.name_{ru,en,tk}`), while CT-1 needs only firm + contract so it fills even without a shipment link. `_contract_line` extracted and reused (DRY with the invoice builder). Three registry keys + templates (built by `build_templates.py`); reuse the existing `InvoiceViewSet.document` endpoint (`?type=ct1_ru|fito_ru|customs_tk&fmt=docx|pdf`). Frontend: the `InvoiceDocumentsButton` menu restructured to per-document-family groups with per-variant languages (so single-language letters show only their language), adding the three letters alongside Invoice/CMR; new `documents.ct1`/`fito`/`customs` i18n keys × tk/ru/en. 6 new backend tests (builders incl. country resolution + no-shipment fallback, render smoke, endpoint) — **21 doc-gen + 48 contracts suite green** on MSSQL; frontend type-check + lint clean. RU/TK docx + PDF samples in `docs/sample_documents/`. (`docs/obsidian/processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **"Documents" generate control on invoices** (feat(frontend)). Makes the P4 document generators usable from the UI: a per-invoice **Documents** dropdown (new `InvoiceDocumentsButton`) added to the action column on both the contract Faktura tab (`InvoicesTab`) and the all-invoices list (`InvoiceList`). The menu is grouped by document family (Invoice / CMR) with Word + PDF in RU/EN (8 entries), each hitting `GET /api/v1/contracts/invoices/{id}/document/?type=&fmt=` and downloading via a new shared `utils/fileDownload.ts::downloadUrl()` (anchor click — the httpOnly auth cookie rides the same-origin GET, same mechanism as the Boss report exports). New `documents.*` i18n keys × tk/ru/en. Type-check + lint clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **Customs/Document Cash-Advance Ledger — money-out side** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db). Implements the `data/avans.xlsx` cash ledger: the document handler receives periodic float top-ups (already modelled as `FinansistAdvance`, the money-**in** side) and spends them on per-shipment customs clearance and batch document fees — the money-**out** side that had no model. New `CustomsExpense` model (`export_customs_expenses`) mirrors the proven `SalesReportExpense` pattern: a 13-code `CustomsExpenseCategory` enum (`GUMRUKLEME`, `KARANTIN`, `CT1`, `FITO`, `ANALIZ`, `PASPORT_SDELKA`, `PLATYOSKA`, `DOC_POST`, `YUZLENME_HAT`, `GUMRUK_AMAL`, `BORDER_RETURN`, `SERTNAMA`, `OTHER`), `amount` (Decimal, default currency **TMT** — manat, not USD), an **optional** `shipment` FK (per-truck fees link; batch fees like "19 trucks quarantine" leave it null and use `quantity`), and verbatim `shipment_code_raw`/`vehicle_plate`/`route_label`/`label_raw` for source/audit fidelity (shipment code Latin-only — no Cyrillic collation, so it can join `Shipment.shipment_code` without an MSSQL collation conflict). API: full CRUD `GET|POST|PATCH|DELETE /api/v1/export/customs-expenses/` (writes gated to `CUSTOMS_EXPENSE_WRITE` = finansist/admin/director/document_team/export_manager + superuser via inline role checks — no `DynamicResourcePermission`/unseeded-rows dependency; reads open to any authenticated user; `?category=&shipment=&currency=&date_from=&date_to=&search=` filters) plus a `GET .../ledger/` summary (DB-aggregated `advances_total` − `expenses_total` = `balance`, `by_category`, `by_date`). The ledger **scopes both sides to one currency** (`?currency=`, default `TMT`) because advances default to USD and mixing units would be meaningless. Shipment detail nests read-only `customs_expenses[]` (prefetched, N+1-safe). Frontend: the `/export/advances` page (AdvancesTracker) is restructured into Tabs — Advances (existing) + a new **Customs expenses** tab with a ledger summary header (in/out/balance tiles + by-category breakdown, date-range filter) and a CRUD ProTable; ShipmentDetail gains a "Customs / Document expenses" section with an add-expense modal pre-filled for that shipment. Writer-role gating mirrors the backend (single shared constant). Migrations `0038` (+`0040` collation fix) applied on MSSQL; **20 backend tests** green (CRUD, role 403s, ledger aggregation, currency-scoping, amount>0, nested-on-detail); frontend type-check + lint clean; full tk/ru/en i18n. (`docs/obsidian/processes/customs-expense-ledger.md` added.)
- **Shipment Board cards: click the card → task modal → act on the task in a drawer (in-board, no detail navigation)** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). Kanban cards on the Shipment Board (`/export/shipments/board`) previously showed only an aggregate count ("0/5") + a progress bar and click-navigated to the shipment detail page — no way to see *what* the tasks were or do them on the board. Now **clicking a card opens a `BoardTasksModal`** listing every task on that shipment (state icon + tag, localized title + assignee role, deadline with overdue-in-red); clicking a task row opens the **shared `SelfBoardTaskDrawer`** — the exact same "do the task" surface used on the Self Kanban (`/me/board`) — so operators start the task, fill its target fields, and mark it done **in place, without leaving the board**. The card no longer navigates to detail; the full detail page is reachable via an explicit **"Open shipment detail →"** link in the modal. The modal and drawer **never overlap** (the modal is hidden while the drawer is open, keeping its shipment id, and reappears on close) — deliberately avoiding an interactive Drawer stacked over an antd Modal, whose focus trap would break the drawer's inputs. On close the modal list reflects the new task state because the page re-resolves the modal's item from live data and `useTaskActions` now also invalidates `['shipments','board']`. Backend: `BoardItemSerializer` returns a per-card `tasks[]` array — the **full `TaskListSerializer` shape (`ITaskListItem`)** so the drawer can consume it directly — read from the board's existing `tasks` prefetch (upgraded with `select_related('shipment__status','assignee_user')`) so it stays **N+1-safe and within the `assertNumQueries ≤ 8` bound**. Tasks sort active-first (open/in_progress/blocked), then by deadline (nulls last), then oldest-created. 36/36 `tests_shipment_board` green on MSSQL (2 new: full-task-shape + active-first ordering). New i18n keys `shipment_board.no_tasks` / `shipment_board.open_detail` × tk/ru/en. Type-check + lint clean.
- **CMR document generator (RU + EN, base single-firm) — P4** (feat(p4)). Second document on the contract document-generation framework: a road consignment note (CMR). Because one `Invoice` == one dispatched truck, CMR is `scope=invoice` and reads transport/cargo from `invoice.shipment` (graceful blanks when the Slice-B link is absent), keeping generation inside `contracts` (which may import `export`; an `export`-side action could not import the contracts renderer — wrong dependency direction). New builder `build_cmr_context` (`services/document_context.py`) — firms by language, localized cargo/packing/country-of-dispatch, single-invoice ref, vehicle/driver transport line, and `gross_with_pallet = weight_gross + pallet_weight`. Two registry keys `cmr_ru`/`cmr_en` + `cmr_ru.docx`/`cmr_en.docx` templates (built by `build_templates.py`); reuses the **existing** `InvoiceViewSet.document` endpoint — `?type=cmr_ru|cmr_en&fmt=docx|pdf` — with **zero** new plumbing (the action dispatches by registry key). **v1 scope is base single-firm**; deferred follow-ons (each a new registry entry, no framework change): the 2-/3-seller firm-split CMRs that aggregate multiple invoices onto one truck, the official 24-box CMR form (current template is a simplified labelled layout with the same Jinja field names so the business can re-skin it), and `route`/`place_loading`/`forwarder`/`tir_carnet` until those sources are mapped. 5 new tests (RU/EN builder incl. gross-with-pallet math + no-shipment blanks, RU/EN render smoke, endpoint `?type=cmr_ru`) — **15 doc-gen tests + 43 contracts suite green** on MSSQL. RU/EN docx + PDF samples in `docs/sample_documents/`. (`docs/obsidian/processes/document-generation.md` updated.)
- **Invoice document generator (RU + EN .docx) — P4 Slice 1** (feat(p4)). First cut of the contract document-generation framework that replaces the manual fill of export documents from the `Export_contracts` master sheet. A document-agnostic core: a **template registry** (`apps/contracts/document_templates/registry.py`, a plain Python dict — not a DB model — keyed by stable document keys `invoice_ru`/`invoice_en`, mapping to a `.docx` template, scope, language, context-builder, and download-filename pattern); authored **`.docx` templates** with Jinja tags via `docxtpl` (built by `document_templates/build_templates.py`, language-specific static labels baked in, only data values tagged); a **pure context builder** (`services/document_context.py::build_invoice_context(invoice, lang)` — object in, dict out; owns `DD.MM.YYYY` dates, money/price/kg formatting, ru→en→tk firm-language fallback, and shipment-vs-invoice fallback for weights/transport/pallets); and a generic **render service** (`services/document_render.py`) with `render_docx` (docxtpl→bytes) and a pluggable `render_pdf` (LibreOffice headless, unique per-call profile to avoid the shared-lock; raises a clear `DocumentRenderError` when the binary is absent — the .docx path has no such dep). Exposed as `GET /api/v1/contracts/invoices/{id}/document/?type=invoice_ru|invoice_en&fmt=docx|pdf` (a thin `@action` on `InvoiceViewSet`; gated by the existing `invoice` resource permission; returns the file as an attachment, **`fmt` not `format`** because `format` is reserved by DRF content negotiation; 400 on unknown type, 503 when PDF requested without LibreOffice). The RU/EN templates reproduce the real `InvoiceRU`/`InvoiceEN` layout (seller/buyer block with tri-lingual firm name/address/bank, country-of-origin, delivery terms, transport, the №/product/HS-code/pieces/packing/gross/net/price/total line table, ИТОГО total, and the pallet note). New dep **`docxtpl>=0.19,<0.20`** in `requirements.txt`; **LibreOffice headless** for PDF is provisioned in `backend/Dockerfile` (`libreoffice-writer` + `fonts-dejavu`/`fonts-liberation`/`fonts-noto-core` for Cyrillic glyphs) so it works in the deployed container regardless of host OS, with an env-driven `LIBREOFFICE_BIN` setting for dev boxes (e.g. Windows `…\soffice.exe`). **PDF verified end-to-end** against real LibreOffice 26.2 — RU + EN sample PDFs render with correct Cyrillic (no tofu) and locale number formatting. 10 new tests (pure builder formatting/language/fallback, RU+EN render smoke with no-leftover-tag assertions, and the API endpoint incl. 400/503 paths) — full contracts suite (43) green on MSSQL. (`docs/obsidian/processes/document-generation.md` added.)
- **Rich Sales Report backend** (feat(p3)). Extended `SalesReport` with 6 new header fields (`currency`, `exchange_rate`, `weight_loaded_kg`, `total_sales_local`, `total_expenses_local`, `net_income_local`). Added two child models: `SalesReportLineItem` (qty × price tiers, up to 14 rows) and `SalesReportExpense` (21-code `ExpenseCategory` enum with `label_raw` for audit fidelity). `SalesReportSerializer` extended with writable nested `line_items`/`expenses` (replace-all semantics, `batch_size=500`), reads derived `total_sales_usd`/`total_expenses_usd`/`net_income_usd` (= local / Kurs). Existing endpoint `set_sales_report` unchanged; nested writes handled in `serializer.update()` inside `transaction.atomic()`. Migration `0037` applied on MSSQL (COLLATE Cyrillic_General_CI_AS on `product_name`/`label_raw`). 3 new tests covering nested persistence + totals computation + replace-all (Karaganda canonical case: 18371 kg × 680 = 12,492,280 KZT; expenses 3,165,995 KZT; net 9,326,285 KZT; all pass). All existing flat-field tests preserved. The line `amount_local` is always recomputed server-side (`qty × price`, client value ignored); the `set_sales_report` role gate now also bypasses for superusers (matches the UI). (`docs/obsidian/processes/sales-report.md` added.)
- **Rich Sales Report UI** (feat(frontend)). New `SalesReportPanel` on the Shipment Detail page (replacing the old flat `SalesReportForm`), shown once the shipment has departed (`yola_chykdy`, step 4+ — system status lags the real sale, so a stricter "sold" gate would block trucks that have already departed and sold). Editable line-item table (product, qty, price → auto `amount`) and expense table (21-code category `Select` with i18n labels; `OTHER` reveals a free-text label), `currency` + `exchange_rate` (Kurs) inputs, and a live read-only summary (gross sales, total expenses, **net**, plus USD equivalents at the Kurs) mirroring the server computation. Editing gated to `sales_rep`/`export_manager`/`director`/superuser AND step ≥ 4 (read-only otherwise; shared `MIN_SALES_REPORT_STEP` constant on the frontend, mirrored as a documented literal in the backend endpoint). New `useSaveSalesReport` hook (invalidates the shipment detail query), `ISalesReport*` types, and `sales_report.*` i18n keys (incl. all 21 expense labels) in tk/ru/en. Type-check clean. (`SalesReportPanel.tsx`, `salesReport/{LineItemsTable,ExpensesTable,salesReportUtils}`, `useSalesReport.ts`.)

### Fixed
- **Shipment "weight to load" field was mislabelled "rejected weight" on the List + detail drawer** (fix(frontend) + fix(p3)). The column `rejected_weight_kg` is a legacy name that actually holds the (unofficial) tonnage that must be loaded — "Ýüklemeli tonna". The Sheet (row 36) showed the right name via a stale admin per-row label override, but the **Shipment List column** and the **Edit/Detail drawer** still rendered the i18n "Rejected (kg)" / "Rejected weight", so the same field read as two opposite quantities across screens. Relabelled all three surfaces to **"Weight to load" / "Ýüklemeli tonna" / "Тоннаж к загрузке"** (tk/ru/en; the EN List keeps the short "To load (kg)" form to match sibling weight columns) and renamed the misleading sheet i18n key `sheet.row.weight_received` → `weight_to_load` (+ `sheet_rows.py` `label_key`). Because the Sheet renders `SheetRowSetting.label_*` when present (`dbLabel ?? t(label_key)`) and that row carried a stale per-row override (a Turkmen-only "Ýüklenmeli tonna" set in all three languages — itself a partial earlier fix that de-localized RU/EN), data migration `0047` **blanks** the persisted `label_tk/ru/en` for this one `field_key` so the Sheet defers to the corrected, per-language i18n fallback — one source of truth, verified identical in meaning across Sheet/List/Drawer in all three languages. The DB column / `field_key` stays `rejected_weight_kg` (no schema change). The separate sales-report `weight_rejected_kg` (db_column `waste_kg`, sale waste) is a different field and was left as-is. JSON valid; no model migrations pending. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-list-vs-sheet.md` updated.)
- **Audit Log: action column showed raw i18n keys instead of labels** (fix(frontend)). `AuditLogPage` only had labels + Tag colors for the 3 filterable actions (`transition`/`create`/`update`), but the `action` column carries ~16 free-form codes written by services (`plan_value_set`, `forecast_value_set`, `actual_value_set`, `daily_board_set`, `variety_override`, `soft_delete`, `delete`, `restore`, `manifest_close`, `local_sell_*`, plus legacy `plan_submitted`/`plan_approved`/`admin_override`/`blocks_field_set` in existing rows). Every unhandled code rendered the literal key (e.g. `audit_log.action_admin_override`) overflowing a too-narrow Tag. Added tk/ru/en labels + a semantic color family for the full code set (verified against the live DB-distinct action set, not just a code grep), widened the column to 160px with `nowrap`, and added a `humanizeAction()` fallback (`plan_value_set` → "Plan value set") as a last-resort net for any future code. Pure display mapping, so existing rows render cleanly too. `IAuditLog.action` retyped `string` (was the 3-value union). Type-check + i18n JSON valid.
- **Boss Dashboard Production & Blocks-heatmap analytics migrated to `HarvestDayEntry`** (fix(p3)). `_aggregate_production` and `_aggregate_blocks_heatmap` in `apps/export/services/boss_analytics.py` still queried the wide `monday_plan_kg…saturday_actual_kg` + `actual_weekly_total_kg` columns that migration `greenhouse.0004` dropped from `WeeklyHarvestPlan` (daily data moved to `HarvestDayEntry`), so `GET /api/v1/export/boss/production/` and `/blocks_heatmap/` both 500'd with `FieldError: Cannot resolve keyword 'monday_plan_kg' into field` (Sentry 127992064). Replaced both with a shared `_sum_harvest_by_block(date_from, date_to)` helper that DB-aggregates `Sum('plan_value')` / `Sum('actual_value')` from `HarvestDayEntry` grouped by block over an inclusive `entry_date` range (mirrors `greenhouse/services/legacy.py`). The helper calls `.order_by()` to strip `HarvestDayEntry.Meta.ordering` (`['entry_date','block__code']`) so it isn't folded into the `GROUP BY` and broken into per-day rows (MSSQL rule). Per-day `entry_date` filtering is also more accurate than the old "any overlapping week counts fully". Output dict keys unchanged, so the boss Excel/PDF exporters and frontend widgets are untouched. Verified: both endpoints return 200 (17 block rows) for daily/seasonal/month scopes; `manage.py check` clean.
- **`import_harvest_plans` no longer crashes on the dropped wide-column schema** (fix(p3)). The command still built `WeeklyHarvestPlan(monday_plan_kg=…, …, monday_actual_kg=…)` against columns that migration `greenhouse.0004` removed (daily data moved to `HarvestDayEntry`), so any run — including `--dry-run` — raised `TypeError: got unexpected keyword arguments: 'monday_plan_kg'…` (Sentry 127681104; the sibling `import_weekly_plan` was already migrated). Rewrote it to the same fill-empties pattern as `import_weekly_plan`: derive ISO week/year from each week's Monday, `get_or_create` the `WeeklyHarvestPlan` header, and write per-day `HarvestDayEntry` plan cells (filling `plan_value` only when NULL — operator forecasts/actuals/daily-board data untouched, idempotent). The un-splittable weekly-actual column (col J) is no longer imported. Also fixed a pre-existing default-path bug (`parents[6]` → `parents[5]` + `.resolve()`) that pointed `DEFAULT_PATH` one directory above the repo. Dry-run verified: 257 block-weeks / 1402 day-entry plan cells, 0 warnings. (`docs/obsidian/reference/data-imports.md` updated.)

### Changed
- **Quota usage `usage_date` now comes from the export code + Source badge** (fix(p3) + feat(frontend)). Auto-created `QuotaUsageRecord` rows took `usage_date = shipment.date`, which is only the creation/import day (e.g. a whole batch stamped `2026-06-09`), so the date didn't reflect when the truck was actually loaded. They now parse the **real date encoded in the operator's export code** — `DD` + 2-letter month + `NNN` + `/YY`, e.g. `12JN121/26` → 12 Jun 2026 — via new `apps/export/services/export_code.py::parse_export_code_date` (English month codes `JA FB MR AP MY JN JL AG SP OC NV DC`, **November = NV**; defensive — falls back to `shipment.date` on a missing/unparseable code). Manual records (no shipment) keep their user-picked date. Frontend: the Quota Usage **list view** gains a **Source** column — `Auto` (from a shipment) vs `Manual` — so the two kinds of rows are distinguishable at a glance (`quota_usage.source*` i18n × tk/ru/en). New management command `fix_quota_usage_dates` (dry-runnable) backfilled the existing draft rows: **67 of 121 corrected** on the live DB (e.g. `06-09 → 06-04`), 2 with no parseable code left on `shipment.date`. 9 new parser unit tests + sheet-junction suite green on MSSQL; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/quota-management.md` updated.)
- **Quota usage `kg_used` now mirrors the actual firm-split weight** (fix(p3)). Auto-created draft `QuotaUsageRecord` rows previously always took `kg_used` from the flat admin `TruckSplitDefault` constant (~18,100/N), so editing a firm's split weight on a shipment never reassigned that firm's quota usage. `sync_draft_quota_usage_for_shipment` now reads each live `ShipmentFirmSplit.weight_kg` and writes it as `kg_used` per firm — changing a split weight (via `POST /shipments/` or `POST /shipments/{id}/firm-splits/`) reassigns this month's usage to the new number. Falls back to `TruckSplitDefault` only when a split carries no weight. Shipment attachment (`QuotaUsageRecord.shipment`) and the one-record-per-firm behavior (a 2-firm truck → 2 usage records, i.e. one shipment across two firms' quotas) are unchanged. The approved-records guard still blocks re-sync. 2 new tests in `tests_shipment_sheet` (kg_used matches per-firm split weight; matches auto-filled weight when omitted) green on MSSQL. (`docs/obsidian/processes/quota-management.md` updated.)
- **Unified shipment code naming: `shipment_code` (system) + `export_code` (manual), "cargo code" eliminated** (refactor(p3) + refactor(frontend) + db). The two shipment codes were named inconsistently and even inverted across layers — the system-generated platform code (`NNNNNNN/YY`) was the model field `cargo_code` shown as "Export Code" almost everywhere but as "Shipment Code" in the Sheet (via cross-wired i18n keys), while the operator-typed pallet tag (`DD|MM|NNN|BLK|YY|VV`) was `official_export_code` shown as "Shipment Code" in some places and "Export Code" in others. Standardized on: **system-generated → `shipment_code` / "Shipment Code"**, **operator-typed → `export_code` / "Export Code"**, and removed the term **"cargo code"** entirely (the freight sense of "cargo" — transloading, peregruz — is untouched). Backend: `Shipment.cargo_code→shipment_code` (keeps `db_column='code'`), `Shipment.official_export_code→export_code` and `CustomsExpense.cargo_code_raw→shipment_code_raw` (both keep their original DB columns via `db_column`, so migration `0041` is **state-only, zero ALTER TABLE / zero MSSQL risk** via `SeparateDatabaseAndState`); serializers, sheet field-keys/label-keys (un-crossed), `validate_official_export_code→validate_export_code`, swap config, permission registry, and all tests follow. Data migration `0042` (export) remaps persisted `field_key` strings (`SheetRowSetting`, `ShipmentComment` + `#cell:` tokens in comment text) and `0021` (core) remaps `RoleFieldPermission.field_name` so existing Sheet cells, comments and grants don't orphan; `AuditLog` history is left as-is. Frontend: model/API field renames, camelCase identifiers (`cargoCode→shipmentCode`), and the i18n keys/values across **tk/ru/en** (system label now "Shipment Code" / "Код отгрузки" / "Iberiş kody"; manual label "Export Code" / "Экспортный код" / "Eksport kody"; "Add cargo" → "Add shipment"). Django `check`, frontend type-check, and i18n value assertions clean. (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/api-contract.md`, obsidian docs updated.)
- **Task drawer opens fast: single-shipment sheet fetch instead of the whole season** (perf, fix(p3) + feat(frontend)). Opening a task to act on it (the `SelfBoardTaskDrawer`, used by both the new Shipment Board task modal and the Self Kanban `/me/board`) was slow because `ActiveDrawerLayout` called `useShipmentSheet()` → `GET /export/shipments/sheet/`, which returns the **entire active-season sheet** (every shipment × every cell) just to render one shipment's editable fields. The sheet endpoint now accepts **`?shipment=<id>`**, returning only that shipment's row alongside the same global config (`rows` / `row_settings` / `users_index` / `current_user_*`); the row config is independent of the shipment list, so the payload shrinks from ~all-season to one row. `useShipmentSheet(shipmentId?)` is parametrized — single-shipment uses query key `['shipments','sheet','row',id]` (still a descendant of `['shipments','sheet']`, so existing invalidations refresh it) — and the drawer passes `task.shipment`. Both boards' drawers now open against a tiny fetch. 31/31 `tests_shipment_sheet` green on MSSQL (1 new: `?shipment=` returns one row + full config); frontend type-check + lint clean. (`.claude/rules/api-contract.md` updated.)
- **Contracts & Invoices wired into the dynamic page-permission system** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). The Contracts module (P4) shipped with its nav entries and routes bypassing the permission matrix entirely via a hardcoded `roles: [ALL_ROLES]` list, so they never appeared on *Admin → Permissions (Page Visibility)* or *Admin → Staff Page Access* and couldn't be toggled per-role. Now two page codes — `contracts.list` ("Contracts") and `contracts.invoices` ("Invoices") — are registered in `permission_registry.PAGE_REGISTRY` (non-`admin.` prefix, so delegated managers may also be granted them), making them appear as rows in both admin permission surfaces automatically. Default visibility is **management-only**: `admin` / `director` / `export_manager` get them by inheritance from `_ALL_PAGES` in `seed_permissions.py` (contracts.* is not `admin.*`); all other roles start hidden until an admin toggles them on (mirrors `_CONTRACT_WRITE_ROLES`). Frontend: the `/contracts`, `/contracts/:id` and `/invoices` routes are now wrapped in `<ProtectedRoute pageCode=…>`, the `AppLayout` nav entries drop the `roles` bypass and flow through `canSeePage()`, and `ROUTE_PAGE_MAP` maps the two routes to their page codes. Reseeded the permission tables (44 new `RolePagePermission` rows = 22 roles × 2 pages; verified visible for the 3 management roles, hidden for the other 12). Frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/permissions-system.md` updated.)
- **Contract/Invoice CRUD enforcement moved from hardcoded roles to the dynamic resource-permission matrix** (feat(p3)). Follow-up to the page-visibility wiring above: `ContractViewSet` / `InvoiceViewSet` previously gated writes on a hardcoded `_CONTRACT_WRITE_ROLES = ('export_manager','director','admin')` tuple (+ an `admin`-only `destroy` override on invoices), so contract/invoice access never appeared on *Admin → Permissions → Resource Permissions* and couldn't be tuned per-role. Now `contract` and `invoice` are registered resources in `RESOURCE_REGISTRY` (all-or-nothing — no `RESOURCE_FIELDS` entry), and both viewsets gate via `DynamicResourcePermission` (`resource_code = 'contract'` / `'invoice'`); the custom invoice `get_permissions` override is removed. Seeded defaults (`seed_permissions`): **contract** → full CRUD for `admin`/`director`/`export_manager`; **invoice** → CRUD for `admin` but **delete is `admin`-only** (`director`/`export_manager` get view+create+edit, no delete — preserves the old "rollback is too easy to mess up" rule); `boss` view-only on both (oversight); all other roles no access (fail-closed). This **tightens API reads** to those roles — previously any authenticated user could `GET` contracts/invoices — which is safe because every frontend consumer lives on the now-management-only contracts/invoices pages (verified: `ContractSelect` is used only inside `InvoiceCreate`). Reseeded the live DB (8 new `RoleResourcePermission` rows; grants verified). Both viewsets' test suites updated with a `_SeededPermsMixin` (seeds the matrix in `setUpTestData`, clears the perm cache in `tearDown` so rows don't leak to other apps' tests) — **33 contract+invoice tests pass on MSSQL**; `manage.py check` clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/permissions-system.md` updated.)
- **Weekly-plan edit window relaxed: block managers can edit the current week (incl. days already passed)** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). Previously `greenhouse_manager` plan edits locked at the **Sunday before** the plan week started, so once a week began nobody could enter or fix its plan without an admin late-edit extension (the "current-week trap"). The cutoff now moves to the **end of the plan week itself** (that week's Sunday 23:59:59 local): a week is editable throughout its own duration — including days that have already passed this week — and ahead of time; only **fully-past** weeks lock (still reopenable via admin `grant-late-edit`). Backend: new `plan_week_cutoff_utc()` + rewritten `_plan_edit_window_closed()` in `apps/greenhouse/services/harvest_day_service.py`; the `set_plan_value()` block message now cites the week-end cutoff. Frontend: for `greenhouse_manager`, `HarvestCell` `past_actual` cells now render **plan-only** (single value — managers never see/edit the actual, which is rollup/admin), click-to-edit when the day is in the current ISO week (`isInCurrentWeek` helper); admin keeps the actual + retro-plan dual view. Late-edit-extension suite updated to the new policy (25 pass); full greenhouse suite (60) green; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/processes/weekly-harvest-planning.md` updated.)

### Data
- **Imported the June 2026 customs/document cash-advance ledger from `avans.xlsx`** (data(p3)). New idempotent `import_avans` management command loaded the full ledger (1–17 Jun 2026) into the new tables: **16 `FinansistAdvance`** float top-ups (634,176 TMT) + **210 `CustomsExpense`** rows (634,176 TMT) — balance reconciles to **0.00 TMT**, matching the source sheet. The Excel trip codes (`MY471`/`JN055`) don't match `Shipment.shipment_code` (0 hits), so expenses link by a **unique full truck-plate match** against `Shipment.truck_plate`: **76 linked**, 5 ambiguous + 129 no-plate/unmatched left `shipment=null` (verbatim `shipment_code_raw`/`vehicle_plate`/`label_raw` keep them auditable; they can be linked later as more shipments get plates). Categories auto-classified from the Turkmen description (GUMRUKLEME 174, BORDER_RETURN 7, ANALIZ 6, …; 5 → OTHER). All imported rows carry a `[import:avans]` marker in `notes`, so re-running deletes-and-reloads cleanly (verified: 2nd run removed 226, re-inserted 226). Currency **TMT**. (`python manage.py import_avans [--dry-run] [--user <name>]`.)
- **Cleared week-25/2026 harvest plan values** (data(p3)). Wiped `plan_value` + plan submission metadata on the 44 filled cells of ISO week 25 (Mon 15 – Sun 21 Jun 2026) across all 15 block plans, keeping the row scaffolding so managers can re-enter from a clean grid. No forecast/actual data existed for the week, so none was affected.

### Added
- **Clients Report — live replacement for `by_clients.xlsx`** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). New analytics screen at `/analytics/clients-report` under a new top-level **Analytics** nav group, reproducing the hand-maintained `data/by_clients.xlsx` workbook (a `COUNTIF`-over-the-master-register dashboard now broken with `#REF!`) live from `export.shipments` for the active season. Backend: `apps/export/services/clients_report.py::build_clients_report(season)` + read-only 60s-cached `GET /api/v1/export/clients-report/` (`ClientsReportViewSet`). Returns (1) a **customer × month** matrix grouped by customer **and country** — so a buyer shipping to two countries appears twice, reproducing the spreadsheet's `Begjan`/`Begjan-Rossiya` route split — with per-row total trucks, `%` share, and **real tonnage** from `Sum(weight_net)/1000` (not the spreadsheet's trucks×18.2 estimate); (2) `by_country` and (3) `by_city` truck/tonnage breakdowns. MSSQL-safe (`TruncMonth`, `Coalesce(Sum,0)`, no JSONField). Frontend: `pages/analytics/ClientsReport.tsx` (matrix `Table` in `ClientsMatrixTable.tsx` with fixed Customer/Total cols, one sortable column per season month, totals summary row) + three Apache ECharts (doughnut by customer, pie by country, pie by city) via the shared `EChart` wrapper (now registers `PieChart`/`BarChart`); hook `useClientsReport.ts`. Page gated by new page_code `analytics.clients` (seeded visible for admin/director/export_manager/boss). Month column labels localized from numeric year+month via new `clients_report.months[]` arrays in tk/ru/en plus the rest of the `clients_report.*` + `nav.group_analytics`/`nav.clients_report` keys. Type-check clean; service verified against live season data; `manage.py check` clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/clients-report.md` added.)
- **Firm-split "no quota" soft warning on the Sheet** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend)). Assigning an export firm to a shipment via the Sheet `firm_splits` cell now flags any chosen firm that has **no remaining quota** — without blocking the save (quota is tracked, not hard-enforced; previously there was *no* feedback at all when assigning to a firm that never received an allocation). New read-only endpoint `GET /api/v1/export/quota-firm-balances/?product_type=tomato` (`QuotaFirmBalancesView`, gated by `quota_issuance` view = exactly the roles that can edit firm splits) returns `{ "<firm_id>": {issued_kg, used_kg, remaining_kg} }` from `compute_firm_quota_balances()`: `remaining = issued − committed` over the active season, where **committed = draft + approved** usage (not approved-only like the dashboard — firm-split assignment auto-creates *draft* usage, so drafts are the live commitment at assignment time and approved-only would under-warn); firms with no allocation absent (= zero); 60 s cache invalidated on usage approval. Frontend: `useQuotaFirmBalances` (fetched only while editing the `firm_splits` cell) tags no-quota firms `⚠ no quota` in the multi-select dropdown and fires a non-blocking `toast.warning` when a selection *adds* such a firm (and treats still-loading balances as "don't warn" to avoid a first-open flash). **Deliberate v1 limits**: product type defaults to `tomato` (not on the sheet payload; pepper is a rare separate domain), per-issuance expiry is not applied (coarse "has any balance" signal, not the FIFO/expiry ledger), and only the Sheet cell is covered (backend create / `set_firm_splits` still neither blocks nor warns). 8 new backend tests (`tests_quota_firm_balances.py`: service math, draft+approved both committed, used-to-zero, absent firm, no-active-season, endpoint 200/403/401); frontend type-check + lint clean. New i18n keys `sheet.firm_no_quota_tag` / `sheet.firm_no_quota_warning` × tk/ru/en. (`docs/obsidian/processes/quota-management.md` updated.)
- **Delegated user management for the Loading Dept Head** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db). A bounded exception to AD-15 (which kept all user/permission admin admin-only): `loading_dept_head` can now **create / edit / delete / reset-password** users — but ONLY of the `loading_dept_head_deputy` and `weight_master` roles — and **grant those two roles a subset of his own visible pages** via a new *Admin → Staff Page Access* editor. Who-may-manage-whom is a hardcoded single source of truth (`MANAGEABLE_BY_ROLE` in `apps/core/roles.py`, with `manageable_roles()` / `can_manage_users()`); admins/superusers keep full reach. Enforcement is **entirely server-side** — the frontend role dropdowns are UX only. `UserManagementViewSet` now scopes a delegated manager's user list to his manageable roles and guards every mutation with `_assert_can_manage` (target's **current** role) + `_assert_can_assign_role` (the **new** role on create / role-change — blocks escalation to `admin`). Page delegation is a **separate scoped endpoint** `GET/PUT /api/v1/export/admin/managed-page-permissions/` (`ManagedPagePermissionsView`), NOT the admin full-matrix PUT: the PUT is a **surgical `update_or_create`** over only the `(managed_role, grantable_page)` pairs in the payload (rows for other roles/pages — including admin-granted ones — are never touched), and the grantable set **excludes every `admin.*` page** so a head can never re-grant user/permission-administration pages (privilege-leak guard). Page grants are role-wide (perms are per-role). The head's **deputy does not** inherit this power. Reachability seeded by data migration `core.0020` (makes `admin.users` + the new `admin.staff_access` page visible to `loading_dept_head`); new page_code `admin.staff_access` registered in `permission_registry.py`. 18 new backend tests (`apps/export/tests_delegated_user_mgmt.py`) cover list scoping, create/patch/delete/set-password bounds, escalation-to-admin rejection, the surgical upsert (an out-of-band admin grant survives a head PUT), and admin-page / unmanaged-role / ungrantable-page rejection — all pass on MSSQL. New i18n keys × tk/ru/en. (ADR-022; `docs/obsidian/processes/permissions-system.md` updated.)
- **Weekly-plan board task per block manager — click-to-navigate + auto-complete** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db). Greenhouse managers now get a durable to-do on their task board to fill the weekly harvest-plan grid, instead of only the ephemeral P1/P2/P3 dispatcher notifications (which are kept — the task is additive). This is the first **non-shipment** Task: `Task.shipment` is now **nullable** and a `kind` discriminator (`shipment` | `weekly_plan`) plus `link` / `scope_year` / `scope_week` columns were added (export migration `0036`; see ADR-021). **Generation is on-demand** — a "Generate plan tasks" button on the WeeklyPlanGrid toolbar (admin / export_manager / director only) POSTs `{year, week}` to new `POST /api/v1/export/tasks/generate-weekly-plan/`, which creates one task per active `BlockManagerAssignment` user for that ISO week (idempotent — re-running skips managers who already have one; `assignee_user`=the manager, `completion_rule=MANUAL_DONE`, `link=/export/plan?week=…&year=…`). On the SelfBoard these render via a new `PlanTaskCard`; clicking navigates to the plan grid at the task's week. **Auto-completion is lazy** — resolved on the `/me/tasks/` read path (`resolve_weekly_plan_tasks_for_user`), NOT on plan-save, because greenhouse (where `set_plan_value` lives) may not import export; the task flips to DONE once **no blank plan cells remain** for the manager's blocks that week (every assigned block has ≥1 `HarvestDayEntry` row and none has `plan_value IS NULL`; explicit `0` counts as filled), worst-case within the 60 s nav-badge poll. A `python manage.py resolve_weekly_plan_tasks` command does the same across all users for cron/backfill. `/me/tasks/` non-supervisor scoping tightened to `assignee_role==role AND (assignee_user IS NULL OR assignee_user==me)` so personal weekly tasks stay private while role-wide shipment tasks remain visible. Service: `apps/export/services/weekly_plan_tasks.py`. 9 new backend tests (generation idempotency, inactive-assignment exclusion, blank-cell/zero/no-rows resolution, endpoint auth, manager isolation) pass on MSSQL; frontend type-check clean. New i18n keys `tasks.fill_weekly_plan` / `plan.generate_tasks` / `plan.generate_tasks_toast` × tk/ru/en. (ADR-021; `docs/obsidian/processes/comments-tasks.md` + `weekly-harvest-planning.md` updated.)
- **Sentry error tracking on the frontend** (feat(frontend)). `@sentry/react` initialised in `src/main.tsx` before `createRoot`, so React render/runtime errors and unhandled promise rejections are captured. DSN read from `import.meta.env.VITE_SENTRY_DSN` with the frontend project DSN baked in as default — set `VITE_SENTRY_DSN=''` to disable (local dev). EU (`de`) region (separate Sentry project from the backend). `environment` set from Vite's `import.meta.env.MODE`. Adapted to the real entry point (`#root` + `StrictMode` + existing dayjs setup), not the wizard's `#app` stub. Type-check clean.
- **Sentry error tracking on the backend** (feat(core)). `sentry-sdk[django]` initialised as early as possible in `config/settings.py` (right after `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, before app/settings load) so it captures errors during the rest of settings load. DSN read from `SENTRY_DSN` env var with the project DSN baked in as default — set `SENTRY_DSN=''` to disable (local dev/CI). DSN targets Sentry's EU (`de`) region to keep event data in the EU (KZ/RU users). `send_default_pii=True`. `environment` auto-set from `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` (falls back to `production`/`development` based on `DEBUG`). `traces_sample_rate` defaults to `0.0` (performance tracing off to protect quota; raise via `SENTRY_TRACES_SAMPLE_RATE`). The Django integration is auto-enabled by `sentry-sdk[django]`, which means **unhandled exceptions** in views/DRF/middleware/Channels AND every `logger.error()` / `logger.exception()` call are captured automatically (the `LoggingIntegration` default `event_level=ERROR`). On top of that, the two **deliberately-swallowed defensive `except` blocks** that previously logged at `warning` level and never reached Sentry now also `sentry_sdk.capture_exception()` so real bugs surface without breaking the protected operation: feedback ticket-resolution notification creation (`apps/feedback/services/notifications.py`) and active-season lookup fallback (`apps/export/services/boss_analytics.py`). Intentional control-flow swallows (query-param parse `except (ValueError, TypeError): pass`, invalid-token logout, "field not filled" lookups) were left alone — they are not errors. `manage.py check` clean; `apps.feedback` 26 tests pass. Frontend Sentry NOT yet added.
- **Sheet undo: Ctrl+Z reverts the last cell change (Google Sheets parity)** (feat(frontend)). Pressing Ctrl/⌘+Z on the Sheet pops the most-recent cell write (LIFO) and replays its reverse. Covers **every** Sheet write path — inline editor edits, paste, cut, delete, clear, dropdowns, dates, FK, the R26 transit/temp combined cell, and the junction cells (firm_splits, block_sources, varieties). Architecture: a bounded (50) in-memory Zustand stack (`stores/undoStore.ts`) of typed, closure-free descriptors keyed by reverse mechanism (`cell` / `multi` / `junction` / `varieties`); capture is distributed across the ~6 write sites via `hooks/undoCapture.ts` (records the cached `before` value before the mutation, drops the entry on rollback via a per-call `onError`, and overwrites the guard baseline with the reconciled server value `onSuccess`); reverse is centralized in `hooks/useApplyUndo.ts` behind a pure, unit-tested `planUndo()` that reuses the existing `writeCell` / `patchMulti` / junction-POST paths. Key safeguards: an `isUndoing` flag so the reverse write (which routes through the capturing `writeCell`) can't ping-pong the stack; a **concurrent-edit guard** (skips with a toast when the cell changed since the recorded edit); junction code→id resolution (skips when a firm/block was deactivated); and the stack clears on Sheet unmount. **Honest cascade handling:** when the original edit had advanced the shipment status, undo restores the value AND toasts that the status advance (`from → to`) was *not* reverted — the lifecycle is forward-only, so a value re-PATCH cannot reverse a status transition, AD-1 timestamps, notifications, or created/closed tasks. Shortcut matches on `e.code === 'KeyZ'` (layout-independent, RU/KZ-safe); `Ctrl+Shift+Z` reserved for a future redo. 21 new unit tests (`undoStore.test.ts` + `planUndo` matrix in `useApplyUndo.test.ts`). 5 new i18n keys × tk/ru/en. **Known limits (documented):** cascade can't be detected for junction/custom edits (those endpoints don't echo the status); reverting varieties back to *empty* isn't possible (the override endpoint no-ops on empty). Type-check + lint + 50 tests clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated.)
- **Sheet clipboard: Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V + Delete on the active cell (Google Sheets parity)** (feat(frontend)). Operators can now copy, cut, paste, and delete cell values with the keyboard instead of re-typing. The shortcuts are handled in `SheetGrid`'s existing `keydown` listener **before** the nav / type-to-edit branches (so they never accidentally open the editor) and only fire on the single `activeCell` while not editing / in join/swap mode and not focused in a form control (native copy/paste still wins inside the open editor, comments composer, and search box).
  - **Ctrl/⌘+C** stores the cell's **raw value** (FK id, option code, ISO date, number, `custom_*` string) plus its formatted **display text** in new `sheetStore.clipboard`. The in-app clipboard is the workhorse (all in-Sheet copy/paste flows through it). It *also* best-effort mirrors the display text to the OS clipboard (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`) for external paste into Excel — but `navigator.clipboard` only exists in a **secure context**, so this no-ops on the plain-http beta server; in-Sheet copy/paste still works fully there. Brief `t('sheet.cell_copied')` toast.
  - **Ctrl/⌘+X** = copy + clear the source (gated by the same editability + clearable checks as Delete; non-clearable cells like `shipment_code` copy only).
  - **Ctrl/⌘+V** is **type-safe**: same-field paste round-trips the raw value through the field's own save path (FK ids, codes, dates, numbers, custom strings); cross-field paste is limited to free-text↔free-text (`text`/`phone`); read-only and junction (`firm_splits`/`block_sources`) targets are rejected with a toast. With no in-app clipboard it falls back to `navigator.clipboard.readText()` for free-text targets only.
  - **Delete / Backspace** clears the active cell (same gate as right-click **Clear cell**).
  New hooks `useSheetClipboard` (the C/X/V/Delete handlers) and shared `useSheetCellWrite` (`writeCell` / `clearCell` — the typed save + clear paths). `useSheetCellWrite` also **deduplicates** the three clear mutations that previously lived inline in `SheetCell` (right-click Clear cell now calls the same `clearCell`), and the cell-editability rule was extracted to `utils/sheetPermissions.ts` (`isCellEditable`) so the clipboard hook and `SheetGrid.renderRow` share one gate. New i18n keys `sheet.cell_copied` / `paste_readonly` / `paste_unsupported` / `paste_incompatible` / `paste_nothing` × tk/ru/en. The paste-routing matrix is extracted as a pure `decidePaste(target, clip)` and unit-tested (14 new tests across `useSheetClipboard.test.ts` + `useSheetCellWrite.test.ts` covering the decision matrix and the clearable/junction/free-text gate predicates). The Ctrl+C/X/V shortcuts match on **`e.code`** (physical key), not `e.key`, so they work on Russian/Kazakh layouts (where `e.key` for Ctrl+C is Cyrillic `с`). **Out of scope (deliberately):** range selection / multi-cell fill (still single-cell), and Ctrl+Z undo (to be designed separately — a forward re-PATCH wouldn't reverse status auto-advance / AD-1 timestamps / notifications). Type-check + lint + tests clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated — new "Clipboard & Delete" section.)
- **Feedback authors are now notified in-app when their ticket is resolved or rejected** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db). Previously the only signal that a ticket reached a terminal state was the admin email-on-create flow (which targets admins, not the author) — the submitter had to keep re-opening *My Tickets* to learn the outcome. Now the admin's `PATCH /feedback/tickets/{id}/` status change into `resolved`/`rejected` creates a per-user `Notification` for the ticket `author` (bell badge), via new `apps/feedback/services/notifications.py`. Two new `Notification.KIND_CHOICES` — `feedback_resolved` (green) / `feedback_rejected` (red) — added to the model in `apps.export` (migration `0034_notification_feedback_kinds`; choices-only, no `ALTER TABLE`, MSSQL-safe). The "should notify" decision is captured *before* `serializer.save()` (where `ticket.status` still holds the old value) and fired after; guards fire on any change *into* a terminal status (incl. `resolved → rejected`) but skip a **self-resolve** (admin resolving own ticket) and a **same-status re-patch** (`new_status != ticket.status`). The notify is wrapped in `try/except` + `logger.warning` so it can never roll back or 500 the admin's status change. `Notification` is lazy-imported inside the service (feedback→export, mirroring the documented greenhouse exception until `Notification` moves to `core`). Link set to `/feedback/my-tickets?ticket={id}` (forward-compat; the bell does not yet click-to-navigate for any kind). Frontend: `feedback_resolved`/`feedback_rejected` added to `INotification['kind']` and the exhaustive `KIND_COLOR` record in `NotificationBell.tsx`. 5 new backend tests (resolve notifies, reject notifies, resolved→rejected re-notifies, self-resolve silent, same-status re-patch silent); all pass on MSSQL; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/feedback-module.md` updated.)
- **Type-to-edit + commit-and-hop on the Sheet (Google Sheets parity)** (feat(frontend)). Two linked behaviors so operators can do spreadsheet-style data entry — arrow to a cell, type a value, arrow to the next — without ever reaching for Enter or the mouse:
  - **Type-to-edit**: typing any single printable character on the active cell opens that cell's editor immediately and **replaces** its content with the typed character. For `text` / `phone` / `number` (and the R26 `transit_days_temp` combined cell) the glyph becomes the editor's seed value (carried on `setEditingCell(cell, seed?)` via new `sheetStore.editSeed`), caret placed after it so the next keystroke appends; `dropdown` / `date` / `datetime` / `status` / `multiselect` just open (they ignore the seed). The `SheetGrid` keydown guard permits **AltGr** (`Ctrl+Alt` → a real glyph, e.g. Turkmen `ş/ç/ý/ň` on some layouts) while still rejecting `Ctrl`/`Meta` shortcuts.
  - **Commit-and-hop**: once a text-like editor is open in **seeded (type-to-edit) mode**, an arrow key **commits the value and moves the selection** one cell in that direction (`preventDefault` suppresses the caret move; the committed value is read at event time so it's exactly what was typed even if antd `InputNumber` also steps). The editor (`handleSeededKeyDown`) owns the commit and signals direction via new `sheetStore.pendingNav`; `SheetGrid` consumes it through the extracted, shared `navigateActiveCell()` (same find-next-non-hidden + scroll logic the bare arrow listener uses). Editors opened via **Enter or click** (`editSeed == null`) keep native caret behavior so a typo can be fixed mid-value; `Escape` always cancels.
  Enter-to-edit and bare arrow navigation are unchanged. Type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated — new "Keyboard navigation & type-to-edit" section.)
- **Row style settings editable directly from the Sheet (gear popover)** (feat(frontend)). The per-row "…" kebab in the Sheet's label band (Col C) is replaced by a **gear icon** (`SheetRowSettingsPopover`). For **admin / director / export_manager / superuser** it opens the row style controls in-place — background + font colors (on one line), font weight / style / family / size — so styling no longer requires opening *Shipment Settings → Sheet Rows*. Everyone else still gets the gear with just the per-user **Hide row** action. The style controls were extracted from `SheetRowStylePopover` into a shared `SheetRowStyleControls` component used by both the admin tab and the Sheet, so the two surfaces stay identical. The **width and alignment** controls were dropped as unusable (the `style_width`/`style_align` columns remain on the model and any pre-set value is still honoured on render, but they're no longer editable in either surface). Sheet edits PATCH the global `SheetRowSetting` via `useSaveSheetRowSetting` (invalidates `['shipments','sheet']` → change shows immediately, `version` refreshes; stale-version 409 → refresh modal). Front-end gate is `canEditRowStyle` in `SheetGrid`; the backend endpoint enforces the same shipment-edit permission. Fallback rows with no `SheetRowSetting` (`id === null`) show the hide action only. New i18n key `sheet.row_settings` × tk/ru/en; type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` updated.)
- **Per-row cell typography in the Sheet Style settings — font weight, italic, font family, font size** (feat(p3) + feat(frontend) + db). The **Style** popover in *Shipment Settings → Sheet Rows* (`SheetRowStylePopover`) gains four controls alongside width/align/colors: **font weight** (Bold / Normal), **font style** (Normal / Italic), **font family** (Default / DM Sans / Inter / Monospace / Serif), and **font size** (8–28px slider). Backed by four new split columns on `SheetRowSetting` — `style_font_weight`, `style_font_style`, `style_font_family`, `style_font_size` (export migrations `0032`/`0033`, all blank-default; the three CharFields are `choices`-validated so DRF auto-rejects bad values, `style_font_size` is range-validated 8–28; no JSONField per ADR-0008). The font-family value is a controlled allowlist key mapped to a CSS stack via `SHEET_FONT_FAMILY` in `SheetCell.tsx` (the client never receives an arbitrary font); `font_size` is multiplied by `sheetZoom` on render so it tracks the rest of the cell. **All Sheet cell text now renders bold by default** — the bold baseline lives in `SheetCell` outside the `style?.` optional chain (`font_weight === 'normal' ? 400 : 700`) so it also applies to fallback rows with no `SheetRowSetting`; admins set `normal` per row to un-bold. Typography applies to the default `.sheet-cell__text` render; special-render cells (shipment code, country-with-flag, firm/block tags) keep their own component weights. Values travel to the frontend in `row_settings[field_key].style` (omitted when unset). i18n keys added in tk/ru/en. 3 new backend tests (persist + bad-choice + out-of-range rejection); all 35 `tests_sheet_settings_admin` pass; frontend type-check clean. (`docs/obsidian/screens/shipment-sheet.md` + `reference/data-model-map.md` updated.)

### Fixed
- **Quota Dashboard 403 → "Failed to load quota data" for every non-superuser role** (fix(p3) + fix(frontend)). The code-review pass (cc28108) switched `QuotaDashboardView` from `[IsAuthenticated]` to `DynamicResourcePermission` with `resource_code = 'quota'`, but `'quota'` is not a registered resource in `RESOURCE_REGISTRY` (nor any role's `RESOURCE_DEFAULTS`). So `get_resource_perm(role, 'quota')` returned `None` for **every** role (export_manager, document_team, director included) → 403 → the red error banner. Fixed by pointing the read-only dashboard at the resource it actually aggregates: `resource_code = 'quota_issuance'`, which is held by exactly the roles that can see the `export.quota` page (verified on the beta DB).
